# Correctness report — tbs_extreme_effects

**Verdict: ERRORS FOUND — REPAIRED BY MINIMAL PATCHES**

7 result(s) of the paper were examined. 5 were found wrong as printed: 5 corrected (5 by a weaker statement), 0 not repaired. In Lean: 7 proved, 0 taken from the literature as cited, 0 not certified, 0 not formalized.

## What was wrong and what was done

### Proposition 3.3 — corrected; the corrected statement is weaker

- **As printed.** \label{prop:id}Suppose that $U$ is multivariate regularly varying and Assumption~\ref{assump:exo}, \ref{assump:iid}, \ref{assump:f_homo} hold, we have \begin{align*} \theta^{\tmop{NETE}} = \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E}[g (X, 1, U /\|U\|) - g (X, 0, U /\|U\|) \mid \|U\|> t] \cdot \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E}[\|U\|^{\alpha} / t^{\alpha} \mid \|U\|> t] . \end{align*}
- **What is wrong.** ``\dots \[ \begin{array}{@{}c@{}}\displaystyle \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E} \left[ \frac{Y (1) - Y (0)}{t^{\alpha}} \mid \|U\|> t \right] \\ \displaystyle = \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E} \left[ \frac{f (X, 1, U) - f (X, 0, U)}{t^{\alpha}} \mid \|U\|> t \right] \\ \displaystyle = \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E} \left[ \frac{f (X, 1, U) - f (X, 0, U)}{\|U\|^{\alpha}} \cdot \left( \frac{\|U\|}{t} \right)^{\alpha} \mid \|U\|> t \right] \\ \displaystyle = \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E} \left[ (g (X, 1, U / \| U \|) - g (X, 0, U / \| U \|) + 2 e (t)) \cdot \left( \frac{\|U\|}{t} \right)^{\alpha} \mid \|U\|> t \right]\end{array}\mbox{''} \] This step uses the conditional-mean identification supplied by exogeneity and consistency, which the paper assumes only for conditioning on $X$, but applies it to conditioning on $(X,U)$ and then on $\{\|U\|>t\}$. In the step quoted in (a): The homogeneity inequality supplies a signed remainder bounded at the realized radius by $2e(\|U\|)$, rather than the displayed equality with the positive term $2e(t)$. ``\dots \[ \frac{\int_1^{\infty} \alpha P (\| U \| > {rt}) r^{\alpha - 1} \tmop{dr}}{P (\| U \| > t)} \leqslant 2\int^{\infty}_1 \alpha r^{\alpha -\beta + \epsilon - 1} \tmop{dr} < \infty .\mbox{''} \] The choice of a positive $\epsilon$ satisfying $\alpha-\beta+\epsilon<0$ requires $\alpha<\beta$, which the proposition does not assume. ``We next argue that $\lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E} [(\| U \| / t)^{\alpha} \mid \| U \| > t] = \alpha / (\beta - \alpha)$.'' The radial moment limit is $\beta/(\beta-\alpha)$ when $\alpha<\beta$, rather than $\alpha/(\beta-\alpha)$. ``We conclude that $h (U)$ is uniformly integrable.'' A uniform bound on first absolute moments does not imply uniform integrability.
- **Counterexample.** Let $X\sim\tmop{Unif}([0,1])$, $D\sim\tmop{Ber}(1/2)$, and $V\sim\tmop{Unif}([0,1])$ be mutually independent. Let $W$ be independent of $(X,D,V)$ with $\mathbb P(W>w)=w^{-3}$ for $w\geq1$, and set $U=W(V,1-V)$ and $\alpha=1$. Define $T(w)=(1-w^{-3})^{-1/3}$, $Y(1)=DW+(1-D)T(W)$, $Y(0)=0$, and $Y=Y(D)$. Take independent copies for the observations, and take $g(x,1,s)=1$, $g(x,0,s)=0$, and $e(r)=0$. The variables $T(W)$ and $W$ have the same distribution, so $(Y(1),Y(0))\perp D\mid X$, while $U\perp(X,D)$ and exact homogeneity holds. The displayed product is $3/2$, whereas $\mathbb E[T(W)\mid W>t]\to1$ and $\mathbb E[W\mid W>t]=3t/2$ give $\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}=3/4$.
- **Correction.** Suppose that $U$ is multivariate regularly varying and Assumption~\ref{assump:exo}, \ref{assump:iid}, \ref{assump:f_homo} hold. Suppose in addition that $\alpha<\beta$, $0<p(X)<1$ almost surely, and, for $d\in\{0,1\}$, $\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U,D]=\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U]$ almost surely. Suppose that $\mathbb E[|Y(d)|\mid\|U\|>t]<\infty$ for both treatments and all sufficiently large $t$. Then we have \begin{align*} \theta^{\tmop{NETE}} &= \lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb E[g(X,1,U/\|U\|)-g(X,0,U/\|U\|)\mid\|U\|>t]\cdot\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb E[\|U\|^{\alpha}/t^{\alpha}\mid\|U\|>t]. \end{align*}
- **Proof.** Write $R_U=\|U\|$, $S_U=U/\|U\|$, and $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$; the last quantity is finite for sufficiently large $t$ and tends to zero. Independence of $U$ from $(X,D)$ gives $\mathbb P(D=1\mid X,U)=p(X)$. Positivity, consistency, and conditional mean exogeneity given $(X,U)$ therefore give, for both treatments, \[ f(X,d,U)=\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U,D=d]=\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U]. \] The assumed absolute tail means justify taking conditional expectations on $\{R_U>t\}$. Homogeneity at the realized radius yields \[ \frac{f(X,1,U)-f(X,0,U)}{R_U^{\alpha}}=g(X,1,S_U)-g(X,0,S_U)+\varepsilon(X,U),\qquad |\varepsilon(X,U)|\leq2\bar e(t)\quad\hbox{on }\{R_U>t\}. \] Consequently, \[ \left|\mathbb … (the corrected paper gives it in full)
- **What the correction gives up.** Identification for distributions that satisfy exogeneity given $X$ but violate conditional mean exogeneity given $(X,U)$, distributions with a degenerate true propensity, the range $\alpha\geq\beta$, and counterfactuals without finite absolute tail means. (The paper's owner accepted the weaker statement.)
- **Formal check.** proved in Lean (the corrected statement), under 7 condition(s) made explicit — `Papers.TbsExtremeEffects.nete_identification` (`TId.lean:18`); axioms: the standard ones only (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound).

### Theorem 3.5 — corrected; the corrected statement is weaker

- **As printed.** \label{thm:rate}Suppose that Assumption \ref{assump:exo}, \ref{assump:overlap}, \ref{assump:iid}, \ref{assump:f_homo}, \ref{assump:mvary} hold, $\alpha < \beta$, where $\alpha$ and $\beta$ are defined in \cref{assump:f_homo} and \cref{assump:mvary} respectively. Furthermore, for any fixed $t$, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, \begin{align*} |p (X) - \hat{p} (X) | \leqslant R_p (n, \delta) & , | \hat{\alpha}_{n} - \alpha| \leqslant R_\alpha(n,\delta),\\ |\mathbb{E}[Y/\|U\|^{\alpha} \mid X, D, U /\|U\|, \|U\|> t] &- \hat{g} (X, D, U /\|U\|) | \leqslant R_g (n_t, \delta), \end{align*} where $n_t = \sum_{i = 1}^{n/2} I (\|U_i \|> t)$ and $R_p, R_g, R_\alpha$ are estimation errors that are monotonically decreasing with respect to sample size. Then, with probability at least $1 - \delta, \delta \in (0, 1 / 2)$, we have \begin{align} \left| {{\widehat{\theta }_{n, t}^{\text{DR} }} } - \theta^{NETE} \right| & \leqslant O (\sqrt{R_p (n/2, \delta)R_g ({n_t}, \delta)} + t^{\beta / 2} n^{-1 / 2}+ \log (1 / \delta) n^{- 1 / (2 +\beta)} \notag\\ & \quad\quad \quad \quad + t^{- \min \{1, \beta\}} + t^{- \beta s / (1 - 2 s)} + \log(t)R_\alpha(n,\delta)+ e (t)).\label{eq:rate_dr} \end{align} and \begin{align} \left| {{\widehat{\theta }_{n, t}^{\text{IPW}}} } - \theta^{NETE} \right| & \leqslant O (R_p (n/2, \delta) +t^{\beta / 2} n^{- 1 / 2} + \log (1 / \delta) n^{- 1 / (2 + \beta)} \notag\\ &\quad\quad \quad \quad + t^{- \min \{1, \beta\}} + t^{- \beta s / (1 - 2 s)}+ \log(t)R_\alpha(n,\delta) + e (t)). \label{eq:rate_ipw} \end{align}
- **What is wrong.** ``\dots \[ \begin{array}{@{}c@{}}\displaystyle | \hat{\theta}_{n, t}^{\tmop{DR}} - \theta^{\tmop{NETE}} | = | \hat{\eta}_{n, t}^{\tmop{DR}} \cdot \hat{\mu}_n - \eta \cdot \mu | \\ \displaystyle \leqslant | \mu | \cdot | \hat{\eta}_{n, t}^{\tmop{DR}} - \eta | + | \hat{\eta}_{n, t}^{\tmop{DR}} | \cdot | \hat{\mu}_n - \mu |.\end{array}\mbox{''} \] This step uses identification of the potential-outcome conditional means by $f(X,d,U)$, which the paper assumes only for the conditional mean exogeneity condition $\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U,D]=\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U]$ for both treatments, but applies it to potential outcomes satisfying only $(Y(1),Y(0))\perp D\mid X$ and noise satisfying $U\perp(X,D)$. ``\dots \[ \begin{array}{@{}c@{}}\displaystyle | \hat{\theta}_{n, t}^{\tmop{DR}} - \theta^{\tmop{NETE}} | \leqslant O ( \sqrt{R_p (n/2, \delta) R_g (nt^{- \beta}, \delta)} + t^{\beta / 2}n^{- 1 / 2} + \log (1 / \delta) n^{- 1 / (2 + \beta)} \\ \displaystyle \quad\quad\quad\quad + t^{- \min \{1,\beta\}} + t^{- \beta s / (1 - 2 s)} + \log (t) R_{\alpha} (n, \delta) + e(t)) .\end{array}\mbox{''} \] The sampling term $t^{\beta/2}n^{-1/2}$ requires control of normalized outcome fluctuations, whereas the assumptions allow integrable stable noise whose sample averages converge more slowly. ``\dots \[ | \hat{\mu}_n - \mu | = \left| \frac{1}{1 - \hat{\alpha}_{n} \hat{\gamma}_n} - \frac{1}{1 - \alpha \gamma} \right| = O (| \hat{\alpha}_{n} - \alpha | + | \hat{\gamma}_n - \gamma |) = O (\log (1 / \delta) n^{- 1 / (2 + \beta)} + R_{\alpha} (n, \delta))\mbox{''} \] The reciprocal difference contains the factor $|1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n|^{-1}$, which boundedness of $\hat\gamma_n$ and sample-size monotonicity of $R_\alpha$ do not control. In the step quoted in (b): The exponent perturbation contains $t^a-1+Kat^a$, with $a=R_\alpha(n,\varepsilon)$, and cannot be bounded by a constant multiple of $a\log t$ when $a\log t$ is unbounded. In the step quoted in (b): Homogeneity is used at radii exceeding $t$, so its contribution is controlled by $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$ rather than by $e(t)$. In the step quoted in (b): The angular bias can have order $t^{-1}\log t$ when $\beta=1$, whereas the displayed bound retains only $t^{-1}$. ``By Lemma \ref{lemma:mu_rate} and \ref{lemma:eta_rate}, with high probability, $ \hat{\gamma}_n $ and $| \hat{\eta}_{n,t}^{\tmop{DR}} |$ is bounded.'' A nuisance event with failure probability $\delta$ leaves no allowance for sampling or radial-estimation failures, and sample-size monotonicity does not compare nuisance bounds at different confidence tolerances. In the step quoted in (c): Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate} retains an adaptation logarithm, an additional endpoint logarithm when $\beta=1$, and exponent $1/3$ when $0<\beta<1$, none of which is supplied by the displayed conversion to $\log(1/\delta)n^{-1/(2+\beta)}$.
- **Counterexample.** Take $m=d=d_z=d_u=2$, $A=I_2$, and independent Pareto type-II components $Z_1,Z_2$ with index $\beta=2$, and set $U=AZ$. Choose $l=1/2$, $u=2$, $\sigma=1/2$, $s=2/5$, $\xi=1/2$, and $\zeta=1$, so that the nominal component density satisfies the tail-model conditions. Let $X=0$, let $r_0$ be a median of $\|U\|$, and set $B=I\{\|U\|>r_0\}$. Let $V$ be an independent fair Bernoulli variable, let $\varepsilon$ be independent standard Gaussian noise, and define $D=B\mathbin{\oplus}V$, $Y(d)=2d+V+\varepsilon$, and $Y=Y(D)$. Take independent copies for the observations, set $\alpha=\hat\alpha_n=0$, $g(x,d,u)=1+d$, $e(r)=I\{r\leq r_0\}$, $p=\hat p=1/2$, $c=1/4$, and $\hat g(x,d,u)=1+d$. For each tolerance $\varepsilon_0\in(0,1)$, use $R_p(q,\varepsilon_0)=(q+1)^{-10}$, $R_\alpha(q,\varepsilon_0)=(q+1)^{-10}$, and $R_g(q,\varepsilon_0)=2+(q+1)^{-10}$, which satisfy the joint uniform nuisance inequalities. Fix $\delta=1/4$ and take $t=n^{1/4}$ along even sample sizes. Here $D$ is independent of both $U$ and the potential-outcome pair, but $\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}=2$ while both estimators converge in probability to $1$ and both displayed error bounds tend to zero.
- **Rests on.** Lemma A.3, Lemma A.2, Lemma A.1, Proposition 3.3, also found wrong as printed.
- **Correction.** Suppose that Assumption \ref{assump:exo}, \ref{assump:overlap}, \ref{assump:iid}, \ref{assump:f_homo}, \ref{assump:mvary} hold, $\alpha < \beta$, where $\alpha$ and $\beta$ are defined in \cref{assump:f_homo} and \cref{assump:mvary} respectively. Suppose in addition that the conditions of Proposition~\ref{prop:id} hold. Suppose that the fitted propensity, regression and exponent are measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data, denoted together by $\mathcal T$, and that $\mathbb E[|Y/\|U\|^{\hat\alpha_n}|\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t]<\infty$ on each joint nuisance event. Furthermore, for any fixed $t$, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, \begin{align*} |p (X) - \hat{p} (X) | \leqslant R_p (n, \delta) & , | \hat{\alpha}_{n} - \alpha| \leqslant R_\alpha(n,\delta),\\ |\mathbb{E}[Y/\|U\|^{\alpha} \mid X, D, U /\|U\|, \|U\|> t] &- \hat{g} (X, D, U /\|U\|) | \leqslant R_g (n_t, \delta), \end{align*} where $n_t = \sum_{i = 1}^{n/2} I (\|U_i \|> t)$ and $R_p, R_g, R_\alpha$ are estimation errors that are monotonically decreasing with respect to sample size. Fix $\delta\in(0,1/2)$ and a deterministic threshold $t$. Run the adaptive Hill selection in Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} at tolerance $\delta/6$, and write its full-input output as $\hat\gamma_n=\widetilde\gamma_n$. Put $\pi_t=\mathbb P(\|U\|>t)$, $M=|\mathcal I|$, $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$, and $G_t=C+\bar e(t)$, where $C$ bounds $|g|$ on its compact domain. Use zero for each first-factor estimate when $M=0$. For a generic evaluation observation put $S_U=U/\|U\|$ and define \[ \begin{aligned} \psi^{\mathrm{DR}}={}&\hat g(X,1,S_U)-\hat g(X,0,S_U)\\ &+\frac{D-\hat p(X)}{\hat p(X)(1-\hat p(X))}\left(\frac{Y}{\|U\|^{\hat\alpha_n}}-\hat g(X,D,S_U)\right),\\ \psi^{\mathrm{IPW}}={}&\frac{Y}{\|U\|^{\hat\alpha_n}}\left(\frac{D}{\hat p(X)}-\frac{1-D}{1-\hat p(X)}\right). \end{aligned} \] For each $q\in\{\mathrm{DR},\mathrm{IPW}\}$, choose $B_q>0$ using only $\mathcal T$, and put \[ \begin{aligned} v_t^q(B_q)&=\mathbb E[(\psi^q)^2I\{|\psi^q|\leq B_q\}\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t],\\ b_t^q(B_q)&=\mathbb E[|\psi^q|I\{|\psi^q|>B_q\}\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t],\\ \mathcal S_t^q&=\sqrt{\frac{12v_t^q(B_q)}{\delta\max\{M,1\}}}+\left(1+\frac{12}{\delta}\right)b_t^q(B_q). \end{aligned} \] Fix $a_0\in(0,\beta)$ and put \[ \begin{aligned} a&=R_\alpha(n,\delta/12),\qquad K=\frac{4}{\beta-a_0},\\ \mathcal A_t&=2LC_W\left((\log t)^k t^{-\min\{1,\beta\}}+t^{-\beta s/(1-2s)}\right),\\ \mathcal E_{\mathrm{DR}}={}&\mathcal S_t^{\mathrm{DR}}+\frac2c R_p(n/2,\delta/12)R_g(n_t,\delta/12)\\ &+2\bar e(t)+\mathcal A_t+\frac{2G_t}{c}(t^a-1+Kat^a),\\ \mathcal E_{\mathrm{IPW}}={}&\mathcal S_t^{\mathrm{IPW}}+\frac{2G_t}{c}R_p(n/2,\delta/12)\\ &+2\bar e(t)+\mathcal A_t+\frac{2G_t}{c}(t^a-1+Kat^a). \end{aligned} \] Suppose moreover, as in Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, that the angular Wasserstein term satisfies the bound that Proposition 3.1 of \citet{zhang2023wasserstein} gives for this Pareto-type model, which is taken from that work and not proved here: there are constants $C_W>0$, $k\geq0$ and $t_W\geq1$ such that \[ W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|})\leq C_W(\log t)^k t^{-\min\{1,\beta\}}+C_W t^{-\beta s/(1-2s)}\qquad(t\geq t_W). \] These are the constants $C_W$ and $k$ in the angular term $\mathcal A_t$ above. Let \[ b_n=C_{\delta/6} \begin{cases} \left(\dfrac{\log((48/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&0<\beta<1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log n\,\log((48/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&\beta=1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log((48/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/(2+\beta)},&\beta>1, \end{cases} \] where $C_{\delta/6}$ is the constant supplied by Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}. Define \[ \begin{aligned} \gamma&=1/\beta,\qquad h=1-\alpha\gamma>0,\\ Q_n&=a(\gamma+b_n)+|\alpha|b_n,\qquad d_n=|1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n|,\\ \mathcal H_n&= \begin{cases} Q_n/(h d_n),&d_n>0,\\ 1/h,&d_n=0. \end{cases} \end{aligned} \] Use Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete}'s convention $\hat\mu_n=0$ when $d_n=0$. For sufficiently large $n$ and $t\geq t_W$, if $a\leq a_0$ and $n\pi_t\geq16\log(12/\delta)$, then, with probability at least $1-\delta$, we have $M\geq n\pi_t/4$ and \[ \left|\widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{DR}}-\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}\right| \leq |\hat\mu_n|\mathcal E_{\mathrm{DR}}+|\eta|\mathcal H_n, \] and \[ \left|\widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}-\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}\right| \leq |\hat\mu_n|\mathcal E_{\mathrm{IPW}}+|\eta|\mathcal H_n, \] where $\eta=\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb E[g(X,1,U/\|U\|)-g(X,0,U/\|U\|)\mid\|U\|>r]$. If moreover, for a constant $V<\infty$ independent of $n,t,\delta$ and every $q\in\{\mathrm{DR},\mathrm{IPW}\}$, $\mathbb E[(\psi^q)^2\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t]\leq V$ on the joint nuisance event, $t\geq\exp(1)$, $a\log t\leq1$ and $Q_n\leq h/2$, then on the same joint success event of probability at least $1-\delta$ we also have \[ \begin{aligned} |\widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{DR}}-\theta^{NETE}|\leq C\biggl(&\sqrt{\frac{V}{\delta n\pi_t}}+R_p(n/2,\delta/12)R_g(n_t,\delta/12)\\ &+\bar e(t)+\mathcal A_t+R_\alpha(n,\delta/12)\log t+b_n\biggr),\\ |\widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}-\theta^{NETE}|\leq C\biggl(&\sqrt{\frac{V}{\delta n\pi_t}}+R_p(n/2,\delta/12)\\ &+\bar e(t)+\mathcal A_t+R_\alpha(n,\delta/12)\log t+b_n\biggr), \end{aligned} \] where $C$ does not depend on $n,t,\delta$, and $\pi_t\asymp t^{-\beta}$ makes the first term of order $\sqrt{V/\delta}\,t^{\beta/2}n^{-1/2}$.
- **Proof.** Proposition~\ref{prop:id} applies because its identification and integrability conditions are assumed. The limiting angular contrast is $\eta$, and the limiting radial moment is \[ \mu=\int_1^\infty r^\alpha\beta r^{-\beta-1}\,\tmop{dr}=\frac{\beta}{\beta-\alpha}=\frac1h. \] The integral converges because $\alpha<\beta$. Consequently $\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}=\eta\mu$. Since $|g|\leq C$, we also have $|\eta|\leq2C$. For either estimator, the product identity gives \[ \widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{estimator}}-\theta^{\tmop{NETE}} =\hat\mu_n(\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{estimator}}-\eta)+\eta(\hat\mu_n-\mu). \] Let $E_\alpha$ be the joint nuisance event at tolerance $\delta/12$. The nuisance … (the corrected paper gives it in full)
- **What the correction gives up.** The unconditional claim under covariate-only exogeneity is lost, including cases where treatment and potential outcomes become dependent after conditioning on $U$. Fitted nuisances using evaluation observations and fitted-exponent pseudo-outcomes without finite conditional absolute means are excluded. The suggested first-factor bound requires $R_\alpha(n,\delta/12)\leq a_0<\beta$ and enough expected exceedances for its probability guarantee. A universal square-root sampling rate without outcome-tail control, a uniformly bounded reciprocal perturbation near its pole, the linear exponent term without control of $R_\alpha(n,\delta)\log t$, the pointwise homogeneity term $e(t)$, and the logarithm-free angular and radial rates are lost. The nuisance bounds are evaluated at the smaller confidence tolerance $\delta/12$. The appended square-root sampling and linear exponent bounds apply only to scores satisfying the stated conditional second-moment bound, thresholds $t\geq\exp(1)$ with $R_\alpha(n,\delta/12)\log t\leq1$, and perturbations satisfying $Q_n\leq h/2$; the general product bounds still retain the reciprocal denominator and full exponent perturbation outside those additional conditions. (The paper's owner accepted the weaker statement.)
- **Formal check.** proved in Lean (the corrected statement), under 25 condition(s) made explicit — `Papers.TbsExtremeEffects.nete_rate` (`TRate.lean:96`); axioms: the standard ones only (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound).

### Lemma A.1 — corrected; the corrected statement is weaker

- **As printed.** \label{lemma:mu_rate}Under Assumption \ref{assump:iid}, \ref{assump:mvary} with probability at least $1 - \delta$, for sufficiently large $n$, we have \begin{align*} | \gamma - \hat{\gamma}_n | & \leqslant O \left( \left( \frac{\log (2 / \delta)}{n} \right)^{1 / (2 + \beta)} \right), \end{align*} where $\gamma = 1 / \beta$ is the EVI of $U$.
- **What is wrong.** ``\dots choosing $k$ to be \[ k = \max \left\{ k \in \{l_n, \cdots, n\} \text{and} \ \forall i \in \{l_n, \cdots, n\}, | \hat{\gamma}(i) - \hat{\gamma}(k) | \leqslant \frac{\hat{\gamma}(i) r_n (\delta)}{\sqrt{i}} \right\} \] where $\hat{\gamma}(i) = \frac{1}{i} \sum_{j=1}^i \log \frac{\|U_{(j)}\|}{\|U_{(i+1)}\|}$ and $r_n (\delta)$ scales like $\sqrt{\log ((2 / \delta) \log (n))}$.'' The comparison at $i=n$ requires rank $n+1$, although Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} orders at most $n/2$ evaluation exceedances. In the step quoted in (a): The common-denominator Hill statistic in this step differs from Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete}'s adjacent-ratio statistic, which telescopes to $k^{-1}\log(\|U_{(1)}\|/\|U_{(k+1)}\|)$. In the step quoted in (a): Comparisons against every candidate can leave the admissible set empty, even when all candidate Hill statistics are available. In the step quoted in (a): The lower rank and tolerance are not calibrated, so their stated orders alone do not establish the adaptive deviation bound. ``By Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary}, we know that \[ g (z) = c \alpha^m \prod_{i = 1}^m (1 + z_i)^{- \alpha - 1}, \|z\|_1 > \zeta k^{(1 - 2 s) / \alpha} .\mbox{''} \] The component density uses the outcome exponent $\alpha$ instead of the tail index $\beta$. ``Let $\tilde{Z}_1, \cdots, \tilde{Z}_m \sim \alpha c^{1 / m} (1 + z)^{- \alpha - 1}, z \geqslant c^{1 / (m \alpha)} - 1$.'' The shifted component distributions alter the nominal product law, so exact proportionality of the original component density does not identify their radial convolution with the radial law of $U$. ``\dots we have that when $\|U\|_1 > \max_i \{a_i \} \zeta k^{(1 - 2 s) / \alpha}$, \[ f (t) = C \beta t^{- \beta - 1} (1 + D (1 - 1 / \beta) t^{- 1} + o (t^{- 1})) .\mbox{''} \] The constant-coefficient density expansion omits a logarithmic term at $\beta=1$. In the step quoted in (g): The stated survival coefficient $D$ corresponds to density coefficient $D(1+1/\beta)$ rather than $D(1-1/\beta)$. ``\dots we get \[ \eta (t) = \frac{1}{\beta (1 - DC^{- 1 / \beta} t^{- 1 / \beta} + o (t^{1 / \beta}))} - \frac{1}{\beta} = O (t^{- 1 / \beta}) .\mbox{''} \] At $\beta=1$ the von Mises function can have order $t^{-1}\log t$, which is not $O(t^{-1})$. In the step quoted in (i): For $\beta>1$ the survival expansion gives von Mises coefficient $-DC^{-1/\beta}/\beta^2$, with the opposite sign to the displayed substitution. ``\dots with probability at least $1 - \delta$, we have \[ | \gamma - \hat{\gamma}_n | \leqslant O \left( \left( \frac{\log (2 / \delta)}{n} \right)^{1 / (2 + \beta)} \right),\mbox{''} \] The cited adaptive deviation bound retains $\log((2/\delta)\log n)$, which cannot be replaced by $\log(2/\delta)$ for fixed $\delta$.
- **Counterexample.** Take $m=d=d_z=d_u=2$, $A=I_2$, and independent $Z_1,Z_2$ with density $(1+z)^{-2}$ on $z\geq0$. Set $U=AZ$, $\beta=1$, $l=1/4$, $u=2$, $\sigma=1/8$, $s=1/4$, $\xi=1/2$, and $\zeta=1$. Let $X=0$, let $D\sim\tmop{Ber}(1/2)$ be independent of $U$, set $Y=0$, and take independent copies for the observations. Choose even $n$, $t=1$, and $l_n=\lceil100\log n\rceil$ for sufficiently large $n$. Let $R=\|U\|_1$, whose survival is $\bar F_R(r)=2/(r+2)+2\log(r+1)/(r+2)^2$ and satisfies $\mathbb P(R>r)\leq4/r$. The comparison at $i=n$ requests an unavailable rank $n+1$; even after unavailable ranks are suppressed, with probability at least $1-4/n$ every computable adjacent-ratio candidate with $k\geq l_n$ satisfies $k^{-1}\log(\|U_{(1)}\|/\|U_{(k+1)}\|)\leq2\log n/l_n\leq1/50$, so any selected output has error from $\gamma=1$ at least $49/50$, whereas the claimed bound tends to zero.

For $n\geq2$, define \[ H_n(k)=\frac1k\sum_{j=1}^k\log\frac{R_{[j]}}{R_{[k+1]}},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n-1. \] For $\delta\in(0,1)$, set \[ \begin{aligned} L_n&=\min\{n-1,\lceil100\log n\rceil\},&J_n&=1+\lceil\log n/\log2\rceil,\\ q_n(\delta)&=\log(4J_n/\delta),&c_n(\delta)&=2\sqrt{q_n(\delta)}+\frac{q_n(\delta)}{\sqrt{L_n}},\\ \rho_n(\delta)&=16c_n(\delta). \end{aligned} \] Let \[ \begin{aligned} \widetilde k_n&=\max\bigl\{k\in\{L_n,\ldots,n-1\}:\\ &\qquad |H_n(i)-H_n(k)|\leq\frac{H_n(i)\rho_n(\delta)}{\sqrt i}\\ &\qquad \text{for every }i\in\{L_n,\ldots,k\}\bigr\},\\ \widetilde\gamma_n&=H_n(\widetilde k_n). \end{aligned} \] For each fixed $\delta\in(0,1)$ and every sufficiently large $n$, with probability at least $1-\delta$, we have \[ |\gamma-\widetilde\gamma_n|\leq C_\delta \begin{cases} \left(\dfrac{\log((8/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&0<\beta<1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log n\,\log((8/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&\beta=1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log((8/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/(2+\beta)},&\beta>1, \end{cases} \] where $C_\delta$ may depend on the fixed distribution and on $\delta$. where $\gamma = 1 / \beta$ is the EVI of $U$.

We give the exponential-spacing argument and the confidence calibration explicitly. Write $R=\|U\|_1$. Fix a member of $M_K$, put $a_i=\|A_{\cdot i}\|_1$, and let $Z_i^0$ be independent nominal Pareto type-II components of index $\beta$. The component density is a positive constant $c_0$ times the nominal product density whenever $\|z\|_1>\zeta K^{(1-2s)/\beta}$. Since \[ R=\sum_{i=1}^m a_iZ_i,\qquad l\leq a_i\leq u,\qquad R\leq u\|Z\|_1, \] the survival and density of $R$ beyond $u\zeta K^{(1-2s)/\beta}$ are $c_0$ times those of $T=\sum_i a_iZ_i^0$. This transfer uses the original … (the corrected paper gives it in full)
- **What the correction gives up.** The guarantee for the printed evaluation-exceedance adjacent-ratio estimate and the selector comparing every $i\in\{l_n,\ldots,n\}$ is replaced by a guarantee for a different full-input Hill estimate with prefix selection. ### Lemma A.2 — corrected; the corrected statement is weaker

- **As printed.** \label{lemma:eta_rate}Undet the assumption of Theorem \ref{thm:rate}, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, we have \begin{align*} | \hat{\eta}_{n, t}^{\tmop{DR}} - \eta | & \leqslant O (\sqrt{R_p (n/2, \delta) R_g (t^{- \beta} n, \delta)} + t^{\beta / 2} n^{- 1 / 2} + t^{- \min \{1, \beta\}} + t^{- \beta s / (1 - 2 s)} + e (t) + \log (t) R_{\alpha} (n,\delta)) . \end{align*}
- **What is wrong.** ``\dots by standard DML theory {\citep{foster2023orthogonal}}, we have \[ | \hat{\eta}_{n, t}^{\tmop{DR}} - \eta^t | \leqslant O \left( \sqrt{R_p (n/2, \delta) R_g (n_t, \delta)} + n_t^{- 1 / 2} \right),\mbox{''} \] Square-root concentration requires control of the normalized DR score's variance, which the assumptions do not supply, and its evaluation average has $|\mathcal I|$ summands rather than the training count $n_t$. ``\dots \[ \begin{array}{@{}c@{}}\displaystyle | \eta^t - \eta | = \left| \mathbb{E} \left[ \frac{f (X, 1, U) - f (X, 0, U)}{\|U\|^{\hat{\alpha}_{n}}} \mid \|U\|> t \right] -\mathbb{E}[g (X, 1, U /\|U\|) - g (X, 0, U /\|U\|)] \right| \\ \displaystyle \leqslant \left| \mathbb{E} \left[ \frac{f (X, 1, U) - f (X, 0, U)}{\|U\|^{\alpha}} - g (X, 1, U /\|U\|) - g (X, 0, U /\|U\|) \mid \|U\|> t \right] \right| \\ \displaystyle \quad \quad + |\mathbb{E}[g (X, 1, U /\|U\|) - g (X, 0, U /\|U\|)] -\mathbb{E}[g (X, 1, U /\|U\|) - g (X, 0, U /\|U\|) \mid \|U\|> t] | \\ \displaystyle \qquad + \left| \mathbb{E} \left[ \frac{f (X, 1, U) - f (X, 0, U)}{\|U\|^{\alpha}} \left( 1 - {\| U \|^{\alpha - \hat{\alpha}_{n}}} \right) \right] \right| \\ \displaystyle \leqslant 2 e (t) + |\mathbb{E}[g (X, 1, U /\|U\|) - g (X, 0, U /\|U\|)] -\mathbb{E}[g (X, 1, U /\|U\|) - g (X, 0, U /\|U\|) \mid \|U\|> t] |, \\ \displaystyle \qquad + \left| \mathbb{E} \left[ \frac{f (X, 1, U) - f (X, 0, U)}{\|U\|^{\alpha}} \left( 1 - {\| U \|^{\alpha - \hat{\alpha}_{n}}} \right) \right] \mid \|U\|> t \right|\end{array}\mbox{''} \] Homogeneity over exceedances bounds the remainder by $2\mathbb E[e(\|U\|)\mid\|U\|>t]$ or $2\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$, and neither quantity is bounded by $2e(t)$ for an arbitrary error function tending to zero. ``\dots we have \[ \begin{array}{@{}c@{}}\displaystyle | \eta^t - \eta | \leqslant 2 e (t) + LW_1 (\mathcal{L}_{U /\|U\|}^t, \mathcal{L}_{U /\|U\|}) + O (\log (t) R_{\alpha} (n, \delta)) \\ \displaystyle \leqslant 2 e (t) + O (t^{- \min \{1, \beta\}} + t^{- \beta s / (1 - 2 s)} + \log (t) R_{\alpha} (n, \delta)),\end{array}\mbox{''} \] At the admissible value $\beta=1$, the angular bias can have order $t^{-1}\log t$, exceeding the displayed $O(t^{-1})$ term. ``\dots \[ \begin{array}{@{}c@{}}\displaystyle \left| \mathbb{E} \left[ \frac{f (X, 1, U) - f (X, 0, U)}{\|U\|^{\alpha}} \left( 1 - {\| U \|^{\alpha - \hat{\alpha}_{n}}} \right) \right] \mid \|U\|> t \right| \leqslant C\mathbb{E} \left[ \left| 1 - {\| U\|^{\alpha - \hat{\alpha}_{n}}} \right| \mid \| U \| > t \right] \\ \displaystyle = O (\log (t) R_{\alpha} (n, \delta)) .\end{array}\mbox{''} \] A uniformly linear exponent-error bound requires control of $R_\alpha(n,\delta)\log t$, whereas $R_\alpha(n,\delta)\to0$ alone permits $t^{R_\alpha(n,\delta)}$ to grow faster than the displayed term. ``and we have \[ | \hat{\eta}_{n, t}^{\tmop{DR}} - \eta^t | \leqslant O \left( \sqrt{R_p (n/2, \delta) R_g (nt^{- \beta}, \delta)} + t^{\beta / 2} n^{- 1 / 2} \right),\mbox{''} \] Monotonicity only gives a bound at a genuine lower bound for $n_t$ and does not permit replacing $R_g(n_t,\delta)$ by $R_g(nt^{-\beta},\delta)$ across arbitrary discontinuous decreasing error functions. ``\dots we get \[ | \hat{\eta}_n^{t} - \eta | \leqslant O (\sqrt{R_p (n/2, \delta) R_g (t^{- \beta} n, \delta)} + t^{\beta / 2} n^{- 1 / 2} + t^{- \min \{1, \beta\}} + t^{- \beta s / (1 - 2 s)} + e (t) + \log (t) R_{\alpha} (n,\delta)) .\mbox{''} \] Combining nuisance, count, and evaluation guarantees with separate failure probabilities does not give total failure probability $\delta$ at the same nuisance-bound arguments. ``By Bernstein's inequality, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, \[ n_t - n\mathbb{P}(\|U\|> t)/2 \geqslant O \left( \log (1 / \delta) + \sqrt{n\mathbb{P}(\|U\|> t) \log (1 / \delta)} \right)\mbox{''} \] A Bernstein lower-tail bound subtracts its deviation term from the expected count, rather than asserting the displayed positive deviation. In the step quoted in (b): The triangle inequality requires subtracting the whole contrast $g(X,1,U/\|U\|)-g(X,0,U/\|U\|)$ and retaining tail conditioning in the exponent-error expectation. ``\dots where $\mathcal{L}_{U /\|U\|}^t$ is the distribution of $U /\|U\|$ conditioning on $\|U\|> t$ and $\mathcal{L}_{U /\|U\|}$ is its limiting distribution as $t \rightarrow \infty$.'' The treatment contrast has Lipschitz constant at most $2L$, so its Wasserstein bound requires $2L$ rather than $L$.
- **Counterexample.** Let $X\sim\tmop{Unif}([0,1])$ and $D\sim\tmop{Ber}(1/2)$ be independent, and let $U=(Z_1,Z_2)$ have independent Pareto type-II components with index $\beta=3$. Take $m=d=d_z=d_u=2$, $A=I_2$, $l=1/4$, $u=2$, $\sigma=1/8$, $s=1/4$, $\xi=1/2$, and $\zeta=1$, and use independent observations. Let $\epsilon$ be independent and symmetric with characteristic function $\mathbb E[\exp(iv\epsilon)]=\exp(-|v|^{3/2})$, and define $Y(d)=\|U\|(d+\epsilon)$ and $Y=Y(D)$. Set $\alpha=\hat\alpha_n=1$, $\hat p=1/2$, $\hat g(x,d,s)=d$, $g(x,d,s)=d$, and $e(t)=0$. Use the positive decreasing bounds $R_p(n,\delta)=R_\alpha(n,\delta)=(1+n)^{-10}$ and $R_g(q,\delta)=(1+q)^{-10}$, which cover these oracle errors surely. Take $t=n^{1/5}$ and any fixed confidence tolerance in $(0,1)$. Here $\eta=1$ and, conditional on $M=|\mathcal I|$, the estimator error has distribution $2M^{-1/3}\epsilon$; since $M$ has order $n^{2/5}$, its scale is $n^{-2/15}$ while the entire printed bound has order $n^{-1/5}$, so its coverage tends to zero.
- **Rests on.** Proposition 3.3, also found wrong as printed.
- **Correction.** Under the assumption of Theorem \ref{thm:rate}, suppose in addition that the conditions of Proposition~\ref{prop:id} hold. Suppose that the fitted propensity, regression, and exponent are measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data. Suppose that the nuisance inequalities of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} hold jointly with probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, uniformly almost surely over the covariate and direction support and both treatments, for each tolerance $\varepsilon$. Suppose that $0<p(X)<1$ almost surely and that the fitted-exponent pseudo-outcome has finite conditional absolute mean on those nuisance events. Put $\pi_t=\mathbb P(\|U\|>t)$ and $M=|\mathcal I|$, and set $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\tmop{DR}}=0$ when $M=0$. Let $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$, let $C$ bound $|g|$ on its compact domain, and put $G_t=C+\bar e(t)$. For a generic evaluation observation define \[ \psi=\hat g(X,1,U/\|U\|)-\hat g(X,0,U/\|U\|)+\frac{D-\hat p(X)}{\hat p(X)(1-\hat p(X))}\left(\frac{Y}{\|U\|^{\hat\alpha_n}}-\hat g(X,D,U/\|U\|)\right). \] For any positive truncation level $B$ chosen using only the training split and independent auxiliary data, define \[ \begin{aligned} v_t(B)&=\mathbb E[\psi^2 I\{|\psi|\leq B\}\mid\mathcal D_1,\hbox{ auxiliary data},\|U\|>t],\\ a_t(B)&=\mathbb E[|\psi|I\{|\psi|>B\}\mid\mathcal D_1,\hbox{ auxiliary data},\|U\|>t]. \end{aligned} \] Fix $a_0\in(0,\beta)$ and write $a=R_\alpha(n,\delta/4)$. For sufficiently large $t\geq1$, if $a\leq a_0$ and $n\pi_t\geq16\log(4/\delta)$, then with probability at least $1-\delta$ we have $M\geq n\pi_t/4$ and \begin{align*} |\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\tmop{DR}}-\eta|\leq{}&\sqrt{\frac{4v_t(B)}{\delta M}}+\left(1+\frac4\delta\right)a_t(B)+\frac2c R_p(n/2,\delta/4)R_g(n_t,\delta/4)\\ &+2\bar e(t)+2L W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|})+\frac{2G_t}{c}\left(t^a-1+K a t^a\right), \end{align*} where $K$ depends on the radial law and $a_0$. Suppose moreover that the angular Wasserstein term satisfies the bound that Proposition 3.1 of \citet{zhang2023wasserstein} gives for this Pareto-type model, which is taken from that work and not proved here: there are constants $C_W>0$, $k\geq0$ and $t_W\geq1$ such that \[ W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|})\leq C_W(\log t)^k t^{-\min\{1,\beta\}}+C_W t^{-\beta s/(1-2s)}\qquad(t\geq t_W). \] Then for $t\geq t_W$ the angular term $2L W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|})$ of the display is at most $2LC_W(\log t)^k t^{-\min\{1,\beta\}}+2LC_W t^{-\beta s/(1-2s)}$, with the logarithmic factor retained. When the conditional score variance $V_t$ is finite, the sampling term may instead be taken to be $\sqrt{4V_t/(\delta M)}$.
- **Proof.** Condition on the training split and independent auxiliary data, denoted together by $\mathcal T$. Write $R_U=\|U\|$, $S_U=U/\|U\|$, and \[ m_d^t(x,s)=\mathbb E[Y/R_U^{\alpha}\mid X=x,D=d,S_U=s,R_U>t],\qquad \eta_\alpha^t=\mathbb E[(f(X,1,U)-f(X,0,U))/R_U^{\alpha}\mid R_U>t]. \] Independence of $U$ from $(X,D)$ gives $\mathbb E[D\mid X,S_U,R_U>t]=p(X)$. Let $\psi_\alpha$ be the score $\psi$ with $\hat\alpha_n$ replaced by $\alpha$, keeping the same fitted functions. Direct conditional expectation over treatment and outcome gives \begin{align*} \mathbb E[\psi_\alpha\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]-\eta_\alpha^t=\mathbb E\left[(p(X)-\hat p(X))\left\{\frac{m_1^t(X,S_U)-\hat g(X,1,S_U)}{\hat … (the corrected paper gives it in full)
- **What the correction gives up.** A finite universal square-root rate for all integrable scores, bounds using only $e(t)$, a logarithm-free angular bias at $\beta=1$, uniform linear dependence on $R_\alpha\log t$, replacement of the random training count inside arbitrary decreasing $R_g$, and confidence $1-\delta$ with unchanged nuisance arguments. The suggested conditional evaluation argument also excludes fits that use the evaluation observations and fits whose nuisance guarantees hold only at a random evaluation point; its nonvacuous bound requires an integrable fitted-exponent pseudo-outcome and an exponent-error bound smaller than $\beta$. Cases in which conditional mean exogeneity given $(X,U)$ fails, or in which either potential outcome has an infinite absolute tail mean, are also excluded. The rate of the angular Wasserstein term is taken from Proposition 3.1 of \citet{zhang2023wasserstein} as an explicit assumption, with its poly-logarithmic factor, and is not proved. (An intermediate result: its strongest true version was adopted.)

### Lemma A.3 — corrected; the corrected statement is weaker

- **As printed.** \label{lemma:eta_ipw} Under the assumption of Theorem \ref{thm:rate}, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, we have \begin{align*} | \hat{\eta}_{n, t}^{\text{IPW}} - \eta | & \leqslant O (R_p (n/2, \delta) + t^{\beta / 2} n^{- 1 / 2} + t^{- \min \{1, \beta\}} + t^{- \beta s / (1 - 2 s)}) + e (t) . \end{align*}
- **What is wrong.** ``\dots we have \[ | \hat{\eta}_{n, t}^{\text{IPW}} - \eta^t | \leqslant O (R_p (n/2, \delta) + n_t^{- 1 / 2}) = O (R_p (n/2, \delta) + t^{\beta / 2} n^{- 1 / 2} + \log (t) R_{\alpha} (n,\delta)) .\mbox{''} \] The conclusion omits the exponent perturbation $\mathbb E[\|U\|^{\alpha-\hat\alpha_n}-1\mid\|U\|>t]$ present in this step, and its proposed linear order requires control of $\log(t)R_\alpha(n,\delta)$. The step quoted in (a) uses the sampling bound of Theorem 1 of \cite{su2023estimated}, which the paper assumes only for outcomes satisfying that theorem's moment bounds and its specified logistic propensity-estimation framework, but applies it to the normalized IPW scores under only the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}. ``Term $| \eta^t - \eta |$ can be bounded in the same way as in the proof of Lemma \ref{lemma:eta_rate}.'' The homogeneity error on exceedances is controlled by $\mathbb E[e(\|U\|)\mid\|U\|>t]$, which need not be bounded by $e(t)$. In the step quoted in (c): The angular bias may have order $t^{-1}\log t$ when $\beta=1$, rather than the asserted $t^{-1}$. ``The rest of the proof is similar.'' The concluding sentence derives neither the missing exponent term nor a joint event with probability at least $1-\delta$.
- **Counterexample.** Take $m=d=d_z=d_u=2$, $A=I_2$, independent nominal Pareto type-II components with $\beta=2$, and $l=1/2$, $u=2$, $\sigma=1/2$, $s=2/5$, $\xi=1/2$, and $\zeta=1$. Let $X\sim\tmop{Unif}([0,1])$ and $D\sim\tmop{Ber}(1/2)$ be independent of the components and each other, use independent copies for observations, and take $p=\hat p=1/2$ and $c=1/4$. Set $Y(d)=d$, $Y=Y(D)$, $\alpha=0$, $g(x,d,s)=\hat g(x,d,s)=d$, $e=0$, and the deterministic training-measurable fit $\hat\alpha_n=1/\log n$. Choose $R_\alpha(n,\delta)=1/\log n$, $R_p(n,\delta)=R_g(n,\delta)=n^{-10}$, $t=n^{1/6}$, and $\delta=1/4$. The IPW average converges in probability to $\exp(-1/6)$ while $\eta=1$ and the entire printed bound tends to zero.
- **Rests on.** Lemma A.2, Proposition 3.3, also found wrong as printed.
- **Correction.** Under the assumption of Theorem \ref{thm:rate}, suppose in addition that the conditions of Proposition~\ref{prop:id} hold. Suppose in addition that the conditions of Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} hold. Use its training split and independent auxiliary data, denoted together by $\mathcal T$, its $\pi_t=\mathbb P(\|U\|>t)$, $M=|\mathcal I|$, $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$, and $G_t=C+\bar e(t)$, where $C$ bounds $|g|$ on its compact domain. Set $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\text{IPW}}=0$ if $M=0$. For a generic evaluation observation define \[ \phi=\frac{Y}{\|U\|^{\hat\alpha_n}}\left(\frac{D}{\hat p(X)}-\frac{1-D}{1-\hat p(X)}\right).\] For any $B>0$ chosen using only $\mathcal T$, put \[\begin{aligned} v_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)&=\mathbb E[\phi^2 I\{|\phi|\leq B\}\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t],\\ b_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)&=\mathbb E[|\phi|I\{|\phi|>B\}\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t].\end{aligned}\] Fix $a_0\in(0,\beta)$ and put $a=R_\alpha(n,\delta/4)$. For sufficiently large $t\geq1$, if $a\leq a_0$ and $n\pi_t\geq16\log(4/\delta)$, then with probability at least $1-\delta$ we have $M\geq n\pi_t/4$ and \[\begin{aligned} |\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\text{IPW}}-\eta|\leq{}&\sqrt{\frac{4v_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)}{\delta M}}+\left(1+\frac4\delta\right)b_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)+\frac{2G_t}{c}R_p(n/2,\delta/4)\\ &+2\bar e(t)+2L W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|})+\frac{2G_t}{c}(t^a-1+K a t^a),\end{aligned}\] where $K$ depends on the radial law and $a_0$. Under the angular assumption of Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, taken from Proposition 3.1 of \citet{zhang2023wasserstein}, the angular Wasserstein term has the bound stated there for $t\geq t_W$, with its logarithmic factor retained. When $V_t^{\text{IPW}}=\tmop{Var}(\phi\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t)$ is finite, the sampling term may instead be taken to be $\sqrt{4V_t^{\text{IPW}}/(\delta M)}$.

- **What the correction gives up.** The universal square-root sampling rate for integrable scores of infinite variance, the omission of exponent error, the pointwise approximation term $e(t)$, the logarithm-free angular bound at $\beta=1$, nonuniform nuisance guarantees, fits using evaluation observations, degenerate true propensities, fitted-exponent pseudo-outcomes without finite absolute tail means, exponent-error bounds not below a fixed $a_0<\beta$, and thresholds with insufficient expected exceedance counts.
