## Errors by root cause

2 independent error(s) — 2 repaired by a minimal patch, 0 not repaired, 0 unfixable and left out of Lean; 3 result(s) inherit one.

### Errors found — repaired

The result as printed is false (the witness shows why). The smallest change that keeps the paper's key contribution was confirmed by an independent referee pass; the F stages formalize it in place of the printed statement.

Key contribution, as judged by the confirming referee: The key-result list includes prop:id, thm:rate, thm:cor-rate, and thm:cor-rate-ipw, covering identification, finite-sample bounds, and both adaptive-rate results. Their repairs preserve the NETE estimand and the contribution at the owner-accepted scope, consistently retaining the additional conditions and angular, radial, homogeneity, and exponent-error terms.

- **`prop:id`** (`Arxiv_template.tex:234-243`): The core carries the printed hypotheses and identification setting faithfully. The homogeneity display could quantify over one fixed treatment argument and radius, or uniformly over both treatment arguments and all positive radii; its sphere could also mean the usual Euclidean sphere or the positive unit sphere for the paper's norm. The proof and global norm convention determine the latter, uniform reading. The substantive error is the proof's first equality: $(Y(1),Y(0))\perp D\mid X$ and $U\perp(X,D)$ do not imply conditional exogeneity given $(X,U)$. The conclusion fails even with overlap, exact homogeneity, finite outcome means, and $\alpha<\beta$. In that moment range, the remaining radial-angular passage can be closed using Potter bounds, a higher radial moment for uniform integrability, and $\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)\to0$; bounded first moments alone do not establish uniform integrability. The preliminary radial-moment sentence also drops the additive one, although the subsequent calculation supplies the correct value $\beta/(\beta-\alpha)$.
  - witness: Take independent $X\sim\text{Unif}([0,1])$, $D\sim\text{Ber}(1/2)$, $V\sim\text{Unif}([0,1])$, and $W$ with $\mathbb P(W>w)=w^{-3}$ for $w\geq1$. Set $U=W(V,1-V)$, $\alpha=1$, and $T(w)=(1-w^{-3})^{-1/3}$. Define $Y(1)=DW+(1-D)T(W)$, $Y(0)=0$, and $Y=Y(D)$. Since $W^{-3}$ is uniform, $T(W)$ has the same law as $W$. Thus $(Y(1),Y(0))\perp D\mid X$, and $U\perp(X,D)$; independent copies satisfy the sampling assumption. Nevertheless, $f(x,1,u)=\|u\|$, $f(x,0,u)=0$, so exact homogeneity holds with $g(x,1,s)=1$, $g(x,0,s)=0$, and $e(t)=0$. The printed right-hand side is $3/2$. But $\mathbb E[W\mid W>t]=3t/2$ and $\mathbb E[T(W)\mid W>t]\to1$, giving $\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}=3/4$. The false step replaces the conditional counterfactual mean by the observed conditional mean after conditioning on $U$; neither stated independence licenses that replacement.
  - Claim: Proposition~\ref{prop:id} identifies $\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}$ from the observed conditional mean under exogeneity given $X$ and independence of $U$ from $(X,D)$.
  - What is wrong: (a) ``\dots \vsDisplay{\begin{array}{@{}c@{}}\displaystyle \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E} \left[ \frac{Y (1) - Y (0)}{t^{\alpha}} \mid \|U\|> t \right] \\ \displaystyle = \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E} \left[ \frac{f (X, 1, U) - f (X, 0, U)}{t^{\alpha}} \mid \|U\|> t \right] \\ \displaystyle = \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E} \left[ \frac{f (X, 1, U) - f (X, 0, U)}{\|U\|^{\alpha}} \cdot \left( \frac{\|U\|}{t} \right)^{\alpha} \mid \|U\|> t \right] \\ \displaystyle = \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E} \left[ (g (X, 1, U / \| U \|) - g (X, 0, U / \| U \|) + 2 e (t)) \cdot \left( \frac{\|U\|}{t} \right)^{\alpha} \mid \|U\|> t \right]\end{array}\mbox{''}} This step uses the conditional-mean identification supplied by exogeneity and consistency, which the paper assumes only for conditioning on $X$, but applies it to conditioning on $(X,U)$ and then on $\{\|U\|>t\}$. (b) In the step quoted in (a): The homogeneity inequality supplies a signed remainder bounded at the realized radius by $2e(\|U\|)$, rather than the displayed equality with the positive term $2e(t)$. (c) ``\dots \vsDisplay{\frac{\int_1^{\infty} \alpha P (\| U \| > {rt}) r^{\alpha - 1} \tmop{dr}}{P (\| U \| > t)} \leqslant 2\int^{\infty}_1 \alpha r^{\alpha -\beta + \epsilon - 1} \tmop{dr} < \infty .\mbox{''}} The choice of a positive $\epsilon$ satisfying $\alpha-\beta+\epsilon<0$ requires $\alpha<\beta$, which the proposition does not assume. (d) ``We next argue that $\lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E} [(\| U \| / t)^{\alpha} \mid \| U \| > t] = \alpha / (\beta - \alpha)$.'' The radial moment limit is $\beta/(\beta-\alpha)$ when $\alpha<\beta$, rather than $\alpha/(\beta-\alpha)$. (e) ``We conclude that $h (U)$ is uniformly integrable.'' A uniform bound on first absolute moments does not imply uniform integrability. Counterexample: Let $X\sim\tmop{Unif}([0,1])$, $D\sim\tmop{Ber}(1/2)$, and $V\sim\tmop{Unif}([0,1])$ be mutually independent. Let $W$ be independent of $(X,D,V)$ with $\mathbb P(W>w)=w^{-3}$ for $w\geq1$, and set $U=W(V,1-V)$ and $\alpha=1$. Define $T(w)=(1-w^{-3})^{-1/3}$, $Y(1)=DW+(1-D)T(W)$, $Y(0)=0$, and $Y=Y(D)$. Take independent copies for the observations, and take $g(x,1,s)=1$, $g(x,0,s)=0$, and $e(r)=0$. The variables $T(W)$ and $W$ have the same distribution, so $(Y(1),Y(0))\perp D\mid X$, while $U\perp(X,D)$ and exact homogeneity holds. The displayed product is $3/2$, whereas $\mathbb E[T(W)\mid W>t]\to1$ and $\mathbb E[W\mid W>t]=3t/2$ give $\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}=3/4$.
  - Proposed fix: The suggested version retains the identification formula but adds conditional mean exogeneity given $(X,U)$, true propensity positivity, $\alpha<\beta$, and finite absolute counterfactual tail means; its proof uses the tail supremum of $e$ and a higher radial moment. No admissible repair preserves the identification claim of Proposition~\ref{prop:id} at its printed scope.
  - Gives up: Identification for distributions that satisfy exogeneity given $X$ but violate conditional mean exogeneity given $(X,U)$, distributions with a degenerate true propensity, the range $\alpha\geq\beta$, and counterfactuals without finite absolute tail means.
  - repair confirmed by an independent referee pass (see Corrected version); the corrected statement is proved in Lean
  - corrected statement: Suppose that $U$ is multivariate regularly varying and Assumption~\ref{assump:exo}, \ref{assump:iid}, \ref{assump:f_homo} hold. Suppose in addition that $\alpha<\beta$, $0<p(X)<1$ almost surely, and, for $d\in\{0,1\}$, $\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U,D]=\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U]$ almost surely. Suppose that $\mathbb E[|Y(d)|\mid\|U\|>t]<\infty$ for both treatments and all sufficiently large $t$. Then we have \begin{align*} \theta^{\tmop{NETE}} &= \lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb E[g(X,1,U/\|U\|)-g(X,0,U/\|U\|)\mid\|U\|>t]\cdot\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb E[\|U\|^{\alpha}/t^{\alpha}\mid\|U\|>t]. \end{align*}
  - contribution narrowed: Identification for distributions that satisfy exogeneity given $X$ but violate conditional mean exogeneity given $(X,U)$, distributions with a degenerate true propensity, the range $\alpha\geq\beta$, and counterfactuals without finite absolute tail means. (the owner accepted the weaker version)
  - inherited by: `thm:rate`, `lem:eta-rate`, `lem:eta-ipw`
- **`lem:mu-rate`** (`Arxiv_template.tex:607-616`): The core faithfully retains the assumptions and algorithm reference. (a) The unresolved readings are the outcome exponent versus a local tail-index meaning of $\alpha$, and the algorithm's adjacent-ratio statistic versus the proof's common-denominator Hill statistic. The input collection, admissible ranks, and calibration of $l_n$ and $r_n(\delta)$ are not reconciled. (b) Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} compares candidates through $n$, although its evaluation collection contains at most $n/2$ observations; moreover, its displayed statistic telescopes and lacks the weights required for a Hill estimator. (c) The proof's comparison against every candidate index is not the cited adaptive selection rule and can leave the admissible set empty even after removing unavailable ranks. (d) The asserted second-order tail expansion, and hence $\eta(t)=O(t^{-1/\beta})$, fail at the admissible value $\beta=1$, where a logarithmic term is present. (e) Two additional proof slips can be closed locally: the proportional component tail should be transferred using the original product Pareto law, rather than the shifted component law; and differentiating the displayed survival expansion gives the density coefficient $D(1+1/\beta)$, not $D(1-1/\beta)$. These corrections do not resolve (b)--(d). (f) I also could not close the final removal of the adaptation factor: [Boucheron and Thomas, Corollary 3.12](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1503.05077) retains $\log((2/\delta)\log n)$, whereas the printed rate retains only $\log(2/\delta)$.
  - witness: (b) Take independent Pareto type-II components $Z_1,Z_2$ with $\beta=1$, $U=Z$, $A=I_2$, $m=d=d_z=d_u=2$, $l=1/4$, $u=2$, $\sigma=1/8$, $s=1/4$, $\xi=1/2$, and $\zeta=1$, with independent observations and fixed threshold $t=1$. This exact product law satisfies every stated tail-model restriction. Nevertheless, $M=|\mathcal I|\leq n/2$, so the comparison at $i=n$ requires an unavailable order statistic. Independently, the algorithm's display equals $k^{-1}\log(\|U_{(1)}\|/\|U_{(k+1)}\|)$, whereas the proof's statistic equals $k^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^k j\log(\|U_{(j)}\|/\|U_{(j+1)}\|)$. They are different random variables. For the permitted choice $l_n=\lceil(\log n)^2\rceil$, every computable algorithmic candidate satisfies $0\leq\hat\gamma_k\leq\log(\max_i\|U_i\|/t)/l_n=O_{\mathbb P}(1/\log n)$, incompatible with $\gamma=1$. (c) On the same radial law, even using the proof's full-sample Hill statistic and deleting the unavailable endpoint, choose $l_n=\lceil(\log n)^2\rceil$ and $r_n(\delta)$ of the stated order. Then $\hat\gamma(l_n)\to1$ in probability, while $\hat\gamma(n-1)=\tfrac12\log n+O_{\mathbb P}(1)$ because the minimum radius is of order $n^{-1/2}$. Both comparison tolerances tend to zero. No candidate can satisfy comparisons with both indices, so the printed maximum is empty with probability tending to one. (d) Direct convolution on this exact product law gives $\bar F(t)=2/(t+2)+2\log(t+1)/(t+2)^2=2t^{-1}+(2\log t-4)t^{-2}+o(t^{-2})$. Thus the displayed constant-coefficient second-order expansion is false. Its tail quantile satisfies $U(q)=2q+\log(2q)-2+o(1)$, and the von Mises function has leading term $-\log q/(2q)$, which is not $O(q^{-1})$. This failure persists even under the favorable interpretation that the density calculation's $\alpha$ means $\beta$.
  - Claim: Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate} asserts that the radial estimator in Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} has error of order $(\log(2/\delta)/n)^{1/(2+\beta)}$ under the stated Pareto-type model.
  - What is wrong: (a) ``\dots choosing $k$ to be \vsDisplay{k = \max \left\{ k \in \{l_n, \cdots, n\} \text{and} \ \forall i \in \{l_n, \cdots, n\}, | \hat{\gamma}(i) - \hat{\gamma}(k) | \leqslant \frac{\hat{\gamma}(i) r_n (\delta)}{\sqrt{i}} \right\}} where $\hat{\gamma}(i) = \frac{1}{i} \sum_{j=1}^i \log \frac{\|U_{(j)}\|}{\|U_{(i+1)}\|}$ and $r_n (\delta)$ scales like $\sqrt{\log ((2 / \delta) \log (n))}$.'' The comparison at $i=n$ requires rank $n+1$, although Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} orders at most $n/2$ evaluation exceedances. (b) In the step quoted in (a): The common-denominator Hill statistic in this step differs from Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete}'s adjacent-ratio statistic, which telescopes to $k^{-1}\log(\|U_{(1)}\|/\|U_{(k+1)}\|)$. (c) In the step quoted in (a): Comparisons against every candidate can leave the admissible set empty, even when all candidate Hill statistics are available. (d) In the step quoted in (a): The lower rank and tolerance are not calibrated, so their stated orders alone do not establish the adaptive deviation bound. (e) ``By Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary}, we know that \vsDisplay{g (z) = c \alpha^m \prod_{i = 1}^m (1 + z_i)^{- \alpha - 1}, \|z\|_1 > \zeta k^{(1 - 2 s) / \alpha} .\mbox{''}} The component density uses the outcome exponent $\alpha$ instead of the tail index $\beta$. (f) ``Let $\tilde{Z}_1, \cdots, \tilde{Z}_m \sim \alpha c^{1 / m} (1 + z)^{- \alpha - 1}, z \geqslant c^{1 / (m \alpha)} - 1$.'' The shifted component distributions alter the nominal product law, so exact proportionality of the original component density does not identify their radial convolution with the radial law of $U$. (g) ``\dots we have that when $\|U\|_1 > \max_i \{a_i \} \zeta k^{(1 - 2 s) / \alpha}$, \vsDisplay{f (t) = C \beta t^{- \beta - 1} (1 + D (1 - 1 / \beta) t^{- 1} + o (t^{- 1})) .\mbox{''}} The constant-coefficient density expansion omits a logarithmic term at $\beta=1$. (h) In the step quoted in (g): The stated survival coefficient $D$ corresponds to density coefficient $D(1+1/\beta)$ rather than $D(1-1/\beta)$. (i) ``\dots we get \vsDisplay{\eta (t) = \frac{1}{\beta (1 - DC^{- 1 / \beta} t^{- 1 / \beta} + o (t^{1 / \beta}))} - \frac{1}{\beta} = O (t^{- 1 / \beta}) .\mbox{''}} At $\beta=1$ the von Mises function can have order $t^{-1}\log t$, which is not $O(t^{-1})$. (j) In the step quoted in (i): For $\beta>1$ the survival expansion gives von Mises coefficient $-DC^{-1/\beta}/\beta^2$, with the opposite sign to the displayed substitution. (k) ``\dots with probability at least $1 - \delta$, we have \vsDisplay{| \gamma - \hat{\gamma}_n | \leqslant O \left( \left( \frac{\log (2 / \delta)}{n} \right)^{1 / (2 + \beta)} \right),\mbox{''}} The cited adaptive deviation bound retains $\log((2/\delta)\log n)$, which cannot be replaced by $\log(2/\delta)$ for fixed $\delta$. Counterexample: Take $m=d=d_z=d_u=2$, $A=I_2$, and independent $Z_1,Z_2$ with density $(1+z)^{-2}$ on $z\geq0$. Set $U=AZ$, $\beta=1$, $l=1/4$, $u=2$, $\sigma=1/8$, $s=1/4$, $\xi=1/2$, and $\zeta=1$. Let $X=0$, let $D\sim\tmop{Ber}(1/2)$ be independent of $U$, set $Y=0$, and take independent copies for the observations. Choose even $n$, $t=1$, and $l_n=\lceil100\log n\rceil$ for sufficiently large $n$. Let $R=\|U\|_1$, whose survival is $\bar F_R(r)=2/(r+2)+2\log(r+1)/(r+2)^2$ and satisfies $\mathbb P(R>r)\leq4/r$. The comparison at $i=n$ requests an unavailable rank $n+1$; even after unavailable ranks are suppressed, with probability at least $1-4/n$ every computable adjacent-ratio candidate with $k\geq l_n$ satisfies $k^{-1}\log(\|U_{(1)}\|/\|U_{(k+1)}\|)\leq2\log n/l_n\leq1/50$, so any selected output has error from $\gamma=1$ at least $49/50$, whereas the claimed bound tends to zero.
  - Proposed fix: The suggested statement replaces the evaluation-exceedance adjacent-ratio estimate by the separately defined full-input Hill estimate $\widetilde\gamma_n$, with ranks through $n-1$, calibrated tolerance, and prefix comparisons. Its proof uses the original product Pareto law and the standing $\ell_1$ norm, and its bound retains the adaptation logarithm and the endpoint logarithm at $\beta=1$. Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} and Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} can use this radial bound only for NETE estimators and thresholds formed with $\widetilde\gamma_n$; it supplies no vanishing radial bound for their printed evaluation-exceedance adjacent-ratio estimate.
  - Gives up: The guarantee for the printed evaluation-exceedance adjacent-ratio estimate and the selector comparing every $i\in\{l_n,\ldots,n\}$ is replaced by a guarantee for a different full-input Hill estimate with prefix selection. The logarithm-free radial bound is lost, and the radial exponent $1/(2+\beta)$ is replaced by $1/3$ for $0<\beta<1$.

For $n\geq2$, define \[ H_n(k)=\frac1k\sum_{j=1}^k\log\frac{R_{[j]}}{R_{[k+1]}},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n-1. \] For $\delta\in(0,1)$, set \[ \begin{aligned} L_n&=\min\{n-1,\lceil100\log n\rceil\},&J_n&=1+\lceil\log n/\log2\rceil,\\ q_n(\delta)&=\log(4J_n/\delta),&c_n(\delta)&=2\sqrt{q_n(\delta)}+\frac{q_n(\delta)}{\sqrt{L_n}},\\ \rho_n(\delta)&=16c_n(\delta). \end{aligned} \] Let \[ \begin{aligned} \widetilde k_n&=\max\bigl\{k\in\{L_n,\ldots,n-1\}:\\ &\qquad |H_n(i)-H_n(k)|\leq\frac{H_n(i)\rho_n(\delta)}{\sqrt i}\\ &\qquad \text{for every }i\in\{L_n,\ldots,k\}\bigr\},\\ \widetilde\gamma_n&=H_n(\widetilde k_n). \end{aligned} \] For each fixed $\delta\in(0,1)$ and every sufficiently large $n$, with probability at least $1-\delta$, we have \[ |\gamma-\widetilde\gamma_n|\leq C_\delta \begin{cases} \left(\dfrac{\log((8/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&0<\beta<1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log n\,\log((8/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&\beta=1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log((8/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/(2+\beta)},&\beta>1, \end{cases} \] where $C_\delta$ may depend on the fixed distribution and on $\delta$.      where $\gamma = 1 / \beta$ is the EVI of $U$.

### `prop:id` (`Arxiv_template.tex:234-243`)

- as printed: \label{prop:id}Suppose that $U$ is multivariate regularly varying and Assumption~\ref{assump:exo}, \ref{assump:iid}, \ref{assump:f_homo} hold, we have \begin{align*} \theta^{\tmop{NETE}} = \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E}[g (X, 1, U /\|U\|) - g (X, 0, U /\|U\|) \mid \|U\|> t] \cdot \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E}[\|U\|^{\alpha} / t^{\alpha} \mid \|U\|> t] . \end{align*}
- corrected: Suppose that $U$ is multivariate regularly varying and Assumption~\ref{assump:exo}, \ref{assump:iid}, \ref{assump:f_homo} hold. Suppose in addition that $\alpha<\beta$, $0<p(X)<1$ almost surely, and, for $d\in\{0,1\}$, $\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U,D]=\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U]$ almost surely. Suppose that $\mathbb E[|Y(d)|\mid\|U\|>t]<\infty$ for both treatments and all sufficiently large $t$. Then we have \begin{align*} \theta^{\tmop{NETE}} &= \lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb E[g(X,1,U/\|U\|)-g(X,0,U/\|U\|)\mid\|U\|>t]\cdot\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb E[\|U\|^{\alpha}/t^{\alpha}\mid\|U\|>t]. \end{align*}
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-33`: $U\perp(X,D)$. — pins reading of ass:iid-independence: The opening model description calls $U$ an independent extreme-noise vector, and Assumption~\ref{assump:iid} states that it is independent of $X,D$. Standard multivariate independence treats the listed variables jointly.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-34`: $\|X\|_1\leqslant R$ almost surely. — pins reading of ass:f-homo-bounded-x: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} bounds the random covariate, and the Notations paragraph explicitly identifies both norm symbols with the $\ell_1$-norm.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-38`: For each norm, there exist $S$, $b_n\rightarrow\infty$, and $c>0$ such that, for every $a>0$, the polar measures $n\tmop{P}((\|U\|/b_n,U/\|U\|)\in\cdot)$ restricted to $[a,\infty]\times\mathbb{S}^+$ converge weakly as finite measures to the corresponding restriction of $c\nu_\beta\times S$. — pins reading of def:regular-variation: Definition~\ref{def:mvarying} specifies $\nu_\beta([r,\infty))=r^{-\beta}$, which is finite away from zero and infinite over the entire radial space. The immediately following discussion identifies the intended Pareto tail limit, fixing the standard tail-measure interpretation.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-39`: $f$ is a fixed measurable version satisfying $f(X,D,U)=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X,D,U]$ almost surely, and this same version is used in Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo}. — pins reading of def:conditional-mean: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} first defines $f$ through conditional expectation and then immediately imposes a pointwise homogeneity property on that function. The standard meaning of conditional expectation fixes the equality almost surely.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-43`: The homogeneity inequality holds for every $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every $t>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R=\{x:\|x\|_1\leqslant R\}$ and $u\in S^{d-1}=\{u\in\mathbb R_+^d:\|u\|_1=1\}$, with $g$ being $L$-Lipschitz on this domain under the $\ell_1$ norm convention. — pins reading of ass:f-homo-asymptotic: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} defines the approximation using the conditional mean on the nonnegative noise domain. The Notations paragraph fixes the norm; Proposition~\ref{prop:id} uses both treatment values, and its added homogeneity condition explicitly specifies every positive radius and the positive unit sphere.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-7`: $Y(D)=Y$ almost surely throughout the paper. — pins reading of ass:consistency: The Causal Inference paragraph states consistency for random variables in the potential-outcome framework; equality of random variables in this framework is understood almost surely.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-8cd5c960`: The homogeneity inequality holds for every $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every radius $t>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R$ and $u\in\mathbb R_+^d$ with $\|u\|_1=1$. — pins reading of prop:id: stated by the repair
- proof change: the printed proof, revised step by step] Write $R_U=\|U\|$, $S_U=U/\|U\|$, and $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$; the last quantity is finite for sufficiently large $t$ and tends to zero. Independence of $U$ from $(X,D)$ gives $\mathbb P(D=1\mid X,U)=p(X)$. Positivity, consistency, and conditional mean exogeneity given $(X,U)$ therefore give, for both treatments, \[ f(X,d,U)=\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U,D=d]=\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U]. \] The assumed absolute tail means justify taking conditional expectations on $\{R_U>t\}$. Homogeneity at the realized radius yields \[ \frac{f(X,1,U)-f(X,0,U)}{R_U^{\alpha}}=g(X,1,S_U)-g(X,0,S_U)+\varepsilon(X,U),\qquad |\varepsilon(X,U)|\leq2\bar e(t)\quad\hbox{on }\{R_U>t\}. \] Consequently, \[ \left|\mathbb E\left[\frac{Y(1)-Y(0)}{t^{\alpha}}\mid R_U>t\right]-\mathbb E\left[(g(X,1,S_U)-g(X,0,S_U))(R_U/t)^{\alpha}\mid R_U>t\right]\right|\leq2\bar e(t)\mathbb E[(R_U/t)^{\alpha}\mid R_U>t]. \] We next establish that the radial moment tends to $\beta/(\beta-\alpha)$. For every real $a<\beta$, the identity $x^a=1+\int_1^x a r^{a-1}\,\tmop{dr}$, together with absolute integrability of the integral, gives \[ \mathbb E[(R_U/t)^a\mid R_U>t]=1+\int_1^{\infty}a r^{a-1}\frac{\mathbb P(R_U>tr)}{\mathbb P(R_U>t)}\,\tmop{dr}. \] For $a=0$ the integral is zero; for $a<0$ its absolute integrability follows from $\int_1^{\infty}|a|r^{a-1}\,\tmop{dr}=1$.  Note that $\| U \|$ is also regularly varying. By Potter's theorem \cite[Theorem 1.56]{bingham1989regular}, for any $\epsilon >0$ and sufficiently large $t$, we have \begin{align*}   \frac{P (\| U \| > {rt})}{P (\| U \| > t)} & \leqslant 2 r^{-\beta + \epsilon} . \end{align*}  For $0<a<\beta$, choose $0<\epsilon<\beta-a$. The Potter bound dominates the absolute integrand by $2a r^{a-\beta+\epsilon-1}$, whose integral is finite. For $a<0$, the bound by $|a|r^{a-1}$ also permits dominated convergence. Regular variation thus gives, for every $a<\beta$, \[ \mathbb E[(R_U/t)^a\mid R_U>t]\longrightarrow1+\int_1^{\infty}a r^{a-\beta-1}\,\tmop{dr}=\frac{\beta}{\beta-a}. \] In particular, the radial moments with $a=\alpha$ are bounded, and the preceding homogeneity error tends to zero. Define \[ G(s)=\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,s)-g(X,0,s)],\qquad h_t(r,s)=G(s)r^{\alpha}. \] Independence of $X$ and $U$ shows that the remaining conditional expectation is $\mathbb E[h_t(R_U/t,S_U)\mid R_U>t]$. Note that by Assumption \ref{assump:f_homo}, $g$ is a continuous function on a compact set and thus is bounded by some constant $C > 0$. Thus $|G(s)|\leq2C$, and $G$ is continuous. If $\alpha\leq0$, then $|h_t(r,s)|\leq2C$ for $r\geq1$. If $\alpha>0$, choose $p>1$ with $p\alpha<\beta$. The radial moment calculation gives \[ \sup_{t\geq t_0}\mathbb E[|G(S_U)(R_U/t)^{\alpha}|^p\mid R_U>t]\leq(2C)^p\sup_{t\geq t_0}\mathbb E[(R_U/t)^{p\alpha}\mid R_U>t]<\infty. \] This establishes uniform integrability in both cases. Definition~\ref{def:mvarying} gives conditional weak convergence of $(R_U/t,S_U)$ to the product of a Pareto radial law with survival $r^{-\beta}$ for $r\geq1$ and the spectral probability measure $S$. For a bounded truncation of $G(s)r^{\alpha}$, weak convergence gives convergence of expectations. Uniform integrability makes the truncation errors tend to zero uniformly, so \[ \lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb E[G(S_U)(R_U/t)^{\alpha}\mid R_U>t]=\left(\int G(s)\,S(\tmop{ds})\right)\frac{\beta}{\beta-\alpha}. \] Weak convergence of the angular marginal and bounded continuity of $G$ also give \[ \lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb E[g(X,1,S_U)-g(X,0,S_U)\mid R_U>t]=\int G(s)\,S(\tmop{ds}). \] Combining these two limits with the vanishing homogeneity error proves the stated product formula.
- why: The core carries the printed hypotheses and identification setting faithfully. The homogeneity display could quantify over one fixed treatment argument and radius, or uniformly over both treatment arguments and all positive radii; its sphere could also mean the usual Euclidean sphere or the positive unit sphere for the paper's norm. The proof and global norm convention determine the latter, uniform reading. The substantive error is the proof's first equality: $(Y(1),Y(0))\perp D\mid X$ and $U\perp(X,D)$ do not imply conditional exogeneity given $(X,U)$. The conclusion fails even with overlap, exact homogeneity, finite outcome means, and $\alpha<\beta$. In that moment range, the remaining radial-angular passage can be closed using Potter bounds, a higher radial moment for uniform integrability, and $\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)\to0$; bounded first moments alone do not establish uniform integrability. The preliminary radial-moment sentence also drops the additive one, although the subsequent calculation supplies the correct value $\beta/(\beta-\alpha)$.

### `thm:rate` (`Arxiv_template.tex:330-357`)

- as printed: \label{thm:rate}Suppose that Assumption \ref{assump:exo}, \ref{assump:overlap}, \ref{assump:iid}, \ref{assump:f_homo}, \ref{assump:mvary} hold, $\alpha < \beta$, where $\alpha$ and $\beta$ are defined in \cref{assump:f_homo} and \cref{assump:mvary} respectively. Furthermore, for any fixed $t$, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, \begin{align*} |p (X) - \hat{p} (X) | \leqslant R_p (n, \delta) & , | \hat{\alpha}_{n} - \alpha| \leqslant R_\alpha(n,\delta),\\ |\mathbb{E}[Y/\|U\|^{\alpha} \mid X, D, U /\|U\|, \|U\|> t] &- \hat{g} (X, D, U /\|U\|) | \leqslant R_g (n_t, \delta), \end{align*} where $n_t = \sum_{i = 1}^{n/2} I (\|U_i \|> t)$ and $R_p, R_g, R_\alpha$ are estimation errors that are monotonically decreasing with respect to sample size. Then, with probability at least $1 - \delta, \delta \in (0, 1 / 2)$, we have \begin{align} \left| {{\widehat{\theta }_{n, t}^{\text{DR} }} } - \theta^{NETE} \right| & \leqslant O (\sqrt{R_p (n/2, \delta)R_g ({n_t}, \delta)} + t^{\beta / 2} n^{-1 / 2}+ \log (1 / \delta) n^{- 1 / (2 +\beta)} \notag\\ & \quad\quad \quad \quad + t^{- \min \{1, \beta\}} + t^{- \beta s / (1 - 2 s)} + \log(t)R_\alpha(n,\delta)+ e (t)).\label{eq:rate_dr} \end{align} and \begin{align} \left| {{\widehat{\theta }_{n, t}^{\text{IPW}}} } - \theta^{NETE} \right| & \leqslant O (R_p (n/2, \delta) +t^{\beta / 2} n^{- 1 / 2} + \log (1 / \delta) n^{- 1 / (2 + \beta)} \notag\\ &\quad\quad \quad \quad + t^{- \min \{1, \beta\}} + t^{- \beta s / (1 - 2 s)}+ \log(t)R_\alpha(n,\delta) + e (t)). \label{eq:rate_ipw} \end{align}
- corrected: Suppose that Assumption \ref{assump:exo}, \ref{assump:overlap}, \ref{assump:iid}, \ref{assump:f_homo}, \ref{assump:mvary} hold, $\alpha < \beta$, where $\alpha$ and $\beta$ are defined in \cref{assump:f_homo} and \cref{assump:mvary} respectively. Suppose in addition that the conditions of Proposition~\ref{prop:id} hold. Suppose that the fitted propensity, regression and exponent are measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data, denoted together by $\mathcal T$, and that $\mathbb E[|Y/\|U\|^{\hat\alpha_n}|\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t]<\infty$ on each joint nuisance event. Furthermore, for any fixed $t$, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, \begin{align*} |p (X) - \hat{p} (X) | \leqslant R_p (n, \delta) & , | \hat{\alpha}_{n} - \alpha| \leqslant R_\alpha(n,\delta),\\ |\mathbb{E}[Y/\|U\|^{\alpha} \mid X, D, U /\|U\|, \|U\|> t] &- \hat{g} (X, D, U /\|U\|) | \leqslant R_g (n_t, \delta), \end{align*} where $n_t = \sum_{i = 1}^{n/2} I (\|U_i \|> t)$ and $R_p, R_g, R_\alpha$ are estimation errors that are monotonically decreasing with respect to sample size. Fix $\delta\in(0,1/2)$ and a deterministic threshold $t$. Run the adaptive Hill selection in Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} at tolerance $\delta/6$, and write its full-input output as $\hat\gamma_n=\widetilde\gamma_n$. Put $\pi_t=\mathbb P(\|U\|>t)$, $M=|\mathcal I|$, $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$, and $G_t=C+\bar e(t)$, where $C$ bounds $|g|$ on its compact domain. Use zero for each first-factor estimate when $M=0$. For a generic evaluation observation put $S_U=U/\|U\|$ and define \[ \begin{aligned} \psi^{\mathrm{DR}}={}&\hat g(X,1,S_U)-\hat g(X,0,S_U)\\ &+\frac{D-\hat p(X)}{\hat p(X)(1-\hat p(X))}\left(\frac{Y}{\|U\|^{\hat\alpha_n}}-\hat g(X,D,S_U)\right),\\ \psi^{\mathrm{IPW}}={}&\frac{Y}{\|U\|^{\hat\alpha_n}}\left(\frac{D}{\hat p(X)}-\frac{1-D}{1-\hat p(X)}\right). \end{aligned} \] For each $q\in\{\mathrm{DR},\mathrm{IPW}\}$, choose $B_q>0$ using only $\mathcal T$, and put \[ \begin{aligned} v_t^q(B_q)&=\mathbb E[(\psi^q)^2I\{|\psi^q|\leq B_q\}\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t],\\ b_t^q(B_q)&=\mathbb E[|\psi^q|I\{|\psi^q|>B_q\}\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t],\\ \mathcal S_t^q&=\sqrt{\frac{12v_t^q(B_q)}{\delta\max\{M,1\}}}+\left(1+\frac{12}{\delta}\right)b_t^q(B_q). \end{aligned} \] Fix $a_0\in(0,\beta)$ and put \[ \begin{aligned} a&=R_\alpha(n,\delta/12),\qquad K=\frac{4}{\beta-a_0},\\ \mathcal A_t&=2LC_W\left((\log t)^k t^{-\min\{1,\beta\}}+t^{-\beta s/(1-2s)}\right),\\ \mathcal E_{\mathrm{DR}}={}&\mathcal S_t^{\mathrm{DR}}+\frac2c R_p(n/2,\delta/12)R_g(n_t,\delta/12)\\ &+2\bar e(t)+\mathcal A_t+\frac{2G_t}{c}(t^a-1+Kat^a),\\ \mathcal E_{\mathrm{IPW}}={}&\mathcal S_t^{\mathrm{IPW}}+\frac{2G_t}{c}R_p(n/2,\delta/12)\\ &+2\bar e(t)+\mathcal A_t+\frac{2G_t}{c}(t^a-1+Kat^a). \end{aligned} \] Suppose moreover, as in Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, that the angular Wasserstein term satisfies the bound that Proposition 3.1 of \citet{zhang2023wasserstein} gives for this Pareto-type model, which is taken from that work and not proved here: there are constants $C_W>0$, $k\geq0$ and $t_W\geq1$ such that \[ W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|})\leq C_W(\log t)^k t^{-\min\{1,\beta\}}+C_W t^{-\beta s/(1-2s)}\qquad(t\geq t_W). \] These are the constants $C_W$ and $k$ in the angular term $\mathcal A_t$ above. Let \[ b_n=C_{\delta/6} \begin{cases} \left(\dfrac{\log((48/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&0<\beta<1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log n\,\log((48/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&\beta=1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log((48/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/(2+\beta)},&\beta>1, \end{cases} \] where $C_{\delta/6}$ is the constant supplied by Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}. Define \[ \begin{aligned} \gamma&=1/\beta,\qquad h=1-\alpha\gamma>0,\\ Q_n&=a(\gamma+b_n)+|\alpha|b_n,\qquad d_n=|1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n|,\\ \mathcal H_n&= \begin{cases} Q_n/(h d_n),&d_n>0,\\ 1/h,&d_n=0. \end{cases} \end{aligned} \] Use Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete}'s convention $\hat\mu_n=0$ when $d_n=0$. For sufficiently large $n$ and $t\geq t_W$, if $a\leq a_0$ and $n\pi_t\geq16\log(12/\delta)$, then, with probability at least $1-\delta$, we have $M\geq n\pi_t/4$ and \[ \left|\widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{DR}}-\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}\right| \leq |\hat\mu_n|\mathcal E_{\mathrm{DR}}+|\eta|\mathcal H_n, \] and \[ \left|\widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}-\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}\right| \leq |\hat\mu_n|\mathcal E_{\mathrm{IPW}}+|\eta|\mathcal H_n, \] where $\eta=\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb E[g(X,1,U/\|U\|)-g(X,0,U/\|U\|)\mid\|U\|>r]$. If moreover, for a constant $V<\infty$ independent of $n,t,\delta$ and every $q\in\{\mathrm{DR},\mathrm{IPW}\}$, $\mathbb E[(\psi^q)^2\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t]\leq V$ on the joint nuisance event, $t\geq\exp(1)$, $a\log t\leq1$ and $Q_n\leq h/2$, then on the same joint success event of probability at least $1-\delta$ we also have \[ \begin{aligned} |\widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{DR}}-\theta^{NETE}|\leq C\biggl(&\sqrt{\frac{V}{\delta n\pi_t}}+R_p(n/2,\delta/12)R_g(n_t,\delta/12)\\ &+\bar e(t)+\mathcal A_t+R_\alpha(n,\delta/12)\log t+b_n\biggr),\\ |\widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}-\theta^{NETE}|\leq C\biggl(&\sqrt{\frac{V}{\delta n\pi_t}}+R_p(n/2,\delta/12)\\ &+\bar e(t)+\mathcal A_t+R_\alpha(n,\delta/12)\log t+b_n\biggr), \end{aligned} \] where $C$ does not depend on $n,t,\delta$, and $\pi_t\asymp t^{-\beta}$ makes the first term of order $\sqrt{V/\delta}\,t^{\beta/2}n^{-1/2}$.

Standard multivariate independence treats the listed variables jointly.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-34`: $\|X\|_1\leqslant R$ almost surely. — pins reading of ass:f-homo-bounded-x: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} bounds the random covariate, and the Notations paragraph explicitly identifies both norm symbols with the $\ell_1$-norm.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-36`: For each component law in $\tilde M_k$, there exists one constant $C_k>0$, independent of $z$, such that $h(z)=C_k\prod_{i=1}^{m}(1+z_i)^{-(\beta+1)}$ whenever $\|z\|_1>\zeta k^{(1-2s)/\beta}$. — pins reading of ass:mvary-density: Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} uses $\varpropto$ on the stated tail region and separately bounds the relative error from the normalized nominal density. Proportionality fixes a constant multiplier, without requiring that multiplier to equal $\beta^m$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-38`: For each norm, there exist $S$, $b_n\rightarrow\infty$, and $c>0$ such that, for every $a>0$, the polar measures $n\tmop{P}((\|U\|/b_n,U/\|U\|)\in\cdot)$ restricted to $[a,\infty]\times\mathbb{S}^+$ converge weakly as finite measures to the corresponding restriction of $c\nu_\beta\times S$. — pins reading of def:regular-variation: Definition~\ref{def:mvarying} specifies $\nu_\beta([r,\infty))=r^{-\beta}$, which is finite away from zero and infinite over the entire radial space. The immediately following discussion identifies the intended Pareto tail limit, fixing the standard tail-measure interpretation.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-39`: $f$ is a fixed measurable version satisfying $f(X,D,U)=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X,D,U]$ almost surely, and this same version is used in Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo}. — pins reading of def:conditional-mean: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} first defines $f$ through conditional expectation and then immediately imposes a pointwise homogeneity property on that function. The standard meaning of conditional expectation fixes the equality almost surely.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-41`: The existential cutoff $t_W$ is chosen sufficiently large for Lemmas~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} and~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw} and so that $\sup_{r\geq t_W}e(r)\leq1$ and $\mathbb P(\|U\|>tr)/\mathbb P(\|U\|>t)\leq4r^{-\beta}$ for every $t\geq t_W$ and $r\geq1$. — pins reading of thm:rate: The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} invokes the first-factor lemmas' sufficiently-large-threshold conclusions, uses the radial moment inequality with $K=4/(\beta-a_0)$, and bounds $G_t$ by $C+1$. The stated Pareto-type radial law and $e(t)\to0$ permit this enlargement of the existential cutoff.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-42`: For each fixed $\mathcal T$-measurable choice $B>0$, the displayed probability bound uses the base conditions of Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}; its angular specialization additionally uses that lemma's angular assumption and $t\geq t_W$, and its variance alternative additionally requires $V_t^{\text{IPW}}<\infty$. — pins reading of lem:eta-ipw: The statement explicitly says ``Under the angular assumption'' and ``When'' the variance is finite; the amended proof applies conditional sampling inequalities to the chosen $B$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-43`: The homogeneity inequality holds for every $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every $t>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R=\{x:\|x\|_1\leqslant R\}$ and $u\in S^{d-1}=\{u\in\mathbb R_+^d:\|u\|_1=1\}$, with $g$ being $L$-Lipschitz on this domain under the $\ell_1$ norm convention. — pins reading of ass:f-homo-asymptotic: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} defines the approximation using the conditional mean on the nonnegative noise domain. The Notations paragraph fixes the norm; Proposition~\ref{prop:id} uses both treatment values, and its added homogeneity condition explicitly specifies every positive radius and the positive unit sphere.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-44`: For each fixed $t$ and tolerance $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, the three nuisance inequalities, with $\delta=\varepsilon$, hold on one $\mathcal T$-measurable event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, simultaneously almost surely over the covariate support, the conditional direction support and both treatments, where $\mathcal T$ consists of the training split and independent auxiliary data. — pins reading of ass:rate-nuisance-errors: Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} fits nuisances on the training split before evaluation; Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}'s stated nuisance reading explicitly specifies the joint training-measurable event and simultaneous support interpretation.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-45`: The training indices are chosen uniformly from the subsets of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ of size $n/2$, independently of the sampled observations; the remaining indices form $\mathcal D_2$. — pins reading of def:nete-algorithm: Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} specifies a random equal split, and the following estimation paragraph uses the first half for fitting and the second half for evaluation, invoking the standard independent sample-splitting convention.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-7`: $Y(D)=Y$ almost surely throughout the paper. — pins reading of ass:consistency: The Causal Inference paragraph states consistency for random variables in the potential-outcome framework; equality of random variables in this framework is understood almost surely.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-0b814294`: The equal split in Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} is defined for positive even sample sizes $n$, with $|\mathcal D_1|=|\mathcal D_2|=n/2$. — pins reading of lem:eta-rate: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-10c2ec7b`: The component dimension in the density of Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} is $m=d_z$. — pins reading of lem:eta-rate: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-6857bb4d`: For each fixed $t$ and tolerance $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, the three nuisance inequalities, with $\delta=\varepsilon$, hold on one $\mathcal T$-measurable event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, simultaneously almost surely over the covariate support, the conditional direction support and both treatments. — pins reading of thm:rate: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-8964f8dd`: Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} specifies the noise law $\mathcal L(U)$. — pins reading of lem:eta-rate: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-8a129280`: The fitted-exponent pseudo-outcome has finite conditional absolute mean under the evaluation tail law: $\mathbb E[|Y|/\|U\|^{\hat\alpha_n}\mid\mathcal D_1,\hbox{ auxiliary data},\|U\|>t]<\infty$ almost surely on each joint nuisance event. — pins reading of lem:eta-rate: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-8cd5c960`: The homogeneity inequality holds for every $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every radius $t>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R$ and $u\in\mathbb R_+^d$ with $\|u\|_1=1$. — pins reading of prop:id: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-8ef6fd29`: The homogeneity inequality holds for both treatments and every positive radius, on the positive unit sphere for the $\ell_1$ norm. — pins reading of lem:eta-rate: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-9567640d`: The equal split uses $n\in2\mathbb N$. — pins reading of def:nete-algorithm: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-b3a62d30`: The symbol $V_t$ denotes $\tmop{Var}(\psi\mid\mathcal D_1,\hbox{ auxiliary data},\|U\|>t)$. — pins reading of lem:eta-rate: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-c3605ca3`: The sample-size domain of $R_g(q,\varepsilon)$ includes every possible training exceedance count $q\in\{0,1,\ldots,n/2\}$, including $q=0$. — pins reading of lem:eta-rate: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-c4d18fdb`: For each fixed $\delta\in(0,1)$ there is a deterministic $n_0$ such that, for every integer $n\geq n_0$, the bound has probability at least $1-\delta$ under the law of the sample of size $n$. — pins reading of lem:mu-rate: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-c90a58f3`: The count $n_t=\sum_{i=1}^{n/2}I(\|U_i\|>t)$ is the training-split exceedance count, with the training observations indexed first after the random split. — pins reading of thm:rate: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-ebdb71b8`: The sampling term comprises both $\sqrt{4v_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)/(\delta M)}$ and $(1+4/\delta)b_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)$. — pins reading of lem:eta-ipw: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-f18f3355`: The target $\eta$ denotes $\int\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,s)-g(X,0,s)]\,S(\tmop{ds})$, where $S$ is the limiting spectral probability measure. — pins reading of lem:eta-rate: stated by the repair
- proof change: the printed proof, revised step by step] Proposition~\ref{prop:id} applies because its identification and integrability conditions are assumed. The limiting angular contrast is $\eta$, and the limiting radial moment is \[ \mu=\int_1^\infty r^\alpha\beta r^{-\beta-1}\,\tmop{dr}=\frac{\beta}{\beta-\alpha}=\frac1h. \] The integral converges because $\alpha<\beta$. Consequently $\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}=\eta\mu$. Since $|g|\leq C$, we also have $|\eta|\leq2C$. For either estimator, the product identity gives \[ \widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{estimator}}-\theta^{\tmop{NETE}} =\hat\mu_n(\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{estimator}}-\eta)+\eta(\hat\mu_n-\mu). \]  Let $E_\alpha$ be the joint nuisance event at tolerance $\delta/12$. The nuisance hypothesis gives $\mathbb P(E_\alpha^c)\leq\delta/12$, and on this event $|\hat\alpha_n-\alpha|\leq R_\alpha(n,\delta/12)=a$. Apply Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} at confidence tolerance $\delta/3$, using truncation level $B_{\mathrm{DR}}$. Its internal nuisance tolerance is $(\delta/3)/4=\delta/12$, its exponent bound is $a$, and its count requirement is $n\pi_t\geq16\log(12/\delta)$. It supplies an event $E_{\mathrm{DR}}$ with failure probability at most $\delta/3$ on which $M\geq n\pi_t/4$ and $|\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{DR}}-\eta|\leq\mathcal E_{\mathrm{DR}}$. Apply Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw} at tolerance $\delta/3$ with $B_{\mathrm{IPW}}$; its sampling coefficients are $12/\delta$ and its nuisance and exponent arguments are also $\delta/12$. It supplies an event $E_{\mathrm{IPW}}$ with failure probability at most $\delta/3$ on which $M\geq n\pi_t/4$ and $|\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}-\eta|\leq\mathcal E_{\mathrm{IPW}}$. The use of $R_p(n/2,\delta/12)$ is permitted by its sample-size monotonicity, since $R_p(n,\delta/12)\leq R_p(n/2,\delta/12)$; no comparison between confidence tolerances is used. Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}, applied to the full-input Hill selection run at tolerance $\delta/6$, supplies an event $E_\gamma$ with failure probability at most $\delta/6$ on which $|\hat\gamma_n-\gamma|\leq b_n$. Thus \[ E=E_\alpha\cap E_{\mathrm{DR}}\cap E_{\mathrm{IPW}}\cap E_\gamma \] satisfies \[ \mathbb P(E^c)\leq\frac{\delta}{12}+\frac{\delta}{3}+\frac{\delta}{3}+\frac{\delta}{6}=\frac{11\delta}{12}\leq\delta. \] This intersection explicitly contains the exponent inequality as well as both first-factor inequalities and the radial inequality. Independence between these events is unnecessary. On $E$, we have $|\hat\gamma_n|\leq\gamma+b_n$ and hence \[ |\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n-\alpha\gamma| =|\hat\gamma_n(\hat\alpha_n-\alpha)+\alpha(\hat\gamma_n-\gamma)| \leq a(\gamma+b_n)+|\alpha|b_n=Q_n. \] If $d_n>0$, exact reciprocal subtraction gives \[ |\hat\mu_n-\mu| =\frac{|\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n-\alpha\gamma|}{h d_n} \leq\frac{Q_n}{h d_n}=\mathcal H_n. \] If $d_n=0$, Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} sets $\hat\mu_n=0$, so \[ |\hat\mu_n-\mu|=1/h=\mathcal H_n. \] Thus $|\hat\mu_n-\mu|\leq\mathcal H_n$ throughout $E$, without any uniform Lipschitz assertion near the pole.  On $E$, the DR product identity and the triangle inequality give \[ \begin{aligned} |\widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{DR}}-\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}| &\leq|\hat\mu_n|\,|\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{DR}}-\eta|+|\eta|\,|\hat\mu_n-\mu|\\ &\leq|\hat\mu_n|\mathcal E_{\mathrm{DR}}+|\eta|\mathcal H_n. \end{aligned} \] Here Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} supplies the score-dependent sampling term, the product $R_p(n/2,\delta/12)R_g(n_t,\delta/12)$, the tail envelope $2\bar e(t)$, the angular Wasserstein term and the full exponent perturbation. Its exponent calculation uses $|\hat\alpha_n-\alpha|\leq a$ on $E_\alpha$ and gives $\mathbb E[\|U\|^a-1\mid\|U\|>t]\leq t^a-1+Kat^a$ for $K=4/(\beta-a_0)$. No linearization of this expression, replacement of $\bar e(t)$ by $e(t)$, or suppression of angular or radial logarithms is made. The count inequality also holds on $E$.  For IPW, the product identity gives \[ \widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}-\theta^{\tmop{NETE}} =\hat\mu_n(\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}-\eta)+\eta(\hat\mu_n-\mu). \] On $E$, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw} gives $|\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}-\eta|\leq\mathcal E_{\mathrm{IPW}}$, and the reciprocal calculation gives $|\hat\mu_n-\mu|\leq\mathcal H_n$. Therefore \[ \begin{aligned} |\widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}-\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}| &\leq|\hat\mu_n|\,|\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}-\eta|+|\eta|\,|\hat\mu_n-\mu|\\ &\leq|\hat\mu_n|\mathcal E_{\mathrm{IPW}}+|\eta|\mathcal H_n. \end{aligned} \] Both bounds and $M\geq n\pi_t/4$ thus hold together with probability at least $1-\delta$.  For the additional assertion, we choose the success events in the preceding proof using the constructions in Lemmas~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} and~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}. Let $E_M=\{M\geq n\pi_t/4\}$. Its failure probability is at most $\exp(-n\pi_t/16)\leq\delta/12$. For each $q\in\{\mathrm{DR},\mathrm{IPW}\}$ put $Z^q=\psi^q I\{|\psi^q|\leq B_q\}$ and $W^q=\psi^q I\{|\psi^q|>B_q\}$. Conditional on $\mathcal T$ and positive $M$, Chebyshev's and Markov's inequalities give two events $E_{Z,q}$ and $E_{W,q}$, each with conditional failure probability at most $\delta/12$, on which \[ \left|M^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I}Z_i^q-\mathbb E[Z^q\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t]\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{12v_t^q(B_q)}{\delta M}},\qquad M^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I}|W_i^q|\leq\frac{12b_t^q(B_q)}\delta. \] If $b_t^q(B_q)=0$, the tail variables vanish almost surely. Take $E_{\mathrm{DR}}=E_\alpha\cap E_M\cap E_{Z,\mathrm{DR}}\cap E_{W,\mathrm{DR}}$ and $E_{\mathrm{IPW}}=E_\alpha\cap E_M\cap E_{Z,\mathrm{IPW}}\cap E_{W,\mathrm{IPW}}$. These events give exactly the first-factor conclusions used in the preceding proof, by the two cited lemmas, and each has failure probability at most $\delta/3$. Since their nuisance and count events are shared, their intersection with $E_\gamma$ actually has failure probability at most \[ \delta/12+\delta/12+4\delta/12+\delta/6=8\delta/12. \]  On $E_\alpha$, the additional second-moment assumption gives $\tmop{Var}(\psi^q\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t)\leq V$. Conditional Chebyshev supplies an event $F_q$ whose failure on $E_\alpha\cap E_M$ has probability at most $\delta/12$ and on which \[ \left|\hat\eta_{n,t}^q-\mathbb E[\psi^q\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t]\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{12V}{\delta M}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{48V}{\delta n\pi_t}}. \] The assertion follows directly if $V=0$, because the centered scores then vanish almost surely. Integrating the conditional failure bounds and using a union bound shows that $E_*=E\cap F_{\mathrm{DR}}\cap F_{\mathrm{IPW}}$ has failure probability at most $10\delta/12\leq\delta$. Thus both general bounds and both finite-variance bounds hold on this one event; it contains the exponent inequality explicitly.  The finite-variance alternatives in Lemmas~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} and~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw} give the same conditional-mean bounds on $E_\alpha$, with this sampling term. Write $z=a\log t\in[0,1]$. Convexity of the exponential gives $\exp(z)-1\leq(\exp(1)-1)z$; also $t^a\leq\exp(1)$ and $a\leq a\log t$. Therefore \[ t^a-1+Kat^a\leq(\exp(1)-1+K\exp(1))a\log t. \] For sufficiently large $t$, $\bar e(t)\leq1$, so $G_t$ is bounded by the fixed constant $C+1$. On $E_*$, $d_n\geq h-Q_n\geq h/2>0$, and consequently \[ |\hat\mu_n|\leq2/h,\qquad \mathcal H_n\leq2Q_n/h^2, \qquad Q_n\leq\gamma a+(a_0+|\alpha|)b_n\leq C_1(a\log t+b_n). \] Here $C_1$ depends only on the fixed model and $a_0$. Substituting the DR mean bound and the finite-variance sampling bound in the DR product identity gives the additional DR display. Substituting the IPW mean bound and its finite-variance sampling bound in the IPW product identity gives the additional IPW display. The factors $2/h$, $|\eta|$, $2/c$, the bounded $G_t$ and the displayed exponent constant can all be absorbed into a constant independent of $n,t,\delta$. Finally the radial tail in the stated model is $\pi_t\asymp t^{-\beta}$, which gives the asserted order of the sampling term. The definition of $b_n$ and every nuisance tolerance remain exactly those displayed in the statement.
- why: The nuisance inequalities could concern only random evaluation arguments or hold simultaneously over their support; the proof's applications of the first-factor lemmas determine the latter reading. Under that stronger reading, the following defects remain. (a) The opening identification equality is false: exogeneity conditional on covariates and independence of the noise from treatment do not imply exogeneity conditional on the noise. (b) The asserted sampling rate has no hypothesis controlling normalized outcome fluctuations. (c) The radial-moment inversion is not uniformly Lipschitz near its pole, and monotone estimation-error bounds need not approach zero. (d) The exponent contribution cannot generally be replaced by $\log(t)R_\alpha(n,\delta)$: its reproduced form contains $t^a-1+Kat^a$. (e) The homogeneity contribution requires $\bar e(t)$, rather than the point value $e(t)$. (f) The angular rate omits a necessary logarithm when $\beta=1$. (g) A nuisance event with failure probability $\delta$ leaves no probability budget for evaluation error; sample-size monotonicity does not compare error bounds at different confidence tolerances. (h) The proof also absorbs the logarithmic factors and the different regimes of Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate} into a rate that this lemma does not supply. These are statement failures in (a)--(g), with the false proof steps identified below; (h) is an additional invalid conversion of the available dependency bound.

### `thm:cor-rate` (`Arxiv_template.tex:363-386`)

- as printed: \label{cor:rate} Under the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, further suppose that \begin{align*} R_p (n, \delta) = \Theta (\log (1 / \delta) n^{- 1 / 2}) , R_g (n, \delta) = \Theta (\log (1 / \delta) n^{- 1 / 2}), R_\alpha (n, \delta) = \Theta( \log(1/\delta) n^{-c_\alpha}), \end{align*} for some $c_\alpha > 0 $, the following conclusions hold. \begin{enumerate} \item If $s \in (0, 1 / (2 + \max \{1, \beta\}))$, takes $t_n = \Theta (n^{ (1 - 2 s) \hat{\gamma}_{n}})$, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, we have \begin{align*} | {{\widehat{\theta }_{n, t}^{\text{DR} }} } - \theta^{NETE} | & = O (e (t_n) + n^{- s} \log (1 / \delta) + n^{-c_\alpha} \log(n)\log (1 / \delta)) . \end{align*} \item If $s \in [1 / (2 + \max \{1, \beta\}), 1 / 2)$, \ takes $t = \Theta (n^{(\hat{\gamma}_{n} / (1 + 2 \min \{1, \hat{\gamma}_{n} \})})$, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, we have \begin{align*} | {{\widehat{\theta }_{n, t}^{\text{DR} }} } - \theta^{NETE} | & = O (e (t_n) + n^{- 1 / (2 + \max \{\beta, 1\})} \log (1 / \delta) + n^{-c_\alpha} \log(n)\log (1 / \delta)) . \end{align*} \end{enumerate}
- corrected: Under the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, further suppose that \begin{align*} R_p(n,\delta)&=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-1/2}),\qquad R_g(n,\delta)=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-1/2}),\\ R_\alpha(n,\delta)&=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-c_\alpha}) \end{align*} for some $c_\alpha>0$. Suppose in addition that the conditions of Proposition~\ref{prop:id} hold. Let $\mathcal T$ consist of the training split and independent auxiliary data. Suppose that $\hat p$, $\hat g$ and $\hat\alpha_n$ are $\mathcal T$-measurable and are the same functions and exponent for every input threshold. Write $R_U=\|U\|$, $S_U=U/\|U\|$, $\gamma=1/\beta$, $\pi_t=\mathbb P(R_U>t)$, and \[ m_d^t(x,v)=\mathbb E[Y/R_U^\alpha\mid X=x,D=d,S_U=v,R_U>t]. \] In each case below, let $\kappa$ be the exponent function specified in that case. Use fixed constants $0<c_-\leq c_+<\infty$ in the threshold order, so that $c_-n^{\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)}\leq t_n\leq c_+n^{\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)}$, and define the deterministic band \[ \ell_n=(c_-/2)n^{\kappa(\gamma)},\qquad u_n=2c_+n^{\kappa(\gamma)},\qquad I_n=[\ell_n,u_n]. \] Suppose that with probability at least $1-\delta/6$, jointly for every $t\in I_n$, \[ \begin{aligned} |\hat\alpha_n-\alpha|&\leq R_\alpha(n,\delta/6),\\ |p(x)-\hat p(x)|&\leq R_p(n/2,\delta/6),\\ |m_d^t(x,v)-\hat g(x,d,v)|&\leq R_g(n_t,\delta/6), \end{aligned} \] simultaneously almost surely over the covariate and conditional direction support and both treatments, where $n_t=\sum_{i=1}^{n/2}I(\|U_i\|>t)$ is the training exceedance count. On this same event suppose that the fitted-exponent pseudo-outcome has finite conditional absolute mean for every $t\in I_n$ and that, for a constant $V<\infty$ independent of $n$ and $t$, \[ \mathbb E[\psi^2\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]\leq V\qquad(t\in I_n), \] where \[ \psi=\hat g(X,1,S_U)-\hat g(X,0,S_U)+\frac{D-\hat p(X)}{\hat p(X)(1-\hat p(X))}\left(\frac{Y}{R_U^{\hat\alpha_n}}-\hat g(X,D,S_U)\right). \] Set the first-factor estimate to zero if there are no evaluation exceedances. Run the full-input Hill selection of Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} at tolerance $\delta/6$, and put \[ b_n=C_{\delta/6}\begin{cases} \left(\dfrac{\log((48/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&0<\beta<1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log n\,\log((48/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&\beta=1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log((48/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/(2+\beta)},&\beta>1, \end{cases} \] with the constant supplied by Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}. Define \[ \bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r). \] Then, for each fixed $\delta\in(0,1/2)$ and every sufficiently large even $n$, the following conclusions hold. Here $k\geq0$ is the exponent of the logarithm in the angular assumption of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, taken from Proposition 3.1 of \citet{zhang2023wasserstein}; under that assumption the angular term at the threshold $t_n$ is of the order shown, up to this power of $\log n$. \begin{enumerate} \item If $s\in(0,1/(2+\max\{1,\beta\}))$, take $t_n=\Theta(n^{(1-2s)\hat\gamma_n})$ and put $\kappa(v)=(1-2s)v$. With probability at least $1-\delta$, we have \[ \begin{aligned} |\widehat\theta_{n,t_n}^{\text{DR}}-\theta^{NETE}|=O\bigl(&\sqrt{V/\delta}\,n^{-s}+\log^2(6/\delta)n^{-1/2-s}\\ &+\bar e(t_n)+(\log n)^k n^{-s}+n^{-c_\alpha}\log n\log(6/\delta)+|\alpha|b_n\bigr). \end{aligned} \] \item If $s\in[1/(2+\max\{1,\beta\}),1/2)$, take $t_n=\Theta(n^{\hat\gamma_n/(1+2\min\{1,\hat\gamma_n\})})$ and put $\kappa(v)=v/(1+2\min\{1,v\})$. Write $q=1/(2+\max\{\beta,1\})$. With probability at least $1-\delta$, we have \[ \begin{aligned} |\widehat\theta_{n,t_n}^{\text{DR}}-\theta^{NETE}|=O\bigl(&\sqrt{V/\delta}\,n^{-q}+\log^2(6/\delta)n^{-1/2-q}\\ &+\bar e(t_n)+(\log n)^k n^{-q}+n^{-c_\alpha}\log n\log(6/\delta)+|\alpha|b_n\bigr). \end{aligned} \] \end{enumerate}
