- proof change: the printed proof, revised step by step] Fix $\delta\in(0,1/2)$ and an even $n$. Let $E_T$ be the joint uniform nuisance and moment event in the statement. Let $E_\gamma$ be the event supplied by Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate} at tolerance $\delta/6$. Each event has failure probability at most $\delta/6$. On $E_\gamma$, $|\hat\gamma_n-\gamma|\leq b_n$. All three displayed definitions of $b_n$ satisfy $b_n\log n\to0$ for fixed $\delta$. The function $v\mapsto(1-2s)v$ is Lipschitz with constant at most $1$. The function $v\mapsto v/(1+2\min\{1,v\})$ is continuous at $v=1$ and has derivative $1/(1+2v)^2$ for $v<1$ and $1/3$ for $v>1$, so it too is Lipschitz with constant at most $1$. Consequently \[ \frac{n^{\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)}}{n^{\kappa(\gamma)}}=\exp\{(\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)-\kappa(\gamma))\log n\}\in[1/2,2] \] on $E_\gamma$ for sufficiently large $n$, and hence $t_n\in I_n$. In particular, the correct radial identity is \[ n^{\beta\hat\gamma_n}=n\exp\{\beta(\hat\gamma_n-\gamma)\log n\},\qquad n^{\beta(\hat\gamma_n-\gamma)}=1+O(b_n\log n) \] on this event.  Put $m=n/2$ and $r=(1-\beta\kappa(\gamma))/2$. In the first case $\beta\kappa(\gamma)=1-2s$, so $r=s$. In the second case, with $a_* =\min\{1,\beta\}$, we have $\kappa(\gamma)=1/(\beta+2a_*)$ and $r=a_*/(\beta+2a_*)=q$. Thus $r>0$ in both cases. The Pareto-type model gives $\pi_t\asymp t^{-\beta}$ for large $t$, and therefore \[ \pi_{\ell_n}\asymp\pi_{u_n}\asymp n^{-1+2r},\qquad m\pi_{u_n}\asymp n^{2r}. \] The training and evaluation exceedance counts at the deterministic threshold $u_n$ are each binomial with parameters $m,\pi_{u_n}$. For either count $N_{u_n}$, the binomial lower-tail inequality gives \[ \mathbb P(N_{u_n}<m\pi_{u_n}/2)\leq\exp(-m\pi_{u_n}/8)\leq\delta/6 \] for sufficiently large $n$. On the complements of these two failures, both counts at every $t\in I_n$ are at least $m\pi_{u_n}/2$.  We next prove uniform sampling control, without conditioning on the selected threshold. Conditional on $\mathcal T$, the evaluation observations $Z_i=(X_i,D_i,Y_i,U_i)$ are independent with the original observation law, and the function $\psi$ is fixed. Write $P_T$ for this conditional law and define \[ A(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\{\psi(Z_i)I(R_i>t)-P_T[\psi I(R_U>t)]\},\qquad C(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\{I(R_i>t)-\pi_t\}. \] For completeness, an independent conditional copy $Z'_1,\ldots,Z'_m$ and Jensen's inequality yield \[ \mathbb E[\sup_{t\in I_n}|A(t)|^2\mid\mathcal T]\leq\mathbb E\left[\sup_{t\in I_n}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\{\psi(Z_i)I(R_i>t)-\psi(Z'_i)I(R'_i>t)\}\right|^2\middle|\mathcal T\right]. \] Introduce independent symmetric signs $\varepsilon_i$. Swapping each independent pair leaves its law invariant, so the right-hand side equals the same expectation with each difference multiplied by $\varepsilon_i$. The inequality $|a-b|^2\leq2|a|^2+2|b|^2$ then bounds it by \[ 4\mathbb E\left[\sup_{t\in I_n}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varepsilon_i\psi(Z_i)I(R_i>t)\right|^2\middle|\mathcal T\right]. \] Conditional on the observations, order those with $R_i>\ell_n$ by decreasing radius. The signed sums indexed by $t\in I_n$ are among the partial sums of independent mean-zero variables in this order. The square-integrable martingale maximal inequality bounds their expected squared maximum by $4\sum_i\psi(Z_i)^2I(R_i>\ell_n)$. Thus, on $E_T$, \[ \mathbb E[\sup_{t\in I_n}|A(t)|^2\mid\mathcal T]\leq16mV\pi_{\ell_n}. \] The same argument with $\psi=1$ gives $\mathbb E[\sup_{t\in I_n}|C(t)|^2\mid\mathcal T]\leq16m\pi_{\ell_n}$. Markov's inequality therefore supplies two conditional events, each with failure probability at most $\delta/6$, on which \[ \sup_{t\in I_n}|A(t)|\leq\sqrt{96mV\pi_{\ell_n}/\delta},\qquad \sup_{t\in I_n}|C(t)|\leq\sqrt{96m\pi_{\ell_n}/\delta}. \] These conditional failure bounds also hold unconditionally on $E_T$ by integration. Suprema of these threshold processes are measurable because they can be taken over a countable dense set together with the band endpoints.  Write $\mu_t=P_T[\psi\mid R_U>t]$ and $M_t=\sum_i I(R_i>t)$. On $E_T$, Cauchy--Schwarz gives $|\mu_t|\leq\sqrt V$. Since \[ \sum_i\psi(Z_i)I(R_i>t)-M_t\mu_t=A(t)-\mu_t C(t), \] the two maximal bounds and the evaluation-count bound imply, simultaneously for every $t\in I_n$, \[ |\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\tmop{DR}}-\mu_t|\leq4\sqrt{\frac{96V\pi_{\ell_n}}{\delta m\pi_{u_n}^2}}=O(\sqrt{V/\delta}\,n^{-r}). \] This inequality holds at $t_n$ on $E_\gamma$.  It remains to bound the conditional score mean. Put $a=R_\alpha(n,\delta/6)$, choose $a_0\in(0,\beta)$, let $C_g$ bound $|g|$ on its compact domain, and set $G_t=C_g+\bar e(t)$. For sufficiently large $n$, $a\leq a_0$ and $G_t$ is bounded uniformly on $I_n$. Let $\psi_\alpha$ denote the score with the exponent replaced by $\alpha$, and let \[ \eta_\alpha^t=\mathbb E[(f(X,1,U)-f(X,0,U))/R_U^\alpha\mid R_U>t]. \] Independence of $U$ from $(X,D)$ and conditional expectation over treatment give \[ P_T[\psi_\alpha\mid R_U>t]-\eta_\alpha^t=\mathbb E\left[(\hat p-p)\left\{\frac{\hat g(X,1,S_U)-m_1^t(X,S_U)}{\hat p}+\frac{\hat g(X,0,S_U)-m_0^t(X,S_U)}{1-\hat p}\right\}\middle|\mathcal T,R_U>t\right]. \] Estimated-propensity overlap and the uniform nuisance event bound its absolute value by $(2/c)R_p(n/2,\delta/6)R_g(n_t,\delta/6)$. The training-count bound and the assumed rates make this $O(\log^2(6/\delta)n^{-1/2-r})$ uniformly on $I_n$.  The exponent calculation in Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} is a conditional-mean calculation: taking the conditional outcome mean first and using homogeneity bounds the exponent difference by \[ \frac{2G_t}{c}\mathbb E[R_U^a-1\mid R_U>t]\leq\frac{2G_t}{c}(t^a-1+Kat^a),\qquad K=4/(\beta-a_0). \] This population inequality holds for every sufficiently large $t$, so it is available throughout $I_n$. Since $a=O(\log(6/\delta)n^{-c_\alpha})$ and $\log t=O(\log n)$ on this band, $a\log t\to0$. The inequalities $\exp(x)-1\leq2x$ for sufficiently small $x\geq0$ and $t^a\leq2$ show that this term is $O(\log(6/\delta)n^{-c_\alpha}\log n)$.  Homogeneity at every radius above $t$ gives an error at most $2\bar e(t)$. The function $v\mapsto\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,v)-g(X,0,v)]$ is $2L$-Lipschitz. Independence of $X$ and $U$ and the Wasserstein coupling inequality therefore give \[ |\eta_\alpha^t-\eta|\leq2\bar e(t)+\mathcal A_t, \] where $\eta$ is the limiting angular contrast. Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} supplies the bound for $\mathcal A_t$; we retain that term without suppressing any logarithm.  Finally Proposition~\ref{prop:id} gives $\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}=\eta/h$, where $h=1-\alpha\gamma>0$. On $E_T\cap E_\gamma$, \[ |\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n-\alpha\gamma|\leq a(\gamma+b_n)+|\alpha|b_n=:Q_n\to0. \] For sufficiently large $n$, $Q_n\leq h/2$, whence $|\hat\mu_n|\leq2/h$ and exact reciprocal subtraction gives $|\hat\mu_n-1/h|\leq2Q_n/h^2$. The product identity \[ \widehat\theta_{n,t_n}^{\text{DR}}-\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}=\hat\mu_n(\hat\eta_{n,t_n}^{\tmop{DR}}-\eta)+\eta(\hat\mu_n-1/h) \] now proves the stated bound with $r=s$ in the first case and $r=q$ in the second. The term $a$ in $Q_n$ is absorbed by $a\log n$, while $|\alpha|b_n$ is retained. There are six failure events: the uniform nuisance event, the Hill event, the two count events, and the two maximal-process events. Each contributes at most $\delta/6$, so their union has probability at most $\delta$. No sequence-wide probability assertion is used.

### `lem:mu-rate` (`Arxiv_template.tex:607-616`)

- as printed: \label{lemma:mu_rate}Under Assumption \ref{assump:iid}, \ref{assump:mvary} with probability at least $1 - \delta$, for sufficiently large $n$, we have \begin{align*} | \gamma - \hat{\gamma}_n | & \leqslant O \left( \left( \frac{\log (2 / \delta)}{n} \right)^{1 / (2 + \beta)} \right), \end{align*} where $\gamma = 1 / \beta$ is the EVI of $U$.
- corrected: Under Assumption \ref{assump:iid}, \ref{assump:mvary}, using the standing convention $\|\cdot\|=\|\cdot\|_1$, let $R_i=\|U_i\|_1$ for all $i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$ and order these full-input radii as $R_{[1]}\geq\cdots\geq R_{[n]}$. For $n\geq2$, define \[ H_n(k)=\frac1k\sum_{j=1}^k\log\frac{R_{[j]}}{R_{[k+1]}},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n-1. \] For $\delta\in(0,1)$, set \[ \begin{aligned} L_n&=\min\{n-1,\lceil100\log n\rceil\},&J_n&=1+\lceil\log n/\log2\rceil,\\ q_n(\delta)&=\log(4J_n/\delta),&c_n(\delta)&=2\sqrt{q_n(\delta)}+\frac{q_n(\delta)}{\sqrt{L_n}},\\ \rho_n(\delta)&=16c_n(\delta). \end{aligned} \] Let \[ \begin{aligned} \widetilde k_n&=\max\bigl\{k\in\{L_n,\ldots,n-1\}:\\ &\qquad |H_n(i)-H_n(k)|\leq\frac{H_n(i)\rho_n(\delta)}{\sqrt i}\\ &\qquad \text{for every }i\in\{L_n,\ldots,k\}\bigr\},\\ \widetilde\gamma_n&=H_n(\widetilde k_n). \end{aligned} \] For each fixed $\delta\in(0,1)$ and every sufficiently large $n$, with probability at least $1-\delta$, we have \[ |\gamma-\widetilde\gamma_n|\leq C_\delta \begin{cases} \left(\dfrac{\log((8/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&0<\beta<1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log n\,\log((8/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&\beta=1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log((8/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/(2+\beta)},&\beta>1, \end{cases} \] where $C_\delta$ may depend on the fixed distribution and on $\delta$. where $\gamma = 1 / \beta$ is the EVI of $U$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-33`: $U\perp(X,D)$. — pins reading of ass:iid-independence: The opening model description calls $U$ an independent extreme-noise vector, and Assumption~\ref{assump:iid} states that it is independent of $X,D$. Standard multivariate independence treats the listed variables jointly.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-36`: For each component law in $\tilde M_k$, there exists one constant $C_k>0$, independent of $z$, such that $h(z)=C_k\prod_{i=1}^{m}(1+z_i)^{-(\beta+1)}$ whenever $\|z\|_1>\zeta k^{(1-2s)/\beta}$. — pins reading of ass:mvary-density: Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} uses $\varpropto$ on the stated tail region and separately bounds the relative error from the normalized nominal density. Proportionality fixes a constant multiplier, without requiring that multiplier to equal $\beta^m$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-38`: For each norm, there exist $S$, $b_n\rightarrow\infty$, and $c>0$ such that, for every $a>0$, the polar measures $n\tmop{P}((\|U\|/b_n,U/\|U\|)\in\cdot)$ restricted to $[a,\infty]\times\mathbb{S}^+$ converge weakly as finite measures to the corresponding restriction of $c\nu_\beta\times S$. — pins reading of def:regular-variation: Definition~\ref{def:mvarying} specifies $\nu_\beta([r,\infty))=r^{-\beta}$, which is finite away from zero and infinite over the entire radial space. The immediately following discussion identifies the intended Pareto tail limit, fixing the standard tail-measure interpretation.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-c4d18fdb`: For each fixed $\delta\in(0,1)$ there is a deterministic $n_0$ such that, for every integer $n\geq n_0$, the bound has probability at least $1-\delta$ under the law of the sample of size $n$. — pins reading of lem:mu-rate: stated by the repair
- proof change: the printed proof, revised step by step] We adopt the non-asymptotic analysis of the adaptive Hill estimator for EVI   in {\citep{boucheron2015tail}}. We give the exponential-spacing argument and the confidence calibration explicitly. Write $R=\|U\|_1$. Fix a member of $M_K$, put $a_i=\|A_{\cdot i}\|_1$, and let $Z_i^0$ be independent nominal Pareto type-II components of index $\beta$. The component density is a positive constant $c_0$ times the nominal product density whenever $\|z\|_1>\zeta K^{(1-2s)/\beta}$. Since \[ R=\sum_{i=1}^m a_iZ_i,\qquad l\leq a_i\leq u,\qquad R\leq u\|Z\|_1, \] the survival and density of $R$ beyond $u\zeta K^{(1-2s)/\beta}$ are $c_0$ times those of $T=\sum_i a_iZ_i^0$. This transfer uses the original nominal components without shifting their supports.  Put $W_i=a_iZ_i^0$, $V_i=\sum_{j\ne i}W_j$, and $f_i(x)=\beta a_i^\beta(a_i+x)^{-\beta-1}$ for $x\geq0$, extended by zero for $x<0$. Partitioning the convolution integral according to the largest summand, whose ties have probability zero, gives \[ f_T(r)=\sum_i\mathbb E\left[f_i(r-V_i)I\{\max_{j\ne i}W_j\leq r-V_i\}\right]. \] On this event $r-V_i\geq r/m$. If $V_i\leq r/2$, the event holds and the mean value theorem gives $|f_i(r-V_i)-f_i(r)|\leq C r^{-\beta-2}V_i$. On the part where $V_i>r/2$, the indicated density is at most $C r^{-\beta-1}$. A union bound using the tails of the $m-1$ summands gives $\mathbb P(V_i>x)\leq Cx^{-\beta}$ for $x\geq1$. Integrating this bound and using $\mathbb E[V_iI\{V_i\leq r/2\}]\leq\mathbb E[\min\{V_i,r/2\}]$ gives \[ \mathbb E[V_iI\{V_i\leq r/2\}]\leq \begin{cases}Cr^{1-\beta},&\beta<1,\\ C\log r,&\beta=1,\\ C,&\beta>1.\end{cases} \] Consequently \[ \left|f_T(r)-\sum_i f_i(r)\right| \leq C\sum_i\left(r^{-\beta-2}\mathbb E[V_iI\{V_i\leq r/2\}]+r^{-\beta-1}\mathbb P(V_i>r/2)\right). \] Since $\sum_i f_i(r)=\beta\sum_i a_i^\beta r^{-\beta-1}(1+O(r^{-1}))$, set \[ b_\beta(r)=\begin{cases}r^{-\beta},&\beta<1,\\r^{-1}\log r,&\beta=1,\\r^{-1},&\beta>1,\end{cases}\qquad C_0=c_0\sum_i a_i^\beta>0. \] The actual radial density and survival therefore satisfy, for sufficiently large $r$, \[ f_R(r)=\beta C_0r^{-\beta-1}(1+O(b_\beta(r))),\qquad \bar F_R(r)=C_0r^{-\beta}(1+O(b_\beta(r))). \] The second estimate follows by integrating the first error from $r$ to infinity: the integrals of $r^{-2\beta-1}$, $r^{-3}\log r$, and $r^{-\beta-2}$ have orders $r^{-2\beta}$, $r^{-2}\log r$, and $r^{-\beta-1}$, respectively. Thus no constant-coefficient expansion at $\beta=1$ is required. When $\beta>1$ and a survival expansion with coefficient $D$ is used, differentiation gives density coefficient $D(1+1/\beta)$; the density and survival envelopes just proved avoid differentiating an unspecified remainder.  Let $Q(v)=F_R^{-1}(1-1/v)$ be the radial tail quantile. Its differentiable tail has $Q(v)\sim(C_0v)^{1/\beta}$ and \[ \eta(v)=\frac{vQ'(v)}{Q(v)}-\gamma =\frac{\bar F_R(Q(v))}{Q(v)f_R(Q(v))}-\frac1\beta =O(b_\beta(Q(v))). \] Choose a fixed $v_0>1$ in that tail and define $\bar\eta(v)=\sup_{w\geq\max\{v,v_0\}}|\eta(w)|$ for every $v>0$. For sufficiently large $v$ this gives \[ \bar\eta(v)\leq \begin{cases}Cv^{-1},&\beta<1,\\Cv^{-1}\log v,&\beta=1,\\Cv^{-1/\beta},&\beta>1.\end{cases} \] For $y\geq z\geq\log v_0$, integrating the quantile derivative gives \[ |\log Q(e^y)-\log Q(e^z)-\gamma(y-z)|\leq\bar\eta(e^z)(y-z). \]  We next prove the simultaneous stochastic bound used for selection. Let $Y_{(1)}\geq\cdots\geq Y_{(n)}$ be decreasing order statistics of independent standard exponential variables, with $Y_{(n+1)}=0$. Their joint density is $n!\exp(-\sum_j y_j)$ on the ordered region. The change of variables $E_j=j(Y_{(j)}-Y_{(j+1)})$ has Jacobian $1/n!$ and $\sum_j y_j=\sum_j E_j$, so the $E_j$ are independent standard exponential variables. The quantile transform represents the ordered radii as $Q(e^{Y_{(j)}})$. Define $B_i=\sum_{j=1}^i(E_j-1)$ and $A_i=i^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^iE_j$ for this argument only.  For $0<\lambda<1$, \[ \log\mathbb E e^{\lambda(E_j-1)}=-\lambda-\log(1-\lambda)\leq\frac{\lambda^2}{2(1-\lambda)},\qquad \log\mathbb E e^{-\lambda(E_j-1)}=\lambda-\log(1+\lambda)\leq\frac{\lambda^2}{2(1-\lambda)}. \] Hence $\exp(\pm\lambda B_i-i\lambda^2/(2(1-\lambda)))$ are nonnegative supermartingales with initial value one. The maximal supermartingale inequality implies \[ \mathbb P\left(\max_{i\leq m}|B_i|\geq\sqrt{2mx}+x\right)\leq2e^{-x}. \] Indeed, each one-sided probability is at most $\exp(-\lambda a+m\lambda^2/(2(1-\lambda)))$ for $a=\sqrt{2mx}+x$; taking $\lambda=\sqrt{2x/m}/(1+\sqrt{2x/m})$ makes this bound $e^{-x}$.  Apply this inequality at the ends of the dyadic intervals $2^a\leq i<2^{a+1}$, using $m=\min\{n,2^{a+1}\}$ and $x=q_n(\delta)$. There are at most $J_n$ such intervals. On each interval $\sqrt{2m}/\sqrt i\leq2$. The union bound therefore supplies an event $E$ of probability at least $1-\delta/2$ on which \[ \sqrt i\,|A_i-1|\leq2\sqrt{q_n(\delta)}+q_n(\delta)/\sqrt i\leq c_n(\delta),\qquad L_n\leq i\leq n. \] For fixed $\delta$, $c_n(\delta)=O_\delta(\sqrt{\log\log n})$ and $c_n(\delta)/\sqrt{L_n}\to0$.  Write $c_n=c_n(\delta)$ and consider the deterministic index \[ k_* =\max\left\{k\in\{L_n,\ldots,n-1\}:\ \frac{n}{2k}\geq v_0,\quad \sqrt k\,\bar\eta\left(\frac{n}{2k}\right)\leq\gamma c_n\right\}. \] The choices below show that this set is nonempty for all sufficiently large $n$. Put $T_*=e^{Y_{(k_*+1)}}$. The number of exponential observations exceeding $\log(n/(2k_*))$ has binomial law with mean $2k_*$. Its multiplicative lower-tail bound gives \[ \mathbb P\left(T_*<\frac{n}{2k_*}\right)\leq\exp(-k_*/4)\leq\delta/2 \] for sufficiently large $n$, because $k_*\geq L_n$. Indeed, if $B\sim\tmop{Bin}(n,p)$ and $\mu=np$, Markov's inequality gives \[ \mathbb P(B\leq\mu/2)\leq e^{\mu\log2/2}\mathbb E[2^{-B}] =e^{\mu\log2/2}(1-p/2)^n \leq e^{-\mu(1-\log2)/2}\leq e^{-\mu/8}, \] using $\log2\leq3/4$; here $p=2k_*/n$ and $\mu=2k_*$. Let $E_*$ be the complementary event. On $E_*$ all spacings involved in $H_n(i)$ for $i\leq k_*$ lie above $\log v_0$. Since $H_n(i)=i^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^i j\log(R_{[j]}/R_{[j+1]})$ by telescoping, the quantile inequality thus gives \[ |H_n(i)-\gamma A_i|\leq\bar\eta(T_*)A_i\leq\frac{\gamma c_n}{\sqrt{k_*}}A_i,\qquad L_n\leq i\leq k_*. \] On $E\cap E_*$, for large enough $n$, $c_n/\sqrt{L_n}\leq1/6$. It follows that \[ |H_n(i)-\gamma|\leq\frac{\gamma c_n}{\sqrt i}+\frac{\gamma c_n}{\sqrt{k_*}}\left(1+\frac{c_n}{\sqrt i}\right) \leq\frac{3\gamma c_n}{\sqrt i},\qquad \gamma/2\leq H_n(i)\leq3\gamma/2. \] For every $L_n\leq i\leq k\leq k_*$, \[ |H_n(i)-H_n(k)|\leq\frac{6\gamma c_n}{\sqrt i} \leq\frac{H_n(i)\rho_n(\delta)}{\sqrt i}. \] Thus $k_*$ qualifies for the displayed prefix rule, and its selected index $\widetilde k_n$ is at least $k_*$. The set in that rule is nonempty almost surely because $k=L_n$ only compares with itself. The comparison at $i=k_*$ now yields \[ |\widetilde\gamma_n-\gamma|\leq|H_n(k_*)-\gamma|+\frac{H_n(k_*)\rho_n(\delta)}{\sqrt{k_*}} \leq\frac{27\gamma c_n}{\sqrt{k_*}}. \] The two events used have total failure probability at most $\delta$; independence between them is unnecessary.  To obtain the displayed rates, when $\beta\ne1$ put $p=\min\{1,1/\beta\}$ and choose a sufficiently small fixed $a_*>0$. Let \[ k_0=\left\lfloor a_* n^{2p/(1+2p)}c_n^{2/(1+2p)}\right\rfloor. \] For all sufficiently large $n$, $k_0\geq L_n$, $k_0<n/(2v_0)$, and \[ \sqrt{k_0}\,\bar\eta(n/(2k_0))\leq C2^p k_0^{p+1/2}n^{-p}\leq\gamma c_n \] by making $a_*$ small. Thus $k_*\geq k_0$, and \[ \frac{c_n}{\sqrt{k_*}}\leq C n^{-p/(1+2p)}c_n^{2p/(1+2p)} \leq C_\delta\left(\frac{\log((8/\delta)\log n)}n\right)^{p/(1+2p)}. \] Here $c_n^2\leq C_\delta\log((8/\delta)\log n)$ for sufficiently large $n$. For $\beta<1$ the exponent is $1/3$, and for $\beta>1$ it is $1/(2+\beta)$.  When $\beta=1$, take \[ k_0=\left\lfloor a_*n^{2/3}\left(\frac{c_n}{\log n}\right)^{2/3}\right\rfloor. \] Again $k_0\geq L_n$ and $k_0<n/(2v_0)$ eventually, and \[ \sqrt{k_0}\,\bar\eta(n/(2k_0))\leq Ck_0^{3/2}n^{-1}\log n\leq\gamma c_n \] for sufficiently small $a_*$. Hence $k_*\geq k_0$ and \[ \frac{c_n}{\sqrt{k_*}}\leq C n^{-1/3}(c_n^2\log n)^{1/3} \leq C_\delta\left(\frac{\log n\,\log((8/\delta)\log n)}n\right)^{1/3}. \] This proves the middle case on the same event of probability at least $1-\delta$, and completes the proof.
- why: The core faithfully retains the assumptions and algorithm reference. (a) The unresolved readings are the outcome exponent versus a local tail-index meaning of $\alpha$, and the algorithm's adjacent-ratio statistic versus the proof's common-denominator Hill statistic. The input collection, admissible ranks, and calibration of $l_n$ and $r_n(\delta)$ are not reconciled. (b) Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} compares candidates through $n$, although its evaluation collection contains at most $n/2$ observations; moreover, its displayed statistic telescopes and lacks the weights required for a Hill estimator. (c) The proof's comparison against every candidate index is not the cited adaptive selection rule and can leave the admissible set empty even after removing unavailable ranks. (d) The asserted second-order tail expansion, and hence $\eta(t)=O(t^{-1/\beta})$, fail at the admissible value $\beta=1$, where a logarithmic term is present. (e) Two additional proof slips can be closed locally: the proportional component tail should be transferred using the original product Pareto law, rather than the shifted component law; and differentiating the displayed survival expansion gives the density coefficient $D(1+1/\beta)$, not $D(1-1/\beta)$. These corrections do not resolve (b)--(d). (f) I also could not close the final removal of the adaptation factor: [Boucheron and Thomas, Corollary 3.12](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1503.05077) retains $\log((2/\delta)\log n)$, whereas the printed rate retains only $\log(2/\delta)$.

### `lem:eta-rate` (`Arxiv_template.tex:682-690`)

- as printed: \label{lemma:eta_rate}Undet the assumption of Theorem \ref{thm:rate}, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, we have \begin{align*} | \hat{\eta}_{n, t}^{\tmop{DR}} - \eta | & \leqslant O (\sqrt{R_p (n/2, \delta) R_g (t^{- \beta} n, \delta)} + t^{\beta / 2} n^{- 1 / 2} + t^{- \min \{1, \beta\}} + t^{- \beta s / (1 - 2 s)} + e (t) + \log (t) R_{\alpha} (n,\delta)) . \end{align*}
- corrected: Under the assumption of Theorem \ref{thm:rate}, suppose in addition that the conditions of Proposition~\ref{prop:id} hold. Suppose that the fitted propensity, regression, and exponent are measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data. Suppose that the nuisance inequalities of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} hold jointly with probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, uniformly almost surely over the covariate and direction support and both treatments, for each tolerance $\varepsilon$. Suppose that $0<p(X)<1$ almost surely and that the fitted-exponent pseudo-outcome has finite conditional absolute mean on those nuisance events. Put $\pi_t=\mathbb P(\|U\|>t)$ and $M=|\mathcal I|$, and set $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\tmop{DR}}=0$ when $M=0$. Let $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$, let $C$ bound $|g|$ on its compact domain, and put $G_t=C+\bar e(t)$. For a generic evaluation observation define \[ \psi=\hat g(X,1,U/\|U\|)-\hat g(X,0,U/\|U\|)+\frac{D-\hat p(X)}{\hat p(X)(1-\hat p(X))}\left(\frac{Y}{\|U\|^{\hat\alpha_n}}-\hat g(X,D,U/\|U\|)\right). \] For any positive truncation level $B$ chosen using only the training split and independent auxiliary data, define \[ \begin{aligned} v_t(B)&=\mathbb E[\psi^2 I\{|\psi|\leq B\}\mid\mathcal D_1,\hbox{ auxiliary data},\|U\|>t],\\ a_t(B)&=\mathbb E[|\psi|I\{|\psi|>B\}\mid\mathcal D_1,\hbox{ auxiliary data},\|U\|>t]. \end{aligned} \] Fix $a_0\in(0,\beta)$ and write $a=R_\alpha(n,\delta/4)$. For sufficiently large $t\geq1$, if $a\leq a_0$ and $n\pi_t\geq16\log(4/\delta)$, then with probability at least $1-\delta$ we have $M\geq n\pi_t/4$ and \begin{align*} |\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\tmop{DR}}-\eta|\leq{}&\sqrt{\frac{4v_t(B)}{\delta M}}+\left(1+\frac4\delta\right)a_t(B)+\frac2c R_p(n/2,\delta/4)R_g(n_t,\delta/4)\\ &+2\bar e(t)+2L W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|})+\frac{2G_t}{c}\left(t^a-1+K a t^a\right), \end{align*} where $K$ depends on the radial law and $a_0$. Suppose moreover that the angular Wasserstein term satisfies the bound that Proposition 3.1 of \citet{zhang2023wasserstein} gives for this Pareto-type model, which is taken from that work and not proved here: there are constants $C_W>0$, $k\geq0$ and $t_W\geq1$ such that \[ W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|})\leq C_W(\log t)^k t^{-\min\{1,\beta\}}+C_W t^{-\beta s/(1-2s)}\qquad(t\geq t_W). \] Then for $t\geq t_W$ the angular term $2L W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|})$ of the display is at most $2LC_W(\log t)^k t^{-\min\{1,\beta\}}+2LC_W t^{-\beta s/(1-2s)}$, with the logarithmic factor retained. When the conditional score variance $V_t$ is finite, the sampling term may instead be taken to be $\sqrt{4V_t/(\delta M)}$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-33`: $U\perp(X,D)$. — pins reading of ass:iid-independence: The opening model description calls $U$ an independent extreme-noise vector, and Assumption~\ref{assump:iid} states that it is independent of $X,D$. Standard multivariate independence treats the listed variables jointly.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-34`: $\|X\|_1\leqslant R$ almost surely. — pins reading of ass:f-homo-bounded-x: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} bounds the random covariate, and the Notations paragraph explicitly identifies both norm symbols with the $\ell_1$-norm.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-36`: For each component law in $\tilde M_k$, there exists one constant $C_k>0$, independent of $z$, such that $h(z)=C_k\prod_{i=1}^{m}(1+z_i)^{-(\beta+1)}$ whenever $\|z\|_1>\zeta k^{(1-2s)/\beta}$. — pins reading of ass:mvary-density: Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} uses $\varpropto$ on the stated tail region and separately bounds the relative error from the normalized nominal density. Proportionality fixes a constant multiplier, without requiring that multiplier to equal $\beta^m$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-38`: For each norm, there exist $S$, $b_n\rightarrow\infty$, and $c>0$ such that, for every $a>0$, the polar measures $n\tmop{P}((\|U\|/b_n,U/\|U\|)\in\cdot)$ restricted to $[a,\infty]\times\mathbb{S}^+$ converge weakly as finite measures to the corresponding restriction of $c\nu_\beta\times S$. — pins reading of def:regular-variation: Definition~\ref{def:mvarying} specifies $\nu_\beta([r,\infty))=r^{-\beta}$, which is finite away from zero and infinite over the entire radial space. The immediately following discussion identifies the intended Pareto tail limit, fixing the standard tail-measure interpretation.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-39`: $f$ is a fixed measurable version satisfying $f(X,D,U)=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X,D,U]$ almost surely, and this same version is used in Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo}. — pins reading of def:conditional-mean: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} first defines $f$ through conditional expectation and then immediately imposes a pointwise homogeneity property on that function. The standard meaning of conditional expectation fixes the equality almost surely.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-43`: The homogeneity inequality holds for every $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every $t>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R=\{x:\|x\|_1\leqslant R\}$ and $u\in S^{d-1}=\{u\in\mathbb R_+^d:\|u\|_1=1\}$, with $g$ being $L$-Lipschitz on this domain under the $\ell_1$ norm convention. — pins reading of ass:f-homo-asymptotic: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} defines the approximation using the conditional mean on the nonnegative noise domain. The Notations paragraph fixes the norm; Proposition~\ref{prop:id} uses both treatment values, and its added homogeneity condition explicitly specifies every positive radius and the positive unit sphere.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-44`: For each fixed $t$ and tolerance $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, the three nuisance inequalities, with $\delta=\varepsilon$, hold on one $\mathcal T$-measurable event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, simultaneously almost surely over the covariate support, the conditional direction support and both treatments, where $\mathcal T$ consists of the training split and independent auxiliary data. — pins reading of ass:rate-nuisance-errors: Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} fits nuisances on the training split before evaluation; Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}'s stated nuisance reading explicitly specifies the joint training-measurable event and simultaneous support interpretation.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-45`: The training indices are chosen uniformly from the subsets of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ of size $n/2$, independently of the sampled observations; the remaining indices form $\mathcal D_2$. — pins reading of def:nete-algorithm: Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} specifies a random equal split, and the following estimation paragraph uses the first half for fitting and the second half for evaluation, invoking the standard independent sample-splitting convention.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-7`: $Y(D)=Y$ almost surely throughout the paper. — pins reading of ass:consistency: The Causal Inference paragraph states consistency for random variables in the potential-outcome framework; equality of random variables in this framework is understood almost surely.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-0b814294`: The equal split in Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} is defined for positive even sample sizes $n$, with $|\mathcal D_1|=|\mathcal D_2|=n/2$. — pins reading of lem:eta-rate: stated by the repair

- proof change: the printed proof, revised step by step] Condition on the training split and independent auxiliary data, denoted together by $\mathcal T$. Write $R_U=\|U\|$, $S_U=U/\|U\|$, and \[ m_d^t(x,s)=\mathbb E[Y/R_U^{\alpha}\mid X=x,D=d,S_U=s,R_U>t],\qquad \eta_\alpha^t=\mathbb E[(f(X,1,U)-f(X,0,U))/R_U^{\alpha}\mid R_U>t]. \] Independence of $U$ from $(X,D)$ gives $\mathbb E[D\mid X,S_U,R_U>t]=p(X)$. Let $\psi_\alpha$ be the score $\psi$ with $\hat\alpha_n$ replaced by $\alpha$, keeping the same fitted functions. Direct conditional expectation over treatment and outcome gives \begin{align*} \mathbb E[\psi_\alpha\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]-\eta_\alpha^t=\mathbb E\left[(p(X)-\hat p(X))\left\{\frac{m_1^t(X,S_U)-\hat g(X,1,S_U)}{\hat p(X)}+\frac{m_0^t(X,S_U)-\hat g(X,0,S_U)}{1-\hat p(X)}\right\}\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t\right]. \end{align*} Indeed, the coefficients of $m_1^t-\hat g_1$ and $m_0^t-\hat g_0$ are respectively $p/\hat p-1$ and $1-(1-p)/(1-\hat p)$, which equal $(p-\hat p)/\hat p$ and $(p-\hat p)/(1-\hat p)$. On the joint nuisance event at tolerance $\delta/4$, the uniform bounds and estimated-propensity overlap therefore imply \[ |\mathbb E[\psi_\alpha\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]-\eta_\alpha^t|\leq\frac2c R_p(n/2,\delta/4)R_g(n_t,\delta/4). \]      where $n_t = \sum_{i = 1}^{n/2} I (\|U_i \|> t)$ is a random variable. Keep this training count as the argument of $R_g$; no regularity of that error function beyond its stated monotonicity is needed. Homogeneity at each realized radius gives \[ |\eta_\alpha^t-\mathbb E[g(X,1,S_U)-g(X,0,S_U)\mid R_U>t]|\leq2\bar e(t). \] The function $s\mapsto\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,s)-g(X,0,s)]$ is $2L$-Lipschitz. Independence of $X$ and $U$ and the definition of the limiting angular target therefore give \[ |\eta_\alpha^t-\eta|\leq2\bar e(t)+2L W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}). \] Proposition 3.1 of \cite{zhang2023wasserstein}, with Wasserstein order one and tail index $\beta$, supplies the stated angular bound with its logarithmic factors. Next, the fitted functions cancel from $\psi-\psi_\alpha$. Taking conditional expectation over the outcome and treatment leaves \[ \mathbb E\left[\left\{\frac{p(X)}{\hat p(X)}\frac{f(X,1,U)}{R_U^{\alpha}}-\frac{1-p(X)}{1-\hat p(X)}\frac{f(X,0,U)}{R_U^{\alpha}}\right\}\left(R_U^{\alpha-\hat\alpha_n}-1\right)\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t\right]. \] On the nuisance event, $|\alpha-\hat\alpha_n|\leq a$. Since $R_U\geq t\geq1$, we have $|R_U^{\alpha-\hat\alpha_n}-1|\leq R_U^a-1$. Homogeneity also gives $|f(X,d,U)/R_U^{\alpha}|\leq G_t$, so \[ |\mathbb E[\psi-\psi_\alpha\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]|\leq\frac{2G_t}{c}\mathbb E[R_U^a-1\mid R_U>t]. \] Choose $\epsilon=(\beta-a_0)/2>0$. Potter's bound gives, uniformly for $r\geq1$ and sufficiently large $t$, \[ \frac{\mathbb P(R_U>tr)}{\pi_t}\leq2r^{-\beta+\epsilon}. \] Integrating the radial tail, for $0\leq a\leq a_0$, yields \begin{align*} \mathbb E[R_U^a-1\mid R_U>t]&=t^a-1+t^a\int_1^{\infty}a r^{a-1}\frac{\mathbb P(R_U>tr)}{\pi_t}\,\tmop{dr}\\ &\leq t^a-1+\frac{2a t^a}{\beta-\epsilon-a}\leq t^a-1+K a t^a. \end{align*} This also covers $a=0$, when both sides vanish. Combining the three mean-error bounds controls $|\mathbb E[\psi\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]-\eta|$ by the nonsampling terms in the statement. For evaluation, $M$ is binomial with parameters $n/2$ and $\pi_t$, independent of the training split and auxiliary data. The binomial lower-tail bound gives \[ \mathbb P(M<n\pi_t/4)\leq\exp(-n\pi_t/16)\leq\delta/4. \] Conditional on $\mathcal T$ and any positive $M$, the evaluation scores are independent with the conditional tail law used to define $v_t(B)$ and $a_t(B)$. Put $Z=\psi I\{|\psi|\leq B\}$ and $W=\psi I\{|\psi|>B\}$. Chebyshev's inequality gives \[ \mathbb P\left(\left|\frac1M\sum Z_i-\mathbb E[Z\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]\right|>\sqrt{\frac{4v_t(B)}{\delta M}}\ \middle|\ \mathcal T,M\right)\leq\delta/4. \] Markov's inequality gives \[ \mathbb P\left(\frac1M\sum|W_i|>\frac{4a_t(B)}\delta\ \middle|\ \mathcal T,M\right)\leq\delta/4; \] if $a_t(B)=0$, the tail variables vanish almost surely and this assertion holds directly. On the intersection of these events, the triangle inequality gives \[ \left|\frac1M\sum\psi_i-\mathbb E[\psi\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{4v_t(B)}{\delta M}}+\left(1+\frac4\delta\right)a_t(B). \] The joint nuisance event fails with probability at most $\delta/4$. Adding its failure probability, the count failure probability, and the two conditional sampling failure probabilities gives total failure probability at most $\delta$. The estimator is the average of these scores on the resulting event, which proves the bound. If $V_t<\infty$, apply Chebyshev directly to the untruncated score average with failure probability $\delta/4$; this gives the stated replacement sampling term, with total failure probability still at most $\delta$.
- why: The reference to Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} imports its hypotheses, not its conclusion, so the lint observation about an absent theorem edge is dismissed. Exogeneity, overlap, the three i.i.d. blocks, and the three Pareto-model blocks are all inherited; the other eight lint observations are dismissed. The setting faithfully distinguishes training and evaluation counts. Ambiguity remains between the displayed covariate-law class and the noise-law interpretation, and between equal and distinct component dimensions. I use the binding limiting angular meaning of $\eta$. Reproduction yields six independent failures: (a) the sampling step asserts a square-root rate without controlling the variance of the normalized score; (b) the homogeneity remainder over exceedances is controlled by an average of $e(\|U\|)$ or its tail supremum, not by $e(t)$; (c) at $\beta=1$, the angular bias can be $\Theta(t^{-1}\log t)$, so the logarithm omitted from [Zhang et al., Proposition 3.1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.09862) is material; (d) exponent error is not uniformly linear in $R_\alpha(n,\delta)\log t$ without a smallness restriction; (e) monotonicity does not permit replacing a random training count by $nt^{-\beta}$ inside an arbitrary $R_g$; and (f) separate nuisance and evaluation guarantees do not retain failure probability $\delta$ with the same nuisance-bound arguments. The evaluation sampling term uses $|\mathcal I|$, rather than the training $n_t$. Both counts can concentrate, but this does not repair (e). Correcting the proof's triangle-inequality signs, conditioning notation, Lipschitz factor, and Bernstein lower-tail sign leaves all six counterexamples intact.

### `lem:eta-ipw` (`Arxiv_template.tex:787-795`)

- as printed: \label{lemma:eta_ipw} Under the assumption of Theorem \ref{thm:rate}, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, we have \begin{align*} | \hat{\eta}_{n, t}^{\text{IPW}} - \eta | & \leqslant O (R_p (n/2, \delta) + t^{\beta / 2} n^{- 1 / 2} + t^{- \min \{1, \beta\}} + t^{- \beta s / (1 - 2 s)}) + e (t) . \end{align*}
- corrected: Under the assumption of Theorem \ref{thm:rate}, suppose in addition that the conditions of Proposition~\ref{prop:id} hold. Suppose in addition that the conditions of Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} hold. Use its training split and independent auxiliary data, denoted together by $\mathcal T$, its $\pi_t=\mathbb P(\|U\|>t)$, $M=|\mathcal I|$, $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$, and $G_t=C+\bar e(t)$, where $C$ bounds $|g|$ on its compact domain. Set $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\text{IPW}}=0$ if $M=0$. For a generic evaluation observation define \[ \phi=\frac{Y}{\|U\|^{\hat\alpha_n}}\left(\frac{D}{\hat p(X)}-\frac{1-D}{1-\hat p(X)}\right).\] For any $B>0$ chosen using only $\mathcal T$, put \[\begin{aligned} v_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)&=\mathbb E[\phi^2 I\{|\phi|\leq B\}\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t],\\ b_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)&=\mathbb E[|\phi|I\{|\phi|>B\}\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t].\end{aligned}\] Fix $a_0\in(0,\beta)$ and put $a=R_\alpha(n,\delta/4)$. For sufficiently large $t\geq1$, if $a\leq a_0$ and $n\pi_t\geq16\log(4/\delta)$, then with probability at least $1-\delta$ we have $M\geq n\pi_t/4$ and \[\begin{aligned} |\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\text{IPW}}-\eta|\leq{}&\sqrt{\frac{4v_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)}{\delta M}}+\left(1+\frac4\delta\right)b_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)+\frac{2G_t}{c}R_p(n/2,\delta/4)\\ &+2\bar e(t)+2L W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|})+\frac{2G_t}{c}(t^a-1+K a t^a),\end{aligned}\] where $K$ depends on the radial law and $a_0$. Under the angular assumption of Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, taken from Proposition 3.1 of \citet{zhang2023wasserstein}, the angular Wasserstein term has the bound stated there for $t\geq t_W$, with its logarithmic factor retained. When $V_t^{\text{IPW}}=\tmop{Var}(\phi\mid\mathcal T,\|U\|>t)$ is finite, the sampling term may instead be taken to be $\sqrt{4V_t^{\text{IPW}}/(\delta M)}$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-33`: $U\perp(X,D)$. — pins reading of ass:iid-independence: The opening model description calls $U$ an independent extreme-noise vector, and Assumption~\ref{assump:iid} states that it is independent of $X,D$. Standard multivariate independence treats the listed variables jointly.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-34`: $\|X\|_1\leqslant R$ almost surely. — pins reading of ass:f-homo-bounded-x: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} bounds the random covariate, and the Notations paragraph explicitly identifies both norm symbols with the $\ell_1$-norm.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-36`: For each component law in $\tilde M_k$, there exists one constant $C_k>0$, independent of $z$, such that $h(z)=C_k\prod_{i=1}^{m}(1+z_i)^{-(\beta+1)}$ whenever $\|z\|_1>\zeta k^{(1-2s)/\beta}$. — pins reading of ass:mvary-density: Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} uses $\varpropto$ on the stated tail region and separately bounds the relative error from the normalized nominal density. Proportionality fixes a constant multiplier, without requiring that multiplier to equal $\beta^m$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-38`: For each norm, there exist $S$, $b_n\rightarrow\infty$, and $c>0$ such that, for every $a>0$, the polar measures $n\tmop{P}((\|U\|/b_n,U/\|U\|)\in\cdot)$ restricted to $[a,\infty]\times\mathbb{S}^+$ converge weakly as finite measures to the corresponding restriction of $c\nu_\beta\times S$. — pins reading of def:regular-variation: Definition~\ref{def:mvarying} specifies $\nu_\beta([r,\infty))=r^{-\beta}$, which is finite away from zero and infinite over the entire radial space. The immediately following discussion identifies the intended Pareto tail limit, fixing the standard tail-measure interpretation.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-39`: $f$ is a fixed measurable version satisfying $f(X,D,U)=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X,D,U]$ almost surely, and this same version is used in Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo}. — pins reading of def:conditional-mean: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} first defines $f$ through conditional expectation and then immediately imposes a pointwise homogeneity property on that function. The standard meaning of conditional expectation fixes the equality almost surely.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-42`: For each fixed $\mathcal T$-measurable choice $B>0$, the displayed probability bound uses the base conditions of Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}; its angular specialization additionally uses that lemma's angular assumption and $t\geq t_W$, and its variance alternative additionally requires $V_t^{\text{IPW}}<\infty$. — pins reading of lem:eta-ipw: The statement explicitly says ``Under the angular assumption'' and ``When'' the variance is finite; the amended proof applies conditional sampling inequalities to the chosen $B$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-43`: The homogeneity inequality holds for every $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every $t>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R=\{x:\|x\|_1\leqslant R\}$ and $u\in S^{d-1}=\{u\in\mathbb R_+^d:\|u\|_1=1\}$, with $g$ being $L$-Lipschitz on this domain under the $\ell_1$ norm convention. — pins reading of ass:f-homo-asymptotic: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} defines the approximation using the conditional mean on the nonnegative noise domain. The Notations paragraph fixes the norm; Proposition~\ref{prop:id} uses both treatment values, and its added homogeneity condition explicitly specifies every positive radius and the positive unit sphere.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-44`: For each fixed $t$ and tolerance $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, the three nuisance inequalities, with $\delta=\varepsilon$, hold on one $\mathcal T$-measurable event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, simultaneously almost surely over the covariate support, the conditional direction support and both treatments, where $\mathcal T$ consists of the training split and independent auxiliary data. — pins reading of ass:rate-nuisance-errors: Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} fits nuisances on the training split before evaluation; Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}'s stated nuisance reading explicitly specifies the joint training-measurable event and simultaneous support interpretation.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-45`: The training indices are chosen uniformly from the subsets of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ of size $n/2$, independently of the sampled observations; the remaining indices form $\mathcal D_2$. — pins reading of def:nete-algorithm: Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} specifies a random equal split, and the following estimation paragraph uses the first half for fitting and the second half for evaluation, invoking the standard independent sample-splitting convention.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-7`: $Y(D)=Y$ almost surely throughout the paper. — pins reading of ass:consistency: The Causal Inference paragraph states consistency for random variables in the potential-outcome framework; equality of random variables in this framework is understood almost surely.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-0b814294`: The equal split in Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} is defined for positive even sample sizes $n$, with $|\mathcal D_1|=|\mathcal D_2|=n/2$. — pins reading of lem:eta-rate: stated by the repair

- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-c3605ca3`: The sample-size domain of $R_g(q,\varepsilon)$ includes every possible training exceedance count $q\in\{0,1,\ldots,n/2\}$, including $q=0$. — pins reading of lem:eta-rate: stated by the repair

- proof change: the printed proof, revised step by step] Write $R_U=\|U\|$ and $S_U=U/\|U\|$, and define \[\begin{aligned} m_d^t(x,s)&=\mathbb E[Y/R_U^\alpha\mid X=x,D=d,S_U=s,R_U>t],\\ \eta_\alpha^t&=\mathbb E[(f(X,1,U)-f(X,0,U))/R_U^\alpha\mid R_U>t].\end{aligned}\] Independence of $U$ from $(X,D)$ gives $\mathbb P(D=1\mid X,S_U,R_U>t)=p(X)$. Let $\phi_\alpha$ be $\phi$ with $\hat\alpha_n$ replaced by $\alpha$. Conditional expectation over treatment and outcome gives \[\begin{aligned} &\mathbb E[\phi_\alpha\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]-\eta_\alpha^t\\ &\quad=\mathbb E\left[(p(X)-\hat p(X))\left\{\frac{m_1^t(X,S_U)}{\hat p(X)}+\frac{m_0^t(X,S_U)}{1-\hat p(X)}\right\}\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t\right].\end{aligned}\] Indeed, the treatment coefficients after subtraction are $p/\hat p-1=(p-\hat p)/\hat p$ and $1-(1-p)/(1-\hat p)=(p-\hat p)/(1-\hat p)$. Homogeneity gives $|m_d^t|\leq G_t$. On the joint nuisance event at tolerance $\delta/4$, estimated-propensity overlap therefore gives \[ |\mathbb E[\phi_\alpha\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]-\eta_\alpha^t|\leq\frac{2G_t}{c}R_p(n/2,\delta/4).\] Taking conditional expectations over the outcome in the exponent difference gives \[\begin{aligned} &\mathbb E[\phi-\phi_\alpha\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]\\ &=\mathbb E\left[\left\{\frac{p(X)f(X,1,U)}{\hat p(X)R_U^\alpha}-\frac{(1-p(X))f(X,0,U)}{(1-\hat p(X))R_U^\alpha}\right\}(R_U^{\alpha-\hat\alpha_n}-1)\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t\right].\end{aligned}\] Since $R_U>t\geq1$ and $|\alpha-\hat\alpha_n|\leq a$, the absolute value is at most $(2G_t/c)\mathbb E[R_U^a-1\mid R_U>t]$. Put $\epsilon=(\beta-a_0)/2$. Potter's bound gives $\mathbb P(R_U>tr)/\pi_t\leq2r^{-\beta+\epsilon}$ for all $r\geq1$ and sufficiently large $t$. Integration gives \[\begin{aligned} \mathbb E[R_U^a-1\mid R_U>t]&=t^a-1+t^a\int_1^\infty a r^{a-1}\frac{\mathbb P(R_U>tr)}{\pi_t}\,\tmop{dr}\\ &\leq t^a-1+\frac{2a t^a}{\beta-\epsilon-a}\leq t^a-1+K a t^a,\end{aligned}\] with $K=4/(\beta-a_0)$; for $a=0$ both sides vanish. The triangle inequality now supplies the decomposition into sampling fluctuation, propensity error, exponent error, and $|\eta_\alpha^t-\eta|$.  Homogeneity at the realized radius gives \[ |\eta_\alpha^t-\mathbb E[g(X,1,S_U)-g(X,0,S_U)\mid R_U>t]|\leq2\bar e(t).\] The function $s\mapsto\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,s)-g(X,0,s)]$ is $2L$-Lipschitz. Independence of $X$ and $U$ and the Wasserstein coupling inequality therefore give \[ |\eta_\alpha^t-\eta|\leq2\bar e(t)+2L W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\|U\|}).\] Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} supplies the angular bound under the identical model assumptions, including its logarithmic factors. Conditional on $\mathcal T$ and positive $M$, the evaluation scores are independent with the conditional exceedance law. Put $Z=\phi I\{|\phi|\leq B\}$ and $W=\phi I\{|\phi|>B\}$. Chebyshev's and Markov's inequalities give, respectively, conditional failure probabilities at most $\delta/4$ for \[\begin{aligned} \left|M^{-1}\sum Z_i-\mathbb E[Z\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]\right|&\leq\sqrt{4v_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)/(\delta M)},\\ M^{-1}\sum|W_i|&\leq4b_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)/\delta.\end{aligned}\] If $b_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)=0$, the second inequality holds almost surely because $W=0$ almost surely. Combining them with $|\mathbb E[W\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]|\leq b_t^{\text{IPW}}(B)$ bounds the sampling fluctuation by the first two terms in the statement. The count has law $M\sim\tmop{Bin}(n/2,\pi_t)$, so \[ \mathbb P(M<n\pi_t/4)\leq\exp(-n\pi_t/16)\leq\delta/4.\] The joint nuisance event also fails with probability at most $\delta/4$. The union of these four failures has probability at most $\delta$. On their complement, the mean-error bounds and the sampling inequality prove the result. If $V_t^{\text{IPW}}<\infty$, direct conditional Chebyshev gives sampling error at most $\sqrt{4V_t^{\text{IPW}}/(\delta M)}$ with failure probability at most $\delta/4$, and the same union bound proves the asserted alternative.
- why: The lint warnings are dismissed: ``Under the assumption of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}'' imports its hypotheses, not its conclusions. The core correctly wires each listed assumption directly, and no dependency on the theorem's conclusion is required. The model-class readings $\mathcal L(X)$ versus $\mathcal L(U)$ and unspecified $m$ versus $m=d_z$ remain unresolved. Reproducing the IPW conditional mean gives propensity error, exponent perturbation, radial approximation bias, angular bias, and sampling fluctuation. (a) The displayed conclusion drops the exponent term retained by its own proof. (b) The cited sampling bound requires outcome-tail conditions absent here; [Su et al., Theorem 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2303.17102v1) assumes outcome moment bounds and a specified logistic estimation framework. (c) Referencing Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} does not permit replacing its radial error envelope by $e(t)$. (d) Its angular bound retains a logarithm at $\beta=1$, which the displayed conclusion omits.

### `thm:cor-rate-ipw` (`Arxiv_template.tex:842-865`)

- as printed: \label{cor:rate_ipw}Under the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, further suppose that \begin{align*} R_p (n, \delta) = \Theta (\log (1 / \delta) n^{- 1 / 2}) , R_g (n, \delta) = \Theta (\log (1 / \delta) n^{- 1 / 2}), R_\alpha (n, \delta) = \Theta( \log(1/\delta) n^{-c_\alpha}), \end{align*} for some $c_\alpha > 0 $, the following conclusions hold. \begin{enumerate} \item If $s \in (0, 1 / (2 + \max \{1, \beta\}))$, takes $t_n = \Theta (n^{ (1 - 2 s) \hat{\gamma}_{n}})$, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, we have \begin{align*} | {{\widehat{\theta }_{n, t}^{\text{IPW} }} } - \theta^{NETE} | & = O (e (t_n) + n^{- s} \log (1 / \delta) + n^{-c_\alpha} \log(n)\log (1 / \delta)) . \end{align*} \item If $s \in [1 / (2 + \max \{1, \beta\}), 1 / 2)$, \ takes $t = \Theta (n^{(\hat{\gamma}_{n} / (1 + 2 \min \{1, \hat{\gamma}_{n} \})})$, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, we have \begin{align*} | {{\widehat{\theta }_{n, t}^{\text{IPW} }} } - \theta^{NETE} | & = O (e (t_n) + n^{- 1 / (2 + \max \{\beta, 1\})} \log (1 / \delta) + n^{-c_\alpha} \log(n)\log (1 / \delta)) . \end{align*} \end{enumerate}
- corrected: Under the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, further suppose that \begin{align*} R_p(n,\delta)&=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-1/2}),\qquad R_g(n,\delta)=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-1/2}),\\ R_\alpha(n,\delta)&=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-c_\alpha}) \end{align*} for some $c_\alpha>0$. Suppose in addition that the conditions of Proposition~\ref{prop:id} hold. Let $\mathcal T$ consist of the training split and independent auxiliary data. Suppose that $\hat p$, $\hat g$ and $\hat\alpha_n$ are $\mathcal T$-measurable and are the same functions and exponent for every input threshold. Write $R_U=\|U\|$, $S_U=U/\|U\|$, $\gamma=1/\beta$, $\pi_t=\mathbb P(R_U>t)$, and \[ m_d^t(x,v)=\mathbb E[Y/R_U^\alpha\mid X=x,D=d,S_U=v,R_U>t]. \] In each case below, let $\kappa$ be the exponent function specified in that case. Use fixed constants $0<c_-\leq c_+<\infty$ in the threshold order, so that $c_-n^{\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)}\leq t_n\leq c_+n^{\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)}$, and define the deterministic band \[ \ell_n=(c_-/2)n^{\kappa(\gamma)},\qquad u_n=2c_+n^{\kappa(\gamma)},\qquad I_n=[\ell_n,u_n]. \] Suppose that with probability at least $1-\delta/6$, jointly for every $t\in I_n$, \[ \begin{aligned} |\hat\alpha_n-\alpha|&\leq R_\alpha(n,\delta/6),\\ |p(x)-\hat p(x)|&\leq R_p(n/2,\delta/6),\\ |m_d^t(x,v)-\hat g(x,d,v)|&\leq R_g(n_t,\delta/6), \end{aligned} \] simultaneously almost surely over the covariate and conditional direction support and both treatments, where $n_t=\sum_{i=1}^{n/2}I(\|U_i\|>t)$ is the training exceedance count. On this same event suppose that the fitted-exponent pseudo-outcome has finite conditional absolute mean for every $t\in I_n$ and that, for a constant $V<\infty$ independent of $n$ and $t$, \[ \mathbb E[\phi^2\mid\mathcal T,R_U>t]\leq V\qquad(t\in I_n),\qquad \phi=\frac{Y}{R_U^{\hat\alpha_n}}\left(\frac{D}{\hat p(X)}-\frac{1-D}{1-\hat p(X)}\right). \] Set the first-factor estimate to zero if there are no evaluation exceedances. Run the full-input Hill selection of Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} at tolerance $\delta/6$, and put \[ b_n=C_{\delta/6}\begin{cases} \left(\dfrac{\log((48/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&0<\beta<1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log n\,\log((48/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/3},&\beta=1,\\ \left(\dfrac{\log((48/\delta)\log n)}{n}\right)^{1/(2+\beta)},&\beta>1, \end{cases} \] with the constant supplied by Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}. Define \[ \bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r). \] Then, for each fixed $\delta\in(0,1/2)$ and every sufficiently large even $n$, the following conclusions hold. Here $k\geq0$ is the exponent of the logarithm in the angular assumption of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, taken from Proposition 3.1 of \citet{zhang2023wasserstein}; under that assumption the angular term at the threshold $t_n$ is of the order shown, up to this power of $\log n$. \begin{enumerate} \item If $s\in(0,1/(2+\max\{1,\beta\}))$, take $t_n=\Theta(n^{(1-2s)\hat\gamma_n})$ and put $\kappa(v)=(1-2s)v$. With probability at least $1-\delta$, we have \[ \begin{aligned} |\widehat\theta_{n,t_n}^{\text{IPW}}-\theta^{NETE}|=O\bigl(&\sqrt{V/\delta}\,n^{-s}+\log(6/\delta)n^{-1/2}\\ &+\bar e(t_n)+(\log n)^k n^{-s}+n^{-c_\alpha}\log n\log(6/\delta)+|\alpha|b_n\bigr). \end{aligned} \] \item If $s\in[1/(2+\max\{1,\beta\}),1/2)$, take $t_n=\Theta(n^{\hat\gamma_n/(1+2\min\{1,\hat\gamma_n\})})$ and put $\kappa(v)=v/(1+2\min\{1,v\})$. Write $q=1/(2+\max\{\beta,1\})$. With probability at least $1-\delta$, we have \[ \begin{aligned} |\widehat\theta_{n,t_n}^{\text{IPW}}-\theta^{NETE}|=O\bigl(&\sqrt{V/\delta}\,n^{-q}+\log(6/\delta)n^{-1/2}\\ &+\bar e(t_n)+(\log n)^k n^{-q}+n^{-c_\alpha}\log n\log(6/\delta)+|\alpha|b_n\bigr). \end{aligned} \] \end{enumerate}

- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-f18f3355`: The target $\eta$ denotes $\int\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,s)-g(X,0,s)]\,S(\tmop{ds})$, where $S$ is the limiting spectral probability measure. — pins reading of lem:eta-rate: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-fc4621e4`: For each fixed $\delta\in(0,1/2)$, the probability guarantee quantifies separately over every sufficiently large even $n$. — pins reading of thm:cor-rate-ipw: stated by the repair
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-fdafcd87`: The constants $c_-$ and $c_+$ in $t_n=\Theta(n^{\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)})$ are deterministic and independent of $n$ and the observed sample. — pins reading of thm:cor-rate-ipw: stated by the repair
- proof change: Fix $\delta\in(0,1/2)$ and an even $n$. Let $E_T$ be the uniform nuisance and moment event from the statement. Its failure probability is at most $\delta/6$. Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}, applied to the full-input Hill selection at tolerance $\delta/6$, supplies an event $E_\gamma$ with failure probability at most $\delta/6$ on which $|\hat\gamma_n-\gamma|\leq b_n$. Each displayed radial regime gives $b_n\log n\to0$ for fixed $\delta$. The first exponent function has derivative $1-2s$. For the second exponent function the derivative is $1/(1+2v)^2$ when $v<1$ and $1/3$ when $v>1$, and the function is continuous at $v=1$. Both exponent functions are therefore Lipschitz with constant at most $1$. On $E_\gamma$, \[ \frac{n^{\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)}}{n^{\kappa(\gamma)}}=\exp\{(\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)-\kappa(\gamma))\log n\}\in[1/2,2] \] for sufficiently large $n$. The threshold order consequently implies $t_n\in I_n$.  Put $m=n/2$ and $r=(1-\beta\kappa(\gamma))/2$. In the first case $\beta\kappa(\gamma)=1-2s$, so $r=s$. In the second case, put $a_*=\min\{1,\beta\}$. Then $\kappa(\gamma)=1/(\beta+2a_*)$ and $r=a_*/(\beta+2a_*)=q$. The Pareto-type model gives $\pi_t\asymp t^{-\beta}$, whence \[ \pi_{\ell_n}\asymp\pi_{u_n}\asymp n^{-1+2r},\qquad m\pi_{u_n}\asymp n^{2r}. \] Both $r=s$ and $r=q$ are positive. The training and evaluation counts at $u_n$ are binomial with parameters $m,\pi_{u_n}$. For either count $N_{u_n}$, \[ \mathbb P(N_{u_n}<m\pi_{u_n}/2)\leq\exp(-m\pi_{u_n}/8)\leq\delta/6 \] for sufficiently large $n$. Outside these two failure events, both counts at every threshold in $I_n$ are at least $m\pi_{u_n}/2$.  Here is uniform sampling control for the IPW score. Conditional on $\mathcal T$, the evaluation observations $Z_i=(X_i,D_i,Y_i,U_i)$ are independent and $\phi$ is a fixed function. Denote their conditional law by $P_T$, and put \[ A(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\{\phi(Z_i)I(R_i>t)-P_T[\phi I(R_U>t)]\},\qquad C(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\{I(R_i>t)-\pi_t\}. \] Introduce an independent conditional copy $Z'_1,\ldots,Z'_m$. Jensen's inequality first bounds the conditional expected squared supremum of $A$ by the expected squared supremum of the difference of the two sample sums. Independent symmetric signs $\varepsilon_i$ may multiply these differences because swapping either member of each independent pair preserves its law. Applying $|a-b|^2\leq2|a|^2+2|b|^2$ to the signed difference gives \[ \mathbb E[\sup_{t\in I_n}|A(t)|^2\mid\mathcal T]\leq4\mathbb E\left[\sup_{t\in I_n}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varepsilon_i\phi(Z_i)I(R_i>t)\right|^2\middle|\mathcal T\right]. \] Conditional on the observations, order those with $R_i>\ell_n$ by decreasing radius. Each sum indexed by $t\in I_n$ is a partial sum of the independent mean-zero signed variables in that order. The square-integrable martingale maximal inequality bounds its expected squared maximum by $4\sum_i\phi(Z_i)^2I(R_i>\ell_n)$. Hence, on $E_T$, \[ \mathbb E[\sup_{t\in I_n}|A(t)|^2\mid\mathcal T]\leq16mV\pi_{\ell_n}. \] Repeating these inequalities with the constant score $1$ gives $\mathbb E[\sup_{t\in I_n}|C(t)|^2\mid\mathcal T]\leq16m\pi_{\ell_n}$. Markov's inequality supplies two events, each with conditional failure probability at most $\delta/6$, on which \[ \sup_{t\in I_n}|A(t)|\leq\sqrt{96mV\pi_{\ell_n}/\delta},\qquad \sup_{t\in I_n}|C(t)|\leq\sqrt{96m\pi_{\ell_n}/\delta}. \] Integration over $\mathcal T$ preserves these failure bounds on $E_T$. The suprema are measurable by taking a countable dense set of thresholds and the band endpoints.  Let $\mu_t=P_T[\phi\mid R_U>t]$ and $M_t=\sum_i I(R_i>t)$. On $E_T$, $|\mu_t|\leq\sqrt V$ by Cauchy--Schwarz. The identity \[ \sum_i\phi(Z_i)I(R_i>t)-M_t\mu_t=A(t)-\mu_t C(t) \] and the evaluation-count bound imply, simultaneously for every $t\in I_n$, \[ |\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\text{IPW}}-\mu_t|\leq4\sqrt{\frac{96V\pi_{\ell_n}}{\delta m\pi_{u_n}^2}}=O(\sqrt{V/\delta}\,n^{-r}). \] It is therefore valid at the selected $t_n$ on $E_\gamma$.  To bound the conditional mean, put $a=R_\alpha(n,\delta/6)$, fix $a_0\in(0,\beta)$, and let $G_t=C_g+\bar e(t)$, where $C_g$ bounds $|g|$. For large $n$, $a\leq a_0$ and $G_t$ is uniformly bounded on $I_n$. Let $\phi_\alpha$ be the score with exponent $\alpha$ and put \[ \eta_\alpha^t=\mathbb E[(f(X,1,U)-f(X,0,U))/R_U^\alpha\mid R_U>t]. \] Independence of $U$ from $(X,D)$ gives the treatment probability $p(X)$ after conditioning on direction and exceedance. Conditional expectation over treatment and outcome therefore yields \[ P_T[\phi_\alpha\mid R_U>t]-\eta_\alpha^t=\mathbb E\left[(p-\hat p)\left\{\frac{m_1^t(X,S_U)}{\hat p}+\frac{m_0^t(X,S_U)}{1-\hat p}\right\}\middle|\mathcal T,R_U>t\right]. \] Homogeneity gives $|m_d^t|\leq G_t$, so overlap and the uniform propensity inequality bound this difference by $(2G_t/c)R_p(n/2,\delta/6)=O(\log(6/\delta)n^{-1/2})$ throughout $I_n$.  Taking the conditional outcome mean in the exponent difference gives \[ \begin{aligned} P_T[\phi-\phi_\alpha\mid R_U>t]=\mathbb E\biggl[\biggl\{&\frac{p(X)f(X,1,U)}{\hat p(X)R_U^\alpha}\\ &-\frac{(1-p(X))f(X,0,U)}{(1-\hat p(X))R_U^\alpha}\biggr\}(R_U^{\alpha-\hat\alpha_n}-1)\biggm|\mathcal T,R_U>t\biggr]. \end{aligned} \] Its absolute value is at most $(2G_t/c)\mathbb E[R_U^a-1\mid R_U>t]$. The radial integration bound in Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw} gives, uniformly for sufficiently large $t$, \[ \mathbb E[R_U^a-1\mid R_U>t]\leq t^a-1+Kat^a,\qquad K=4/(\beta-a_0). \] On $I_n$, $\log t=O(\log n)$ and $a\log t\to0$. Thus $t^a\leq2$ and $t^a-1\leq2a\log t$ for large $n$, and the exponent contribution is $O(\log(6/\delta)n^{-c_\alpha}\log n)$.  Homogeneity at the realized radius bounds the difference between $\eta_\alpha^t$ and the conditional angular contrast by $2\bar e(t)$. The function $v\mapsto\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,v)-g(X,0,v)]$ is $2L$-Lipschitz. Independence of $X$ and $U$ and the Wasserstein coupling inequality then give \[ |\eta_\alpha^t-\eta|\leq2\bar e(t)+\mathcal A_t. \] Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} supplies the angular bound, including its logarithmic factors, and the displayed conclusion retains $\mathcal A_{t_n}$ itself.  By Proposition~\ref{prop:id}, $\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}=\eta/h$ with $h=1-\alpha\gamma>0$. On $E_T\cap E_\gamma$, \[ |\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n-\alpha\gamma|\leq a(\gamma+b_n)+|\alpha|b_n=:Q_n\to0. \] For sufficiently large $n$, $Q_n\leq h/2$. Hence $|1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n|\geq h/2$, $|\hat\mu_n|\leq2/h$, and exact reciprocal subtraction gives $|\hat\mu_n-1/h|\leq2Q_n/h^2$. Finally, \[ \widehat\theta_{n,t_n}^{\text{IPW}}-\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}=\hat\mu_n(\hat\eta_{n,t_n}^{\text{IPW}}-\eta)+\eta(\hat\mu_n-1/h). \] Combining the sampling, propensity, homogeneity, angular and exponent bounds proves the claimed display with $r=s$ in the first case and $r=q$ in the second. The contribution of $a$ from $Q_n$ is absorbed by $a\log n$, and $|\alpha|b_n$ is retained. The uniform nuisance event, Hill event, two count events and two maximal-process events have total failure probability at most $6(\delta/6)=\delta$. This establishes a separate guarantee for each sufficiently large even $n$.

## Findings withdrawn by the owner

Not errors: the paper's owner showed each finding misreads the paper. The printed statement stands, under the owner's reading (or side condition), which the formalization adopts.

- `thm:cor-rate` (raised at F2.5): the owner's reading (`ass:pin-owner-1`): In the rate assumptions $R_p(n,\delta)=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-1/2})$, $R_g(n,\delta)=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-1/2})$ and $R_\alpha(n,\delta)=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-c_\alpha})$, the dependence on the tolerance $\delta$ is the displayed factor $\log(1/\delta)$: the implied constants and the sample size from which the bounds hold do not depend on $\delta$. — the withdrawn finding: [statement-wrong] The nuisance Θ rates leave confidence uniformity unspecified: fixed-tolerance ∀ε ∃cε,Cε,n0ε versus uniform ∃c,C,n0 ∀ε; RateTheta selects the uniform reading. Edit the frozen rate clause to state the intended dependence explicitly.
- `thm:cor-rate-ipw` (raised at F2.5): the owner's reading (`ass:pin-owner-2`): In the rate assumptions $R_p(n,\delta)=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-1/2})$, $R_g(n,\delta)=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-1/2})$ and $R_\alpha(n,\delta)=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-c_\alpha})$, the dependence on the tolerance $\delta$ is the displayed factor $\log(1/\delta)$: the implied constants and the sample size from which the bounds hold do not depend on $\delta$. — the withdrawn finding: [statement-wrong] The three Theta assumptions leave open whether their constants and sufficiently-large-n onset depend on tolerance; Lean selects uniform constants and onset over all tolerances.
- `def:nete-algorithm` (raised at F2.5): the owner's reading (`ass:pin-owner-3`): In Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} the outputs are given a value on every sample by convention: when the index set $\mathcal I$ is empty the averages defining $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}$ and $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{DR}}$ are taken to be $0$, and a Hill statistic whose reference radius $\\|U_{(k+1)}\\|$ is $0$ is taken to be $0$. These conventions concern events the results exclude: the bounds are stated on events where $\|\mathcal I\|\geq1$, and the radii are positive almost surely. — the withdrawn finding: [statement-wrong] The frozen algorithm leaves empty evaluation averages and zero Hill radial denominators undefined on admissible data, whereas Lean selects totalized values.
- `ass:overlap` (raised at F2.5): the owner's reading (`ass:pin-owner-4`): In Assumption~\ref{assump:overlap} the overlap constant $c\in(0,1/2)$ is one deterministic constant: it does not depend on the sample size $n$, on the sample, or on the threshold $t$. — the withdrawn finding: [statement-wrong] The frozen overlap condition does not distinguish a deterministic overlap constant for each fixed sample size from one deterministic constant shared by all sample sizes; Lean selects the latter.

## Suggestions to the authors

Not errors: readings the paper determines but does not state, and side conditions the proofs use silently.

- clarification (`ass:pin-33`, used by `prop:id`): $U\perp(X,D)$. — pins reading of ass:iid-independence: The opening model description calls $U$ an independent extreme-noise vector, and Assumption~\ref{assump:iid} states that it is independent of $X,D$. Standard multivariate independence treats the listed variables jointly.
- clarification (`ass:pin-34`, used by `prop:id`): $\\|X\\|_1\leqslant R$ almost surely. — pins reading of ass:f-homo-bounded-x: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} bounds the random covariate, and the Notations paragraph explicitly identifies both norm symbols with the $\ell_1$-norm.
- clarification (`ass:pin-38`, used by `prop:id`): For each norm, there exist $S$, $b_n\rightarrow\infty$, and $c>0$ such that, for every $a>0$, the polar measures $n\tmop{P}((\\|U\\|/b_n,U/\\|U\\|)\in\cdot)$ restricted to $[a,\infty]\times\mathbb{S}^+$ converge weakly as finite measures to the corresponding restriction of $c\nu_\beta\times S$. — pins reading of def:regular-variation: Definition~\ref{def:mvarying} specifies $\nu_\beta([r,\infty))=r^{-\beta}$, which is finite away from zero and infinite over the entire radial space. The immediately following discussion identifies the intended Pareto tail limit, fixing the standard tail-measure interpretation.
- clarification (`ass:pin-39`, used by `prop:id`): $f$ is a fixed measurable version satisfying $f(X,D,U)=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X,D,U]$ almost surely, and this same version is used in Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo}. — pins reading of def:conditional-mean: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} first defines $f$ through conditional expectation and then immediately imposes a pointwise homogeneity property on that function. The standard meaning of conditional expectation fixes the equality almost surely.
- clarification (`ass:pin-43`, used by `prop:id`): The homogeneity inequality holds for every $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every $t>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R=\{x:\\|x\\|_1\leqslant R\}$ and $u\in S^{d-1}=\{u\in\mathbb R_+^d:\\|u\\|_1=1\}$, with $g$ being $L$-Lipschitz on this domain under the $\ell_1$ norm convention. — pins reading of ass:f-homo-asymptotic: Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} defines the approximation using the conditional mean on the nonnegative noise domain. The Notations paragraph fixes the norm; Proposition~\ref{prop:id} uses both treatment values, and its added homogeneity condition explicitly specifies every positive radius and the positive unit sphere.
- clarification (`ass:pin-7`, used by `prop:id`): $Y(D)=Y$ almost surely throughout the paper. — pins reading of ass:consistency: The Causal Inference paragraph states consistency for random variables in the potential-outcome framework; equality of random variables in this framework is understood almost surely.
- clarification (`ass:pin-fix-8cd5c960`, used by `prop:id`): The homogeneity inequality holds for every $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every radius $t>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R$ and $u\in\mathbb R_+^d$ with $\\|u\\|_1=1$. — pins reading of prop:id: stated by the repair
- clarification (`ass:pin-36`, used by `thm:rate`): For each component law in $\tilde M_k$, there exists one constant $C_k>0$, independent of $z$, such that $h(z)=C_k\prod_{i=1}^{m}(1+z_i)^{-(\beta+1)}$ whenever $\\|z\\|_1>\zeta k^{(1-2s)/\beta}$. — pins reading of ass:mvary-density: Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} uses $\varpropto$ on the stated tail region and separately bounds the relative error from the normalized nominal density. Proportionality fixes a constant multiplier, without requiring that multiplier to equal $\beta^m$.
- clarification (`ass:pin-41`, used by `thm:rate`): The existential cutoff $t_W$ is chosen sufficiently large for Lemmas~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} and~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw} and so that $\sup_{r\geq t_W}e(r)\leq1$ and $\mathbb P(\\|U\\|>tr)/\mathbb P(\\|U\\|>t)\leq4r^{-\beta}$ for every $t\geq t_W$ and $r\geq1$. — pins reading of thm:rate: The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} invokes the first-factor lemmas' sufficiently-large-threshold conclusions, uses the radial moment inequality with $K=4/(\beta-a_0)$, and bounds $G_t$ by $C+1$. The stated Pareto-type radial law and $e(t)\to0$ permit this enlargement of the existential cutoff.
- clarification (`ass:pin-42`, used by `thm:rate`): For each fixed $\mathcal T$-measurable choice $B>0$, the displayed probability bound uses the base conditions of Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}; its angular specialization additionally uses that lemma's angular assumption and $t\geq t_W$, and its variance alternative additionally requires $V_t^{\text{IPW}}<\infty$. — pins reading of lem:eta-ipw: The statement explicitly says ``Under the angular assumption'' and ``When'' the variance is finite; the amended proof applies conditional sampling inequalities to the chosen $B$.
- clarification (`ass:pin-44`, used by `thm:rate`): For each fixed $t$ and tolerance $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, the three nuisance inequalities, with $\delta=\varepsilon$, hold on one $\mathcal T$-measurable event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, simultaneously almost surely over the covariate support, the conditional direction support and both treatments, where $\mathcal T$ consists of the training split and independent auxiliary data. — pins reading of ass:rate-nuisance-errors: Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} fits nuisances on the training split before evaluation; Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}'s stated nuisance reading explicitly specifies the joint training-measurable event and simultaneous support interpretation.

- reading of the symbol `X`: $X\in\mathcal{X}$ denotes the covariate in the potential-outcome model. In Proposition~\ref{prop:id}, Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $\\|X\\|_1\leqslant R$, so the covariate takes values in $B_R$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: In $\eta=\int\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,s)-g(X,0,s)]\,S(\tmop{ds})$, $\mathbb E_X$ integrates with respect to the population marginal law of $X$, rather than an empirical covariate distribution. — Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} gives bounded support, inherited by Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}. The binding target definitions for Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} and both corollaries specify integration against the population marginal law of $X$.
- reading of the symbol `D`: $D\in\{0,1\}$ denotes the treatment indicator. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}: In the proof's expansions $f(t)=C\beta t^{-\beta-1}(1+D(1-1/\beta)t^{-1}+o(t^{-1}))$ and $\bar F(t)=Ct^{-\beta}(1+Dt^{-1}+o(t^{-1}))$, $D$ denotes a fixed expansion coefficient rather than the treatment indicator. — Section~\ref{sec:prelin} introduces $D$ as binary treatment. In the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}, equation~\ref{eq:density_f} and the following survival expansion instead use $D$ as an asymptotic coefficient; there is no explicit redefining sentence.
- reading of the symbol `Y`: $Y$ is the observed outcome satisfying consistency $Y=Y(D)$, where $Y(d)$ denotes the potential outcome when treatment is set to $d$. — The potential-outcome setup in Section~\ref{sec:prelin} explicitly assumes $Y(D)=Y$ throughout the paper; the extreme-data setup retains that definition.
- reading of the symbol `Y(d)`: $Y(d)$ denotes the potential outcome under intervention $d\in\{0,1\}$, with consistency $Y(D)=Y$ throughout. In Proposition~\ref{prop:id}: For both $d\in\{0,1\}$, $\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U,D]=\mathbb E[Y(d)\mid X,U]$ almost surely, and $\mathbb E[\|Y(d)\|\mid\\|U\\|>t]<\infty$ for all sufficiently large $t$. — The potential-outcome paragraph in Section~\ref{sec:prelin} defines the binary intervention and consistency. Proposition~\ref{prop:id} additionally imposes conditional-mean exogeneity given $(X,U)$ and conditional absolute integrability in sufficiently high tails.
- reading of the symbol `U`: $U$ denotes the extreme-noise vector in $\mathbb R_+^d$, with $\\|U\\|=\\|U\\|_1$ throughout the treatment-effect analysis. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} specifies the noise law $\mathcal L(U)$, with $U=AZ$ and $m=d_z$ in the component density. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} specifies the noise law $\mathcal L(U)$, with $U=AZ$ and $m=d_z$ in the component density. — Assumption~\ref{assump:iid} specifies the extreme-noise vector. The current proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate} in discovery/core.json writes the scalar radial tail quantile as $Q(v)=F_R^{-1}(1-1/v)$ and no longer uses $U(t)$ or its former expansion.
- reading of the symbol `d`: $d$ denotes the dimension in $U\in\mathbb R_+^d$ and in sphere notation; when used as the treatment argument in $Y(d)$, $f(x,d,u)$, or $g(x,d,u)$, it is a locally bound treatment value in $\{0,1\}$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $m\geq d\geq2$. — Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} imposes $m\geq d\geq2$. Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} inherit this assumption through Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}.
- reading of the symbol `t`: $t>0$ is the level defining the strict exceedance event $\{\\|U\\|_1>t\}$, except when it is the argument of a scalar function in a tail calculation. In Proposition~\ref{prop:id}: $t$ is a deterministic limit parameter tending to infinity. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: The finite-threshold analysis uses a fixed deterministic $t$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The selected threshold is $t=t_n$, with $c_-n^{\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)}\leq t_n\leq c_+n^{\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)}$, where $0<c_-\leq c_+<\infty$ are deterministic and independent of $n$ and the observed sample. Here $\kappa(v)=(1-2s)v$ when $s\in(0,1/(2+\max\{1,\beta\}))$, and $\kappa(v)=v/(1+2\min\{1,v\})$ when $s\in[1/(2+\max\{1,\beta\}),1/2)$. The threshold-indexed nuisance and conditional-moment conditions hold jointly for every deterministic $t\in I_n=[(c_-/2)n^{\kappa(\gamma)},2c_+n^{\kappa(\gamma)}]$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: The bound concerns sufficiently large $t\geq1$, subject to $n\pi_t\geq16\log(4/\delta)$, where $\pi_t=\mathbb P(\\|U\\|>t)$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: The bound concerns sufficiently large $t\geq1$, subject to $n\pi_t\geq16\log(4/\delta)$, where $\pi_t=\mathbb P(\\|U\\|>t)$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: The main bounds concern deterministic $t\geq t_W$, subject to $n\pi_t\geq16\log(12/\delta)$, where $\pi_t=\mathbb P(\\|U\\|>t)$; the additional simplified bounds also require $t\geq\exp(1)$ and $a\log t\leq1$, where $a=R_\alpha(n,\delta/12)$. — Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} states its main bounds for sufficiently large $n$ and $t\geq t_W$, and its additional simplified bounds require $t\geq\exp(1)$ and $a\log t\leq1$.
- reading of the symbol `\alpha`: $\alpha\in\mathbb R$ is the fixed population homogeneity exponent governing the normalization $f(x,d,tu)/t^\alpha$ and the definition of $\theta^{\tmop{NETE}}$; it is distinct from the supplied approximation $\hat\alpha_n$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:id}, Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $\alpha<\beta$. — Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} introduces the exponent through its homogeneity inequality, and equation~\ref{eq:nete} calls it a known index from prior knowledge. Proposition~\ref{prop:id} and Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} impose $\alpha<\beta$; the remaining listed results inherit that restriction. The positive constant in the synthetic example specializes the same exponent.
- reading of the symbol `\beta`: $\beta\in(0,\infty)$ is the population regular-variation index of the extreme-noise law, with radial limiting measure satisfying $\nu_\beta([r,\infty))=r^{-\beta}$ for $r>0$. — Definition~\ref{def:mvarying} defines the index through the radial limiting measure; Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} uses the same $\beta$ in its Pareto-type model.
- reading of the symbol `R`: $R$ is the fixed covariate bound in $\\|X\\|_1\leqslant R$, except where explicitly rebound as a local radial variable. This local $R$ is distinct from the covariate bound. — Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} introduces the covariate bound. The replacement proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate} in discovery/core.json explicitly says, "Write $R=\\|U\\|_1$."
- reading of the symbol `f`: $f$ denotes a population function with its domain and meaning determined by its local definition, rather than an estimated regression function. In Proposition~\ref{prop:id}, Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: In the extreme-effect model and its decompositions, $f$ is the real-valued population conditional-mean function satisfying $f(X,D,U)=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X,D,U]$, whose pointwise values $f(x,d,u)$ are those appearing in the asymptotic homogeneous property. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: In the extreme-effect model and its decompositions, $f$ is the real-valued population conditional-mean function satisfying $f(X,D,U)=\mathbb E[Y\mid X,D,U]$, whose pointwise values $f(x,d,u)$ appear in the asymptotic homogeneous property. — The Pareto type-II definition supplies the univariate density. Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} defines $f(X,D,U)=\mathbb E[Y\mid X,D,U]$, and the printed tail-index calculation separately defines a weighted-sum density. Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} inherit the conditional-mean definition through Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}; their additional definitions of $m_d^t(x,v)$ specify threshold-conditional normalized means and do not redefine $f$.
- reading of the symbol `g`: $g(x,d,u)$ is the population asymptotic normalized conditional-mean function, given by $g(x,d,u)=\lim_{t\to\infty}f(x,d,tu)/t^\alpha$ on its covariate, treatment, and direction domain. In Proposition~\ref{prop:id}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: The homogeneity inequality $\|f(x,d,tu)/t^\alpha-g(x,d,u)\|\leqslant e(t)$ holds for every $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every $t>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R$ and $u\in\mathbb R_+^d$ with $\\|u\\|_1=1$; $g$ is $L$-Lipschitz on this domain. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: The homogeneity inequality $\|f(x,d,tu)/t^\alpha-g(x,d,u)\|\leqslant e(t)$ holds for every $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every $t>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R$ and $u\in\mathbb R_+^d$ with $\\|u\\|_1=1$; $g$ is $L$-Lipschitz on this domain. — Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} and the existing scoped readings still determine the conditional-mean meaning.
