- reading of the symbol `e`: $e(t)$ is a deterministic, nonnegative uniform approximation-error bound in the asymptotic homogeneous property, allowing $e(t)=0$ and tending to zero as $t\rightarrow\infty$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:id}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: The bound $\|f(x,d,ru)/r^\alpha-g(x,d,u)\|\leq e(r)$ holds for both $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every $r>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R$ and $u\in\mathbb R_+^d$ with $\\|u\\|_1=1$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The bound $\|f(x,d,ru)/r^\alpha-g(x,d,u)\|\leq e(r)$ holds for both $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every $r>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R$ and $u\in\mathbb R_+^d$ with $\\|u\\|_1=1$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: The approximation-error envelope used in the bound is $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$, and $G_t=C+\bar e(t)$; no equality $\bar e(t)=e(t)$ is assumed. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: The approximation-error envelope used in the bound is $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$, and $G_t=C+\bar e(t)$; no equality $\bar e(t)=e(t)$ is assumed. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The approximation-error envelope used in the bound is $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$, evaluated at the selected threshold $t_n$; no equality $\bar e(t)=e(t)$ is assumed. — Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} explicitly define $\bar e(t)=\sup_{r\geq t}e(r)$ and use $\bar e(t_n)$ in their bounds. Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} assumes convergence to zero without monotonicity; the inherited positive-sphere homogeneity interpretation remains unchanged.
- reading of the symbol `c`: $c$ denotes a deterministic positive constant whose identity and additional restrictions are supplied by its local definition. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: In the overlap assumption, $c\in(0,1/2)$ bounds the estimated propensity uniformly by $c\leq\hat p(x)\leq1-c$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: In the regular-variation definition, $c>0$ is the mass multiplier in $c\nu_\beta\times S$, independently of the overlap constant; in the overlap assumption and the error bounds containing $1/c$, $c\in(0,1/2)$ satisfies $c\leq\hat p(x)\leq1-c$ for every $x\in\mathcal X$. The constants $c_1,c_2,c_\alpha$ are separate symbols. — Definition~\ref{def:mvarying} and Assumption~\ref{assump:overlap} define distinct local constants. The error bounds in Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} and Lemmas~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} and~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw} use the overlap constant in their factors $1/c$. Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} inherit both definitions through Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}; their displayed conclusions do not explicitly contain $1/c$. The printed tail-density calculation locally uses $c$ as a proportionality constant.
- reading of the symbol `p`: $p(x)=\mathbb P(D=1\mid X=x)$ is the population propensity function on $\mathcal X$, distinct from its fitted estimate $\hat p(x)$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: $0<p(X)<1$ almost surely; this does not impose a uniform positive lower bound on $p$ or $1-p$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $0<p(X)<1$ almost surely; this does not impose a uniform positive lower bound on $p$ or $1-p$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}: In the proof's rate derivation for $\beta\ne1$, $p=\min\{1,1/\beta\}$ is a fixed scalar exponent used in the choice of $k_0$ and the resulting convergence rate, rather than the propensity function. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: $0<p(X)<1$ almost surely; this does not impose a uniform positive lower bound on $p$ or $1-p$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:id}: $0<p(X)<1$ almost surely for the population propensity; this does not impose a uniform positive lower bound on $p$ or $1-p$. Its statement retains population propensity positivity, as does Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}.
- reading of the symbol `\hat{p}`: $\hat p$ is the sample-dependent estimator of the propensity function fitted using the first half $\mathcal D_1$ of the split sample by regression of $D$ on $X$, with $\hat p(x)\in[c,1-c]$ for every $x\in\mathcal X$; the fitted function is fixed conditional on the training split and all randomness used in fitting. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: $\hat p$ is measurable with respect to $\mathcal D_1$ and independent auxiliary data; its error inequality holds jointly with the regression and exponent inequalities with probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, uniformly almost surely over the covariate support, for each tolerance $\varepsilon$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $\hat p$ is measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data $\mathcal T$, and is the same fitted function for every input threshold. With probability at least $1-\delta/6$, jointly for every $t\in I_n=[\ell_n,u_n]$, its inequality $\|p(x)-\hat p(x)\|\leq R_p(n/2,\delta/6)$ holds together with the regression and exponent inequalities, simultaneously almost surely over the covariate and conditional direction support and both treatments. The additional estimation-rate condition is $R_p(n,\delta)=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-1/2})$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: $\hat p$ is measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data $\mathcal T$; for each fixed $t$ and $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, its inequality $\|p(X)-\hat p(X)\|\leq R_p(n,\varepsilon)$ holds jointly with the regression and exponent inequalities on one $\mathcal T$-measurable event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, simultaneously almost surely over the covariate support, the conditional direction support and both treatments. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: $\hat p$ is measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data $\mathcal T$; its error inequality holds jointly with the regression and exponent inequalities with probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, uniformly almost surely over the covariate support, for each tolerance $\varepsilon$. — Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} specify $\mathcal T$-measurability, the same fitted function for every threshold, and a joint nuisance event uniform over the deterministic threshold band.
- reading of the symbol `i`: $i$ is a locally bound integer index whose range is supplied by the expression in which it occurs. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: In $n_t=\sum_{i=1}^{n/2}I(\\|U_i\\|>t)$, $i\in\{1,\ldots,n/2\}$ indexes the training observations, indexed first after the random split. — Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} explicitly identifies the indices in $n_t$ as training observations indexed first after the random split; its corollaries and Lemmas~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} and~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw} inherit the training-split convention.

- reading of the symbol `D_i`: $D_i\in\{0,1\}$ is the observed-treatment coordinate of the $i$th sampled tuple $(X_i,D_i,Y_i,U_i)$, indexed by $i=1,\ldots,N$ in the model and $i=1,\ldots,n$ in the estimation notation. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: After the random equal split, $D_i$ is the treatment coordinate of a training observation for $1\leq i\leq n/2$ and of an evaluation observation for $n/2<i\leq n$. — The binding reading of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} indexes the training observations first after the random split; Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} inherit its assumptions.
- reading of the symbol `Y_i`: $Y_i$ is the observed-outcome coordinate of the $i$th sampled tuple $(X_i,D_i,Y_i,U_i)$, indexed by $i=1,\ldots,N$ in the model and $i=1,\ldots,n$ in the estimation notation. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: After the random equal split, $Y_i$ is the observed-outcome coordinate of a training observation for $1\leq i\leq n/2$ and of an evaluation observation for $n/2<i\leq n$. — The binding reading of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} indexes the training observations first after the random split; Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} inherit its assumptions.

- reading of the symbol `S`: As a spectral measure, $S$ is the population probability measure on $\mathbb{S}^+=\{u\in\mathbb{R}_+^d:\\|u\\|=1\}$ in the regular-variation limit, equivalently the limiting law of $U/\\|U\\|$ conditional on $\\|U\\|>t$ as $t\rightarrow\infty$, with the $\ell_1$ norm used after the notation convention. — Definition~\ref{def:mvarying} defines the measure, and the identification proof describes the limiting conditional radial-angular law. - reading of the symbol `b_n`: $b_n$ is a deterministic positive sequence on the index domain of its local definition. - reading of the symbol `\mathcal{A}`: $\mathcal{A}$ is the fixed population matrix class, with column indices $i\in\{1,\ldots,d_z\}$ and fixed constants $0<l<1<u$ and $\sigma>0$. — The matrix-class display in Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} defines a subset of the matrix space, rather than a matrix or a probability measure on matrices.
- reading of the symbol `M_k`: $M_k$ is a fixed population distribution class indexed by $k\in\{1,2,\ldots\}$, rather than by the sample size or a selected Hill order statistic. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: $M_k=\{\mathcal L(U):U=AZ,\ A\in\mathcal A,\ \mathcal L(Z)\in\tilde M_k\}$; the class concerns the noise law, with $m=d_z$ in the component density. — Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} defines $M=\bigcup_{k=1}^{\infty}M_k$. Lemmas~\ref{lemma:mu_rate} and~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} specify the noise law $\mathcal L(U)$, and Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} and Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw} incorporate those conditions.
- reading of the symbol `M`: $M$ denotes the object specified by its local definition, either a class of probability laws or an observed exceedance count. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}: $M=\bigcup_{k=1}^{\infty}M_k$ is a class of noise laws, with $M_k=\{\mathcal L(U):U=AZ,\ A\in\mathcal A,\ \mathcal L(Z)\in\tilde M_k\}$ and $m=d_z$ in the component density. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: In the score bound and the zero-count convention, $M=\|\mathcal I\|$ is the realized second-half evaluation exceedance count. The tail-model assumption separately concerns the noise law $\mathcal L(U)$, with $m=d_z$ in its component density. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: In the sampling terms, count bound and zero-count convention, $M=\|\mathcal I\|$ is the realized second-half evaluation exceedance count. In the inherited tail-model definition, $M=\bigcup_{k=1}^{\infty}M_k$ separately denotes the class of noise laws, with component dimension $m=d_z$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: In the score bound and zero-count convention, $M=\|\mathcal I\|$ is the realized second-half evaluation exceedance count. The tail-model assumption separately concerns the noise law $\mathcal L(U)$, with $m=d_z$ in its component density. — Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} now explicitly puts $M=\|\mathcal I\|$ and uses it in the sampling terms and zero-count convention, while retaining Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary}.
- reading of the symbol `s`: In the tail model, $s\in(0,1/2)$ is a fixed model constant governing both the density error $\xi k^{-s}$ and the exact-tail threshold $\zeta k^{(1-2s)/\beta}$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: In $m_d^t(x,s)$, $S_U=s$, $s\mapsto\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,s)-g(X,0,s)]$, and $\int\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,s)-g(X,0,s)]\,S(\tmop{ds})$, $s\in\mathbb S^+=\{u\in\mathbb R_+^d:\\|u\\|_1=1\}$ is an angular argument or bound integration variable. In $t^{-\beta s/(1-2s)}$, $s$ retains its scalar tail-model meaning. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $s$ is the scalar tail-model constant in the threshold exponent and the two item restrictions: the first item assumes $0<s<1/(2+\max\{1,\beta\})$, and the second assumes $1/(2+\max\{1,\beta\})\leqslant s<1/2$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: In the proof's $m_d^t(x,s)$ and $s\mapsto\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,s)-g(X,0,s)]$, $s\in\mathbb S^+=\{u\in\mathbb R_+^d:\\|u\\|_1=1\}$ is an angular argument, distinct from the scalar tail-model constant. In Proposition~\ref{prop:id}: In the proof's $G(s)=\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,s)-g(X,0,s)]$, $h_t(r,s)=G(s)r^\alpha$, and $\int G(s)\,S(\tmop{ds})$, $s\in\mathbb S^+=\{u\in\mathbb R_+^d:\\|u\\|_1=1\}$ is an angular argument or bound integration variable. — Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} defines the scalar tail-model constant. Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} print the two scalar regimes and threshold rules. The proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:id} uses $s$ in $G(s)$, $h_t(r,s)$, and spectral integration; the existing lemma scopes describe their angular arguments.
- reading of the symbol `\sigma`: $\sigma>0$ is a fixed model constant imposing $JA=\sqrt{\det(A^\mathsf{T}A)}\geq\sigma$ on matrices in $\mathcal{A}$. — Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} defines $JA$, imposes its lower bound, and explicitly lists $\sigma$ among the constants.
- reading of the symbol `\xi`: $\xi\in(0,1)$ is a fixed model constant bounding the relative density approximation error by $\xi k^{-s}$. — Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} gives the density inequality and explicitly calls $\xi$ a constant.
- reading of the symbol `\zeta`: $\zeta>0$ is a fixed constant in the tail-model class: for each class index $k$, the exact proportional-density region is $\\|z\\|_1>\zeta k^{(1-2s)/\beta}$. — Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} defines the region in $\tilde M_k$ and explicitly lists $\zeta>0$ among its constants.
- reading of the symbol `n`: $n$ is the size of the full input dataset $\mathcal{D}=\{(X_i,D_i,Y_i,U_i)\}_{i=1}^n$, before its split into two equal halves; when used as the argument of an estimation-error function, it denotes that function's sample-size argument. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}: $n$ is the integer size of the full input sample whose radii are ordered, with $n\geq2$ for the definition of $H_n(k)$; for each fixed $\delta\in(0,1)$ there is a deterministic $n_0$ such that the deviation bound holds with probability at least $1-\delta$ under the sample-size-$n$ law for every integer $n\geq n_0$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The full-input sample size $n$ is a positive even integer, with $\|\mathcal D_1\|=\|\mathcal D_2\|=n/2$; this restriction does not restrict a generic sample-size argument of an estimation-error function to even integers. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: For each fixed $\delta\in(0,1/2)$, the probability guarantee holds separately under the sample-size-$n$ law for every sufficiently large even $n$. — Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} quantify separately over every sufficiently large even $n$ for each fixed $\delta\in(0,1/2)$. The equal-split convention remains in force.
- reading of the symbol `\delta`: $\delta$ is a fixed confidence tolerance in $(0,1)$, used in probability guarantees of the form $1-\delta$ and in the adaptive Hill tuning rule. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}: For each fixed $\delta\in(0,1)$ there is a deterministic $n_0$ such that, for every integer $n\geq n_0$, the bound has probability at least $1-\delta$ under the law of the sample of size $n$; $n_0$ and $C_\delta$ may depend on $\delta$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: $\delta\in(0,1/2)$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: For each fixed $\delta\in(0,1/2)$, the probability guarantee holds separately under the sample-size-$n$ law for every sufficiently large even $n$; the sample-size requirement may depend on $\delta$. The full-input Hill selection uses tolerance $\delta/6$. — Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} fixes $\delta\in(0,1/2)$. Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} give their guarantees separately for each fixed $\delta\in(0,1/2)$ and every sufficiently large even $n$, and run Hill selection at tolerance $\delta/6$.
- reading of the symbol `n_t`: $n_t$ is the observed number of threshold exceedances in the designated half of the sample, with the half and strictness of the threshold determined by its local definition. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $n_t=\sum_{i=1}^{n/2}I(\\|U_i\\|>t)$ is the realized training-split exceedance count for positive even $n$, taking values in $\{0,\ldots,n/2\}$; it is distinct from the evaluation count $\|\mathcal I\|$ and from its expectation $n\mathbb P(\\|U\\|>t)/2$. — Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} defines the first-half count with strict exceedances; Lemmas~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} and~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw} use its nuisance conditions. Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} use the same count in their simultaneous threshold-dependent regression bounds.
- reading of the symbol `R_p`: $R_p(n,\delta)$ is a deterministic positive error-bound function, monotonically decreasing in its sample-size argument, used in the high-probability inequality $\|p(X)-\hat p(X)\|\leqslant R_p(n,\delta)$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $R_p(n,\varepsilon)=\Theta(\log(1/\varepsilon)n^{-1/2})$. On one joint nuisance event of probability at least $1-\delta/6$, the three nuisance inequalities hold simultaneously for every $t\in I_n$, almost surely over the covariate and conditional direction support and both treatments; the propensity inequality is $\|p(x)-\hat p(x)\|\leqslant R_p(n/2,\delta/6)$, using the same fitted propensity for every input threshold. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: For each fixed $t$ and tolerance $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, the three nuisance inequalities hold on one $\mathcal T$-measurable event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, simultaneously almost surely over the covariate support, conditional direction support and both treatments. The displayed propensity inequality uses $R_p(n,\varepsilon)$; the training-split propensity bound entering the final error bounds uses $R_p(n/2,\varepsilon)$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: For each tolerance $\varepsilon$, the nuisance inequalities hold jointly with probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, uniformly almost surely over the covariate and direction support and both treatments. The training-split propensity bound used in the proof is $\|p(x)-\hat p(x)\|\leqslant R_p(n/2,\varepsilon)$. — Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} explicitly require the three nuisance bounds jointly for every $t\in I_n$, with probability at least $1-\delta/6$, and use $R_p(n/2,\delta/6)$ for the fitted training-split propensity.
- reading of the symbol `R_g`: $R_g(q,\delta)$ is a nonnegative regression-error bound function, nonincreasing in its sample-size argument $q$, for estimating the conditional mean of $Y/\\|U\\|^\alpha$ given $(X,D,U/\\|U\\|)$ and $\\|U\\|>t$; evaluation at $q=n_t$ uses the observed tail count. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $R_g(n,\delta)=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-1/2})$. On one joint nuisance event of probability at least $1-\delta/6$, simultaneously for every $t\in I_n$, $\|m_d^t(x,v)-\hat g(x,d,v)\|\leqslant R_g(n_t,\delta/6)$ almost surely over the covariate and conditional direction support and both treatments, where $m_d^t(x,v)=\mathbb E[Y/\\|U\\|^\alpha\mid X=x,D=d,U/\\|U\\|=v,\\|U\\|>t]$ and $n_t=\sum_{i=1}^{n/2}I(\\|U_i\\|>t)$ is the training-split exceedance count. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: For each tolerance $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, the bound $R_g(n_t,\varepsilon)$ applies uniformly almost surely over the covariate and direction support and both treatments, on the joint nuisance event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$; its sample-size argument $n_t$ is the training-split exceedance count, distinct from $M=\|\mathcal I\|$. For positive even $n$, its domain includes every $q\in\{0,1,\ldots,n/2\}$, including $q=0$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: For each fixed $t$ and tolerance $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, the regression-error bound $R_g(n_t,\varepsilon)$ holds together with the propensity and exponent inequalities on one $\mathcal T$-measurable event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, simultaneously almost surely over the covariate support, conditional direction support, and both treatments; $n_t=\sum_{i=1}^{n/2}I(\\|U_i\\|>t)$ is the training-split exceedance count, with training observations indexed first after the random split. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: For each tolerance $\varepsilon$, $R_g(n_t,\varepsilon)$ applies uniformly almost surely over the covariate and direction support and both treatments on the joint nuisance event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$; $n_t$ is the training-split exceedance count, distinct from $M=\|\mathcal I\|$. Its sample-size domain includes every possible training exceedance count $q\in\{0,1,\ldots,n/2\}$, including $q=0$. — Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} specify a joint nuisance event of probability at least $1-\delta/6$ for every threshold in the deterministic band $I_n$, with the regression bound indexed by the training exceedance count.
- reading of the symbol `R_\alpha`: $R_\alpha(n,\delta)$ is a deterministic positive error-bound function, monotonically decreasing in its sample-size argument, used in the high-probability inequality $\|\hat\alpha_n-\alpha\|\leqslant R_\alpha(n,\delta)$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $R_\alpha(n,\delta)=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-c_\alpha})$ for some $c_\alpha>0$. — Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} gives the defining error inequality and states monotonicity in sample size. Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} additionally prescribe its rate.
- reading of the symbol `\widehat{\theta}_{n,t}^{\text{DR}}`: $\widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\text{DR}}$ is the sample estimator formed by multiplying the split-sample normalized DR first-factor estimate by $\hat\mu_n=(1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n)^{-1}$, wherever that denominator and the first-factor estimate are defined. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}: The estimator is evaluated at $t_n=\Theta(n^{(1-2s)\hat\gamma_n})$ in the first item and $t_n=\Theta(n^{\hat\gamma_n/(1+2\min\{1,\hat\gamma_n\})})$ in the second item, with deterministic positive order constants independent of $n$ and the sample. The fitted $\hat p$, $\hat g$, and $\hat\alpha_n$ are measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data $\mathcal T$ and are the same functions and exponent for every input threshold. The Hill estimate $\hat\gamma_n$ uses all $n$ input radii at tolerance $\delta/6$. Use $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{DR}}=0$ when there are no evaluation exceedances and $\hat\mu_n=0$ when $1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n=0$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: The threshold $t$ is deterministic, the fitted propensity, regression, and exponent are measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data $\mathcal T$, and $\hat\gamma_n=\widetilde\gamma_n$ is computed from all $n$ input radii at tolerance $\delta/6$; use $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{DR}}=0$ when $M=0$ and $\hat\mu_n=0$ when $1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n=0$. — Corollary~\ref{cor:rate} specifies fitted nuisances shared across thresholds, full-input Hill selection at tolerance $\delta/6$, and zero first-factor estimates for empty evaluation tails; it inherits the reciprocal convention of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}.
- reading of the symbol `\widehat{\theta}_{n,t}^{\text{IPW}}`: $\widehat\theta_{n,t}^{\text{IPW}}$ is the split-sample product $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}\hat\mu_n$, using $\hat p$ fitted on $\mathcal D_1$, the supplied exponent $\hat\alpha_n$, and the normalized IPW score averaged over evaluation exceedances in $\mathcal D_2$, with $\hat\mu_n=(1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n)^{-1}$ wherever the average and reciprocal are defined. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The estimator is evaluated at $t_n=\Theta(n^{(1-2s)\hat\gamma_n})$ in the first item and $t_n=\Theta(n^{\hat\gamma_n/(1+2\min\{1,\hat\gamma_n\})})$ in the second item, with deterministic positive order constants independent of $n$ and the sample. The fitted $\hat p$, $\hat g$, and $\hat\alpha_n$ are measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data $\mathcal T$ and are the same functions and exponent for every input threshold. The Hill estimate $\hat\gamma_n$ uses all $n$ input radii at tolerance $\delta/6$. Use $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}=0$ when there are no evaluation exceedances and $\hat\mu_n=0$ when $1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n=0$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: The threshold $t$ is deterministic, the fitted propensity, regression, and exponent are measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data $\mathcal T$, and $\hat\gamma_n=\widetilde\gamma_n$ is computed from all $n$ input radii at tolerance $\delta/6$; use $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}=0$ when $M=0$ and $\hat\mu_n=0$ when $1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n=0$. — Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} specifies fitted nuisances shared across thresholds, full-input Hill selection at tolerance $\delta/6$, and zero first-factor estimates for empty evaluation tails; it inherits the reciprocal convention of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}.
- reading of the symbol `\hat{\eta}_{n,t}^{\tmop{DR}}`: $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\tmop{DR}}$ is the average normalized doubly robust score over exceedances in the second half $\mathcal{D}_2$, using $\hat p$ and $\hat g$ fitted on the first half $\mathcal{D}_1$ and the supplied exponent $\hat\alpha_n$, wherever $\|\mathcal{I}\|>0$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: Set $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\tmop{DR}}=0$ when $M=\|\mathcal I\|=0$; conditional on $\mathcal D_1$ and the independent auxiliary data, the fitted $\hat p$, $\hat g$, and $\hat\alpha_n$ are fixed, and for $M>0$ the estimator averages the displayed score $\psi$ over the evaluation exceedances. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}: The estimator is evaluated at $t_n=\Theta(n^{(1-2s)\hat\gamma_n})$ in the first item and $t_n=\Theta(n^{\hat\gamma_n/(1+2\min\{1,\hat\gamma_n\})})$ in the second item, with deterministic positive order constants independent of $n$ and the sample. Set $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\tmop{DR}}=0$ when there are no evaluation exceedances. The fitted $\hat p$, $\hat g$, and $\hat\alpha_n$ are $\mathcal T$-measurable, where $\mathcal T$ consists of the training split and independent auxiliary data, and are the same functions and exponent for every input threshold; they are held fixed in the score moments conditional on $\mathcal T$ and $\\|U\\|>t$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: Set $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\tmop{DR}}=0$ when $M=\|\mathcal I\|=0$; conditional on the training split and independent auxiliary data $\mathcal T$, the fitted $\hat p$, $\hat g$, and $\hat\alpha_n$ are fixed, and for $M>0$ the estimator averages $\psi^{\mathrm{DR}}$ over the evaluation exceedances. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: Set $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\tmop{DR}}=0$ when $M=\|\mathcal I\|=0$; conditional on the training split and independent auxiliary data $\mathcal T$, the fitted $\hat p$, $\hat g$, and $\hat\alpha_n$ are fixed, and for $M>0$ the estimator averages the normalized doubly robust score over the evaluation exceedances. — Corollary~\ref{cor:rate} specifies training-measurable fitted functions and exponent shared across thresholds, zero for an empty evaluation tail, and deterministic constants in the adaptive threshold order.
- reading of the symbol `\hat{\eta}_{n,t}^{\text{IPW}}`: $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}$ is the sample average over the threshold exceedances in the second half $\mathcal{D}_2$ of the split sample, using $\hat p$ fitted on the first half $\mathcal{D}_1$ and the supplied exponent $\hat\alpha_n$, wherever $\|\mathcal{I}\|>0$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The estimator is evaluated at $t_n=\Theta(n^{(1-2s)\hat\gamma_n})$ in the first item and $t_n=\Theta(n^{\hat\gamma_n/(1+2\min\{1,\hat\gamma_n\})})$ in the second item, with deterministic positive order constants independent of $n$ and the sample. Set $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}=0$ when there are no evaluation exceedances. The fitted propensity and exponent are $\mathcal T$-measurable, where $\mathcal T$ consists of the training split and independent auxiliary data, and are the same function and exponent for every input threshold; they are held fixed in the score moments conditional on $\mathcal T$ and $\\|U\\|>t$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}=0$ when $M=\|\mathcal I\|=0$. The fitted propensity and exponent are measurable with respect to $\mathcal T$, the training split together with independent auxiliary data, and are held fixed in the score moments conditional on $\mathcal T$ and $\\|U\\|>t$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}=0$ when $M=\|\mathcal I\|=0$. The fitted propensity and exponent are measurable with respect to $\mathcal T$, the training split together with independent auxiliary data, and are held fixed in the score moments conditional on $\mathcal T$ and $\\|U\\|>t$; the threshold $t$ is deterministic. — Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} specifies training-measurable fitted functions and exponent shared across thresholds, zero for an empty evaluation tail, and deterministic constants in the adaptive threshold order.
- reading of the symbol `\eta`: $\eta$ is a population quantity whose scalar or functional meaning is determined by its local use. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: $\eta=\int\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,s)-g(X,0,s)]\,S(\mathrm{d}s)$, where $S$ is the limiting spectral probability measure. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: $\eta=\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathbb E[g(X,1,U/\\|U\\|)-g(X,0,U/\\|U\\|)\mid\\|U\\|>t]$ is the scalar population first-factor target. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: $\eta=\int\mathbb E_X[g(X,1,s)-g(X,0,s)]\,S(\tmop{ds})$, where $S$ is the limiting spectral probability measure. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}: $\eta(v)=vQ'(v)/Q(v)-\gamma=\bar F_R(Q(v))/(Q(v)f_R(Q(v)))-1/\beta$ is the deterministic von Mises function on the differentiable tail of $Q(v)=F_R^{-1}(1-1/v)$, where $R=\\|U\\|_1$, $F_R$ is its population distribution function, $\bar F_R$ its survival function, $f_R$ its density, and $\gamma=1/\beta$. — The proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate} in discovery/core.json defines $Q(v)=F_R^{-1}(1-1/v)$ and displays the von Mises function in terms of $Q$, the radial survival function, and the radial density.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal{D}`: $\mathcal D=\{(X_i,D_i,Y_i,U_i)\}_{i=1}^n$ denotes the indexed input dataset of $n$ observations. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: The sample size $n$ is a positive even integer, and the equal split satisfies $\|\mathcal D_1\|=\|\mathcal D_2\|=n/2$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The input size $n$ is a positive even integer; after the random equal split, indices $1,\ldots,n/2$ designate the training observations and indices $n/2+1,\ldots,n$ designate the evaluation observations. — Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} specifies the indexed input and equal split. Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} specifies training-first indexing after that split, inherited by Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal{D}_1`: $\mathcal D_1$ is the first half of the random equal split of the indexed dataset $\mathcal D$, containing $n/2$ observations and supplying the observations for nuisance regression. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: The sample size $n$ is a positive even integer, and $\|\mathcal D_1\|=\|\mathcal D_2\|=n/2$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The input size $n$ is a positive even integer, and after the random split the training observations are indexed first, so $\mathcal D_1=\{(X_i,D_i,Y_i,U_i)\}_{i=1}^{n/2}$ and $n_t=\sum_{i=1}^{n/2}I(\\|U_i\\|>t)$ counts its exceedances. — The binding reading of Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} specifies that the training observations are indexed first after the random split; Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} inherit its assumptions. Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} uses equal halves.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal{D}_2`: $\mathcal D_2$ is the second equal part of the split dataset, used for evaluation, with the equal split defined for positive even sample sizes $n$ and $\|\mathcal D_1\|=\|\mathcal D_2\|=n/2$. — Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} defines the equal split and uses its second part for evaluation; the binding reading of Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate} explicitly specifies positive even $n$ and both split cardinalities.
- reading of the symbol `\hat{\alpha}_n`: $\hat{\alpha}_n\in\mathbb{R}$ is the supplied approximate value of the outcome scaling index $\alpha$, obtainable from prior knowledge or heuristics. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: $\hat{\alpha}_n$ is measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data; for each tolerance $\varepsilon$, its bound $\|\hat{\alpha}_n-\alpha\|\leq R_\alpha(n,\varepsilon)$ holds jointly with the other nuisance inequalities with probability at least $1-\varepsilon$. The bound uses $a=R_\alpha(n,\delta/4)\leq a_0$ for fixed $a_0\in(0,\beta)$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: $\hat\alpha_n$ is measurable with respect to $\mathcal T$, the training split and independent auxiliary data. For every fixed $t$ and tolerance $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, $\|\hat\alpha_n-\alpha\|\leq R_\alpha(n,\varepsilon)$ holds on one $\mathcal T$-measurable event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, jointly with the other nuisance inequalities, simultaneously almost surely over the covariate support, conditional direction support and both treatments. The bound uses $a=R_\alpha(n,\delta/12)\leq a_0$ for fixed $a_0\in(0,\beta)$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: $\hat{\alpha}_n$ is measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data; for every tolerance $\varepsilon$, its inequality $\|\hat{\alpha}_n-\alpha\|\leq R_\alpha(n,\varepsilon)$ holds jointly with the other nuisance inequalities with probability at least $1-\varepsilon$. The exponent-error quantity is $a=R_\alpha(n,\delta/4)\leq a_0$, where $a_0\in(0,\beta)$ is fixed. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $\hat\alpha_n$ is $\mathcal T$-measurable, where $\mathcal T$ consists of the training split and independent auxiliary data, and is the same exponent for every input threshold. Its rate satisfies $R_\alpha(n,\delta)=\Theta(\log(1/\delta)n^{-c_\alpha})$ for some fixed $c_\alpha>0$. With probability at least $1-\delta/6$, the inequality $\|\hat\alpha_n-\alpha\|\leq R_\alpha(n,\delta/6)$ holds jointly with the propensity and regression inequalities for every $t\in I_n=[(c_-/2)n^{\kappa(\gamma)},2c_+n^{\kappa(\gamma)}]$, simultaneously almost surely over the covariate and conditional direction support and both treatments. — Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} require $\mathcal T$-measurability, the same exponent for every input threshold, and a joint nuisance event of probability at least $1-\delta/6$ uniformly over their deterministic threshold band.
- reading of the symbol `\hat{g}`: $\hat g(x,d,s)$ is the fitted regression of $Y/\\|U\\|^{\hat\alpha_n}$ on $(X,D,U/\\|U\\|)$ using $\mathcal D_1$, with $s\in\mathbb S^+$ for the paper's $\ell_1$ norm. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: $\hat g$ is measurable with respect to $\mathcal D_1$ and independent auxiliary data and is held fixed in the conditional evaluation-score expectations; its nuisance inequality holds uniformly almost surely over the covariate and direction support and both treatments on the stipulated joint nuisance events. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: For each fixed $t$ and tolerance $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, $\hat g$ is measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data, denoted together by $\mathcal T$, and is held fixed in the conditional evaluation-score expectations; its nuisance comparison is with $\mathbb E[Y/\\|U\\|^{\alpha}\mid X,D,U/\\|U\\|,\\|U\\|>t]$, with bound $R_g(n_t,\varepsilon)$, although the fitted response uses $\hat\alpha_n$. This inequality holds simultaneously almost surely over the covariate support, conditional direction support and both treatments on one $\mathcal T$-measurable joint nuisance event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: $\hat g$ is measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data, denoted together by $\mathcal T$; its nuisance inequality holds uniformly almost surely over the covariate and direction support and both treatments on the stipulated joint nuisance events. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $\hat g$ is measurable with respect to the training split and independent auxiliary data, denoted together by $\mathcal T$, and is the same fitted function for every input threshold; with probability at least $1-\delta/6$, jointly for every $t\in I_n$, $\|m_d^t(x,v)-\hat g(x,d,v)\|\leq R_g(n_t,\delta/6)$ simultaneously almost surely over the covariate and conditional direction support and both treatments, where $m_d^t(x,v)=\mathbb E[Y/\\|U\\|^\alpha\mid X=x,D=d,U/\\|U\\|=v,\\|U\\|>t]$ and $n_t$ is the training exceedance count. — Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} require the same $\mathcal T$-measurable fitted functions for every input threshold and specify a joint nuisance event over every $t\in I_n$ with probability at least $1-\delta/6$.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal{I}`: $\mathcal I=\{i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}: (X_i,D_i,Y_i,U_i)\in\mathcal D_2,\ \\|U_i\\|>t\}$ is the evaluation exceedance index set at the threshold supplied to the estimator. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: At the selected threshold $t_n$, $\mathcal I=\{i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}:(X_i,D_i,Y_i,U_i)\in\mathcal D_2,\ \\|U_i\\|>t_n\}$. — Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} defines the evaluation exceedance set. Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} substitute their selected threshold into the estimator but do not introduce $M_{t_n}$ or specify the previously recorded full-sample threshold construction.
- reading of the symbol `\hat{\gamma}_n`: $\hat\gamma_n$ denotes a sample EVI estimate computed from ordered $\ell_1$ radii; the displayed definition in Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} is $\hat\gamma_n=k^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^k\log(\\|U_{(j)}\\|/\\|U_{(j+1)}\\|)$ on the evaluation exceedances, with the displayed adaptive choice of $k$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: The full-input convention is $\hat\gamma_n=\widetilde\gamma_n=H_n(\widetilde k_n)$ at tolerance $\delta/6$. Here $R_{[1]}\geq\cdots\geq R_{[n]}$ order all input radii, $H_n(k)=k^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^k\log(R_{[j]}/R_{[k+1]})$ for $1\leq k\leq n-1$, and $\widetilde k_n$ is the maximum $k\in\{L_n,\ldots,n-1\}$ satisfying $\|H_n(i)-H_n(k)\|\leq H_n(i)\rho_n(\delta/6)/\sqrt i$ for every $i\in\{L_n,\ldots,k\}$, using the displayed tuning definitions for $L_n$ and $\rho_n$. — Equation~\ref{eq:mu} prints the evaluation-exceedance formula with adjacent order statistics. Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} instead explicitly identifies its full-input output with $\widetilde\gamma_n$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}; the two corollaries and first-factor lemmas inherit the theorem's assumptions.
- reading of the symbol `t_n`: $t_n$ denotes the selected threshold sequence, depending on $\hat\gamma_n$, with $t_n=\Theta(n^{(1-2s)\hat\gamma_n})$ for $s\in(0,1/(2+\max\{1,\beta\}))$ and $t_n=\Theta(n^{\hat\gamma_n/(1+2\min\{1,\hat\gamma_n\})})$ for $s\in[1/(2+\max\{1,\beta\}),1/2)$; the $t$ in the corresponding estimator denotes this selected threshold. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The threshold order means $c_-n^{\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)}\leq t_n\leq c_+n^{\kappa(\hat\gamma_n)}$, with deterministic constants $0<c_-\leq c_+<\infty$ independent of $n$ and the observed sample; $\kappa(v)=(1-2s)v$ in the first regime and $\kappa(v)=v/(1+2\min\{1,v\})$ in the second. The estimate $\hat\gamma_n$ is obtained by full-input Hill selection at tolerance $\delta/6$. — Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} explicitly denote both selected thresholds by $t_n$ and specify deterministic constants $c_-$ and $c_+$ in their threshold orders.
- reading of the symbol `S_i`: $S_i=U_i/\\|U_i\\|_1$ for $i\in\mathcal{I}$ is the observed direction at the finite threshold $t$, taking values in $\mathbb{S}^+$. — Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} defines $S_i=U_i/\\|U_i\\|$ for $i\in\mathcal I$ and uses it in Equation~\ref{eq:eta_dr}. The notation paragraph makes its support the positive $\ell_1$ unit sphere; the listed results inherit this algorithmic definition.
- reading of the symbol `l_n`: $l_n$ is the deterministic integer lower endpoint of the adaptive Hill candidate range, with $1\leq l_n\leq n$ wherever that range is used. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The algorithm's lower candidate index is $l_n=\min\{n-1,\lceil100\log n\rceil\}=L_n$ for $n\geq2$, and its candidate range is $\{l_n,\ldots,n-1\}$. — Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} in discovery/core.json explicitly calibrates $l_n$; the experimental paragraph at flat.tex line 1091 separately chooses $l_n=30$.
- reading of the symbol `k`: $k$ is context-dependent: in adaptive Hill estimation it is a locally bound integer candidate or the data-dependent selected integer, in $M=\bigcup_{k=1}^{\infty}M_k$ it is a locally bound positive integer model-class index, and in an angular logarithmic factor it is a fixed nonnegative real exponent distinct from both indices. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}: $k$ in $H_n(k)$ is a candidate integer with $1\leq k\leq n-1$. The selected index is $\widetilde k_n=\max\{k\in\{L_n,\ldots,n-1\}:\|H_n(i)-H_n(k)\|\leq H_n(i)\rho_n(\delta)/\sqrt i\ \text{for every }i\in\{L_n,\ldots,k\}\}$, where $L_n=\min\{n-1,\lceil100\log n\rceil\}$; $H_n$ uses all $n$ input radii. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: The selected radial index is $\widetilde k_n=\max\{k\in\{L_n,\ldots,n-1\}:\|H_n(i)-H_n(k)\|\leq H_n(i)\rho_n(\delta/6)/\sqrt i\ \text{for every }i\in\{L_n,\ldots,k\}\}$, where $L_n=\min\{n-1,\lceil100\log n\rceil\}$ and $H_n$ uses all $n$ input radii. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: The radial step uses all $n$ input radii and selects $k=\max\{q\in\{l_n,\ldots,n-1\}:\|\hat\gamma(i)-\hat\gamma(q)\|\leq\hat\gamma(i)r_n(\delta)/\sqrt i\ \text{for every }i\in\{l_n,\ldots,q\}\}$, with $l_n=\min\{n-1,\lceil100\log n\rceil\}$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The selected radial index is $\widetilde k_n=\max\{k\in\{L_n,\ldots,n-1\}:\|H_n(i)-H_n(k)\|\leq H_n(i)\rho_n(\delta/6)/\sqrt i\ \text{for every }i\in\{L_n,\ldots,k\}\}$, where $L_n=\min\{n-1,\lceil100\log n\rceil\}$ and $H_n$ uses all $n$ input radii. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}, Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: In the angular bound $W_1(\mathcal L_{U/\\|U\\|}^t,\mathcal L_{U/\\|U\\|})\leq C_W(\log t)^k t^{-\min\{1,\beta\}}+C_Wt^{-\beta s/(1-2s)}$, $k\geq0$ is a fixed exponent supplied by the angular assumption, independent of the Hill selection; the same exponent appears in the corresponding factor $(\log n)^k$. — Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} in discovery/core.json defines the Hill selection; Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} defines the model-class union. Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, and Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} specify the angular exponent $k\geq0$; Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw} inherits that angular bound.
- reading of the symbol `r_n`: $r_n$ is a deterministic positive tolerance function of the confidence argument $\delta$, indexed by the integer sample size $n$, rather than an estimated quantity. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: For a confidence argument $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, the algorithm uses $J_n=1+\lceil\log n/\log2\rceil$, $q_n(\varepsilon)=\log(4J_n/\varepsilon)$ and $r_n(\varepsilon)=16\{2\sqrt{q_n(\varepsilon)}+q_n(\varepsilon)/\sqrt{l_n}\}=\rho_n(\varepsilon)$, where $l_n=L_n=\min\{n-1,\lceil100\log n\rceil\}$. — Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} in discovery/core.json defines $r_n(\delta)=16c_n(\delta)$ with the same calibration used for $\rho_n(\delta)$ in Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}.
- reading of the symbol `\hat{\gamma}_i`: $\hat\gamma_i$ is the common-denominator Hill statistic computed from all $n$ input radii ordered as $\\|U_{(1)}\\|\geq\cdots\geq\\|U_{(n)}\\|$, for integers $1\leq i\leq n-1$ with positive reference radius $\\|U_{(i+1)}\\|$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: The candidate indexed Hill statistics in the radial selection are $H_n(i)=i^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^i\log(R_{[j]}/R_{[i+1]})$, computed from all $n$ input radii, with $1\leq i\leq n-1$; the selection compares candidates $i\in\{L_n,\ldots,k\}$ for $k\in\{L_n,\ldots,n-1\}$ at tolerance $\delta/6$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The candidate indexed Hill statistics are $H_n(i)=i^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^i\log(R_{[j]}/R_{[i+1]})$, computed from all $n$ input radii, with $1\leq i\leq n-1$; the selection compares candidates $i\in\{L_n,\ldots,k\}$ for $k\in\{L_n,\ldots,n-1\}$ at tolerance $\delta/6$. — The current construction of Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} in discovery/core.json explicitly orders all $n$ noise observations and defines the common-denominator statistic for $1\leq i\leq n-1$.
- reading of the symbol `\hat{\mu}_n`: $\hat{\mu}_n=(1-\hat{\alpha}_n\hat{\gamma}_n)^{-1}$ is the sample plug-in estimate of the limiting radial moment $(1-\alpha\gamma)^{-1}$, defined when $1-\hat{\alpha}_n\hat{\gamma}_n\ne0$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: $\hat\mu_n=0$ when $1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n=0$, and $\hat\mu_n=(1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n)^{-1}$ otherwise. — Theorem~\ref{thm:rate} explicitly uses $\hat\mu_n=0$ when $\|1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n\|=0$; Corollaries~\ref{cor:rate} and~\ref{cor:rate_ipw} inherit its estimator convention.
- reading of the symbol `j`: In radial order-statistic notation, $j$ is a locally bound integer rank or summation index, ranging over the ranks specified by its display. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:mu_rate}: In $H_n(k)=k^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^k\log(R_{[j]}/R_{[k+1]})$, $j$ ranges over $\{1,\ldots,k\}$, with $1\leq k\leq n-1$; the denominator is the common full-input radius $R_{[k+1]}$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: In the full-input Hill output, $j$ ranges over $\{1,\ldots,\widetilde k_n\}$ in $H_n(\widetilde k_n)=\widetilde k_n^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde k_n}\log(R_{[j]}/R_{[\widetilde k_n+1]})$; the denominator is common to every summand. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: In $\hat\gamma(k)=k^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^k\log(\\|U_{(j)}\\|/\\|U_{(k+1)}\\|)$, $j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}$ with $1\leq k\leq n-1$, and the denominator is common to every summand. In the equivalent expression $k^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^k j\log(\\|U_{(j)}\\|/\\|U_{(j+1)}\\|)$, each adjacent spacing has weight $j$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: In the full-input Hill output, $j$ ranges over $\{1,\ldots,\widetilde k_n\}$ in $H_n(\widetilde k_n)=\widetilde k_n^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde k_n}\log(R_{[j]}/R_{[\widetilde k_n+1]})$; the denominator is common to every summand. — Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} in discovery/core.json defines $\hat\gamma(k)$ using a common rank-$k+1$ denominator and also displays its equivalent weighted adjacent-spacing sum.
- reading of the symbol `U_{(j)}`: $U_{(j)}$ denotes a noise observation ranked by decreasing $\ell_1$ norm within the collection used by the radial step; only its norm is used, and it is defined only for ranks present in that collection. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: The radial output uses all $n$ input radii $R_i=\\|U_i\\|_1$, ordered as $R_{[1]}\geq\cdots\geq R_{[n]}$, and is $\hat\gamma_n=\widetilde\gamma_n$. Thus the radial ranks refer to the full input rather than to $\{U_i:i\in\mathcal I\}$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: $U_{(j)}$ is ranked within all $n$ input noise observations, with $\\|U_{(1)}\\|\geq\cdots\geq\\|U_{(n)}\\|$; it is defined for $j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$. The radial step uses this full-input collection. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The radial output uses all $n$ input radii $R_i=\\|U_i\\|_1$, ordered as $R_{[1]}\geq\cdots\geq R_{[n]}$, with the full-input Hill selection run at tolerance $\delta/6$. Thus the radial ranks refer to the full input rather than to $\{U_i:i\in\mathcal I\}$. — Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} in discovery/core.json orders all $n$ input noise observations by decreasing $\ell_1$ norm.
- reading of the symbol `\widehat{\theta}_{n,t}^{\mathrm{estimator}}`: $\widehat{\theta}_{n,t}^{\mathrm{estimator}}$ denotes the sample estimator computed from the supplied inputs and split dataset, with nuisance functions fitted on $\mathcal D_1$, the score average computed using the exceedances in $\mathcal D_2$, and the radial factor computed using all $n$ input radii, with $\hat\mu_n=0$ when $1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n=0$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_rate}: The score factor $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{DR}}$ is set to zero when $M=\|\mathcal I\|=0$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:eta_ipw}: The score factor $\hat\eta_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IPW}}$ is set to zero when $M=\|\mathcal I\|=0$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}: The radial estimate uses $\hat\gamma_n=\widetilde\gamma_n$ computed from all input radii by the adaptive Hill selection at tolerance $\delta/6$; each score factor is set to zero when $M=\|\mathcal I\|=0$, and $\hat\mu_n=0$ when $\|1-\hat\alpha_n\hat\gamma_n\|=0$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rate_ipw}: The estimator is evaluated at the selected threshold $t_n$, using the same $\mathcal T$-measurable nuisance functions $\hat p$, $\hat g$ and exponent $\hat\alpha_n$ for every input threshold; its radial estimate uses the full-input Hill selection at tolerance $\delta/6$, and its first-factor estimate is zero when there are no evaluation exceedances. — The current construction of Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} in discovery/core.json specifies all-input Hill estimation and explicitly sets the radial factor to zero when its denominator is zero.

- note on `ass:consistency`: pinned reading: $Y(D)=Y$ almost surely throughout the paper. (The Causal Inference paragraph states consistency for random variables in the potential-outcome framework; equality of random variables in this framework is understood almost surely.)
- note on `ass:f-homo-asymptotic`: pinned reading: The homogeneity inequality holds for every $d\in\{0,1\}$ and every $t>0$, uniformly over $x\in B_R=\{x:\\|x\\|_1\leqslant R\}$ and $u\in S^{d-1}=\{u\in\mathbb R_+^d:\\|u\\|_1=1\}$, with $g$ being $L$-Lipschitz on this domain under the $\ell_1$ norm convention. (Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} defines the approximation using the conditional mean on the nonnegative noise domain. The Notations paragraph fixes the norm; Proposition~\ref{prop:id} uses both treatment values, and its added homogeneity condition explicitly specifies every positive radius and the positive unit sphere.)
- note on `ass:f-homo-bounded-x`: pinned reading: $\\|X\\|_1\leqslant R$ almost surely. (Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} bounds the random covariate, and the Notations paragraph explicitly identifies both norm symbols with the $\ell_1$-norm.)
- note on `ass:iid-independence`: pinned reading: $U\perp(X,D)$. (The opening model description calls $U$ an independent extreme-noise vector, and Assumption~\ref{assump:iid} states that it is independent of $X,D$. Standard multivariate independence treats the listed variables jointly.)
- note on `ass:mvary-density`: pinned reading: For each component law in $\tilde M_k$, there exists one constant $C_k>0$, independent of $z$, such that $h(z)=C_k\prod_{i=1}^{m}(1+z_i)^{-(\beta+1)}$ whenever $\\|z\\|_1>\zeta k^{(1-2s)/\beta}$. (Assumption~\ref{assump:mvary} uses $\varpropto$ on the stated tail region and separately bounds the relative error from the normalized nominal density. Proportionality fixes a constant multiplier, without requiring that multiplier to equal $\beta^m$.)
- note on `ass:rate-nuisance-errors`: pinned reading: For each fixed $t$ and tolerance $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, the three nuisance inequalities, with $\delta=\varepsilon$, hold on one $\mathcal T$-measurable event of probability at least $1-\varepsilon$, simultaneously almost surely over the covariate support, the conditional direction support and both treatments, where $\mathcal T$ consists of the training split and independent auxiliary data. (Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} fits nuisances on the training split before evaluation; Theorem~\ref{thm:rate}'s stated nuisance reading explicitly specifies the joint training-measurable event and simultaneous support interpretation.)
- note on `def:conditional-mean`: pinned reading: $f$ is a fixed measurable version satisfying $f(X,D,U)=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X,D,U]$ almost surely, and this same version is used in Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo}. (Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo} first defines $f$ through conditional expectation and then immediately imposes a pointwise homogeneity property on that function. The standard meaning of conditional expectation fixes the equality almost surely.)
- note on `def:nete-algorithm`: pinned reading: The training indices are chosen uniformly from the subsets of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ of size $n/2$, independently of the sampled observations; the remaining indices form $\mathcal D_2$. (Algorithm~\ref{alg:nete} specifies a random equal split, and the following estimation paragraph uses the first half for fitting and the second half for evaluation, invoking the standard independent sample-splitting convention.)
- note on `def:regular-variation`: pinned reading: For each norm, there exist $S$, $b_n\rightarrow\infty$, and $c>0$ such that, for every $a>0$, the polar measures $n\tmop{P}((\\|U\\|/b_n,U/\\|U\\|)\in\cdot)$ restricted to $[a,\infty]\times\mathbb{S}^+$ converge weakly as finite measures to the corresponding restriction of $c\nu_\beta\times S$. (Definition~\ref{def:mvarying} specifies $\nu_\beta([r,\infty))=r^{-\beta}$, which is finite away from zero and infinite over the entire radial space. The immediately following discussion identifies the intended Pareto tail limit, fixing the standard tail-measure interpretation.)
- note on `lem:eta-ipw`: the corrected statement stands as one statement; the referee would state it as a general statement with corollaries: Keep the truncated-score IPW bound as the main statement, and separate its angular-bound specialization and finite-variance sampling alternative into blocks under their respective additional conditions.
- note on `lem:eta-rate`: the corrected statement stands as one statement; the referee would state it as a general statement with corollaries: Keep the general truncated-score DR bound as the main statement; separate the angular-rate specialization under the additional Wasserstein hypothesis and the sampling specialization under finite conditional score variance into blocks.
- note on `prop:id`: [statement-wrong] 'g being L-Lipschitz on this domain' admits two formal readings. (A) Per treatment: for each d, \|g(x,d,u) − g(x',d,u')\| ≤ L(‖x−x'‖_1 + ‖u−u'‖_1) on B_R × S^{d−1}. (B) Joint in (x,d,u): \|g(x,d,u) − g(x',d',u')\| ≤ L(‖x−x'‖_1 + \|d−d'\| + ‖u−u'‖_1), which additionally forces \|g(x,1,u) − g(x,0,u)\| ≤ L. LipschitzG realizes (B). NL edit to pin it: say either 'for each d, g(·,d,·) is L-Lipschitz on B_R × S^{d−1}' or 'g is L-Lipschitz jointly in (x,d,u), with d in {0,1} as a real coordinate'. (rewind rejected by the orchestrator on prop:id: The author settled the reading (session, 2026-10-09): in Assumption~\ref{assump:f_homo}, ``$g$ is $L$-Lipschitz'' is per treatment. For each $d\in\{0,1\}$, $g(\cdot,d,\cdot)$ is $L$-Lipschitz on $B_R\times S^{d-1}$ under the $\ell_1$ norm: $\|g(x,d,u)-g(x^\prime,d,u^\prime)\|\le L(\\|x-x^\prime\\|_1+\\|u-u^\prime\\|_1)$. Nothing is assumed across the two treatments; in particular $\|g(x,1,u)-g(x,0,u)\|\le L$ is not assumed. The Lean statement is being changed to this reading.)
